Re-running the same grep WITHOUT `head` returned **eight** production write sites: slot replace, the officer-re-request supersede, stage→judge→promote, `_collapse_duplicate_docs`, `dedupe_income_proof`. `_is_single_instance()` already returned True for every document type; the behaviour was fully built, and the three duplicate cases in production all predated a fix that landed weeks earlier. The sprint was cancelled. Two habits, both cheap: (1) `head`/`tail`/`| head -N` are for READING, never for CONCLUDING — an absence claim needs the unfiltered output, or a `wc -l` to prove the list was short; (2) before proposing to build a mechanism, grep the codebase for the mechanism's own nouns (`supersede`, `dedupe`, `collapse`, `promote`) rather than only the field it would write — the existing implementation rarely uses the name you have in your head. The residual real gap turned out to be one line inside the machinery that already existed, found only by reading it. (Document dedup, 2026-07-23)

- **A fact that several rules depend on needs ONE owner and ONE derivation — and when a rule reads a single field, ask whether that field is the actual QUESTION.** `ScholarshipApplication.reporting_date` sized the bursary, gated payment eligibility and triggered the semester-result request. It was NULL for 45% of applications whose pathway needed confirming (against 3.8% of the rest), because the copy from the offer letter sat at the BOTTOM of `autofill_pathway_from_offer`, below four `return False` guards about the PATHWAY: a letter whose programme disagreed with the declaration — precisely the case that raises the confirm query — abandoned the function and took the date with it. Three compounding shapes, each worth recognising on its own: (1) **a fact riding as a passenger in a function whose real job is something else** inherits every one of that function's exits — the fix is to give it its own function called from the same triggers, above the guards; (2) **the same rule implemented twice** (`award._stpm_continuing` and `income_engine.semester_result_gap` each hand-rolled "started before the cohort year" against the same field) is wrong in both places simultaneously and gets fixed in one — extract the predicate; (3) **the field was not the question**: what sizes the bursary is the course-START YEAR, and a Form 6 letter routinely carries an intake RANGE ("6 / 2025 – 12 / 2026") and no reporting date at all, so reading one field could never have worked for that document family. Corroborating tell that this was systemic rather than a one-off: a test named `test_autofill_persists_date_even_when_pathway_locked` already existed — the identical bug had been found, fixed and pinned for the LOCK guard, and nobody checked the four guards above it. (Reporting date, 2026-07-23)

- **A student's free-text answer must never populate a field that moves money — they can misread the question, and a parsed date looks identical to a verified one.** The system detects a missing reporting date and asks the student for it; the obvious "fix" was to parse that answer into the field, reusing the pattern that already turns a typed answer into structured state for Vircle IDs and pension claims. On live data that rule would have been wrong for **half** the parseable answers: #123 replied "16 july 2026", meaning the day he collected a confirmation letter, while his offer showed he had started in June 2025 — the parse would have made him a fresh entrant and committed RM3,000 instead of RM1,000, from an authoritative-looking column, with no flag. The distinction that matters is not "structured vs free text" but **what the human was asked to do**: a Vircle ID is copied off their own screen; a reporting date is a question about a process they may not understand. Official documents are the authority for money facts; a student's words are a prompt for a human to look. (Reporting date, 2026-07-23)

- **A key ASSEMBLED at runtime is invisible to a static scanner — put the check where the two halves can actually be compared, which is usually the other side of the stack.** The cockpit rendered a literal `admin.scholarship.verdict.item.pathway_type_switch` on a live case. Three guards existed and none could fire: i18n PARITY only proves en == ms == ta (all three were equally missing it); the web `admin-scholarship-i18n` scanner matches STATIC `t('a.b.c')` literals, and this key is built as `` `…item.${item.code}` `` so there is nothing to match; and `next build`/jest never resolve keys at all. The 2026 R7 lesson ("parity does not prove a key EXISTS") was right but its remedy — a source scanner — only covers the static half. The fix that generalises: **when one side of a contract generates the identifier, write the test on THAT side** — here a pytest that AST-scans every module emitting a verdict item and asserts each code resolves in all three locale files. Three properties make such a cross-tree test worth having rather than decorative: it must **fail loudly, never skip**, if the other tree can't be found; it must **sanity-check its own scan** (assert a minimum code count, or renaming the helper silently turns it into a no-op that passes forever — the same trap as the `_SUBJECT_BM` drift test); and it must **fail on a new UNVERIFIABLE case** (a `_item(code, …)` whose code is a variable), so the gap becomes a decision instead of an escape hatch. General form: for any `prefix + <runtime value>` lookup — i18n keys, feature-flag names, metric names, S3 paths, template names — the enumerable side is the code that produces the value, not the code that consumes it. (Verdict-item i18n, 2026-07-23)

- **The best fix for a keep-in-sync pair is to DELETE one side — have the server send the computed value, not the rule.** This file already records ~5 instances of one rule living in two places and drifting (verdict tally↔chip paint ×3, `academic_engine._SUBJECT_BM`↔`subjects.ts`, assignment offer-set↔accept-set), and each was fixed by *syncing harder*: cross-referencing comments, a drift test, "treat the pair as one unit of change". The payments advance-window rule ("a run may be paid no earlier than the 25th of the preceding month") needed the earliest valid date in a UI message — the reflex was a `paymentWindow.ts` mirroring `payments.earliest_payment_date` plus a paired test, i.e. a sixth pair. Instead the API returns the computed date with the error (`{code: 'too_early', earliest: '2026-07-25'}`) and the frontend renders it verbatim; `adminMutate` was widened to preserve the whole error body so any code can carry a detail. Nothing to keep in sync, no drift test, no second implementation. **Before mirroring a rule across the language boundary, ask what the other side actually needs — usually a VALUE, not the rule that produced it.** Mirroring is right only when the client must decide *without* a round trip (e.g. an offer-set filter rendered before any request). (Payments window rules, 2026-07-22)

- **A settings DEFAULT is not a production VALUE — and the tell is not "am I unsure?", it is "does this sentence name an env var and a value?".** THIRD occurrence, and the second in two days: I wrote that `DECLINE_COOLOFF_DAYS=0` in production (so an embargo bug was "inert, safe to defer") having read `os.environ.get('DECLINE_COOLOFF_DAYS', '0')` in `settings/base.py`. Live value: **7**, with two applications mid-embargo. The false claim propagated into a technical-debt entry, CLAUDE.md, decisions.md, a retrospective and project memory — every one of them concluding "safe to defer" *because* of the wrong number, i.e. the error changed a decision, not just a sentence. The two prior lessons on this (2026-06-15 "verify flag state against the running system"; 2026-07-21 "asserted a feature-flag state from stale project memory") did NOT prevent it, because they are phrased as advice for when you already suspect you are guessing — and a code default does not *feel* like a guess, it feels like reading the source. **Worst detail: the correct value was ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN** — `halatuju_api/CLAUDE.md` has stated "`DECLINE_COOLOFF_DAYS=7`" since 2026-06-27, in a file loaded into context every session. This was not missing information; it was a code default silently outranking an explicit doc statement. Cheapest possible guard before any live-value claim: `grep -rn '<VAR_NAME>' CLAUDE.md docs/` — the answer is often already recorded, and a contradiction between that and your assumption is itself the signal to go check the running service. **The operational trigger, which needs no judgement: any sentence containing an env-var name AND a value/state is a claim about a running system.** Such a sentence may only be written from pasted `gcloud run services describe` / `information_schema` / live-query output. A settings file may be cited for what a knob MEANS, never for what it IS. If you cannot verify in the moment, write "unverified" — a missing number costs a follow-up question; a wrong number silently justifies the wrong decision. Corollary: when you discover such an error, grep every artifact for the wrong value — it will have been copied. (Org-admin reject / DECLINE_COOLOFF_DAYS, 2026-07-22)

- **When a request bundles a MECHANISM with a GOAL, verify the goal isn't already met before building the mechanism — and report the finding, because it often changes the design.** The owner asked for an org-admin reject whose email is sent "48 hours later", then justified it as stopping the student submitting a document or changing status. The 48h embargo machinery already existed (`decline_due_at` + `release_pending_declines`), so the obvious move was to wire it up. But tracing the four student write paths (`ApplicationDetailView.patch`, `IncomeRouteSwitchView`, `_current_application` for uploads, `confirm_profile`) plus `send_application_reminders` showed all of them gate on `POST_SHORTLIST_EDITABLE` — so `_record_reject` ALONE freezes the case the instant it runs, with or without the delay. Told that, the owner dropped the delay entirely, which deleted a whole mechanism from the sprint (and with it the embargo's status-MASKING, which would have shown a shortlisted student the stage "Interviewed" for two days while their uploads silently 403'd — a bug the feature would have shipped). The mechanism a request names is a HYPOTHESIS about how to reach the goal; check what the code already guarantees, state it plainly, and let the owner re-decide. (Org-admin reject, 2026-07-21)

- **A "we decided not to build X" entry with a `Revisit if:` clause is a TRIGGER, not a closed door — check whether the trigger has fired before either re-litigating or blindly obeying it.** decisions.md 2026-07-19 rejected a `rejection_note` field ("the QC reject hangs its reason on the DecisionReopen trail"), closing with *"Revisit if: the owner wants a rejection reason surfaced independently of the reopen trail."* The org-admin reject needed exactly that, and not as a preference: at `shortlisted` no verdict was ever recorded, so there is no `DecisionReopen` row to attach a reason to — the old approach is impossible, not merely worse. Reading only the Decision line would have wrongly blocked the field; ignoring the entry would have lost the reasoning that keeps the QC path unchanged. Grep decisions.md for the area BEFORE designing, and read each hit's `Revisit if:` as the acceptance test for reopening it. (Org-admin reject, 2026-07-21)

- **Imported/external text will overflow any `CharField(max_length=N)` — classify long content into the unlimited field AND add a hard save-guard; never trust the source to fit.** The contract `.docx` import 500'd (`DataError: value too long for type character varying(255)`) because `_docx_structure` routed every Word `Heading`-styled paragraph into `heading_*` (varchar 255), but real authors style full sub-clauses as a Heading — the donor agreement had 9 "headings" of 256–539 chars. Worse, the create-form upload path swallowed the 500 as a soft-fail and produced a SILENTLY EMPTY draft (a swallowed write error that looks like "nothing imported"). Two-layer fix, both needed: (1) classify at parse time — a Heading longer than a title threshold is really body; (2) a guard in the save (`replace_clauses`) that folds any still-oversized heading into the body, covering EVERY write path (hand-typed, Gemini, copy-from), not just this importer. General rule: a model field with a length cap that receives user/imported text needs a guard at the choke-point write, and a soft-failing populate path must SURFACE the error, not leave an empty artefact. (Contract import 500 / empty upload, 2026-07-21)

- **`?format=<x>` is RESERVED by Django REST Framework — never use it as your own query param.** The contract Preview's "Open PDF" used `?format=pdf` to switch the response to a PDF; it 404'd for MONTHS and looked like a render failure. `format` is DRF's content-negotiation URL override (`URL_FORMAT_OVERRIDE`): DRF resolves it to a RENDERER during `perform_content_negotiation()`, which runs in `APIView.initial()` **before authentication and before your `get()`**. With no renderer named `pdf`, DRF raises `Http404` there — so the view never runs and the custom `if format=='pdf'` branch is dead code. The tell in logs: the same endpoint returns **401 without the param but 404 with `?format=<unregistered>`**, in a few ms (no rendering). Diagnosis shortcut: `curl` the live URL unauthenticated with `?format=json` (→401, a real renderer) vs `?format=pdf` (→404) — if they differ, it's the param collision, not auth and not your code. Use any other name (`?output=pdf`, `?as=pdf`) or a dedicated path. Same trap for `?ordering=`/`?search=`/`?page=` if the matching DRF filter/pagination backend is enabled. Add a test that asserts your endpoint returns the file for the real param AND that `?format=<yourvalue>` 404s, so nobody reintroduces it. (Contract Preview PDF, TD-163, 2026-07-21)

- **Word's auto-numbering (`1.`, `1.1`, `i)`) is NOT in `paragraph.text` — it's generated from the paragraph's list definition (`numPr`), so read the structure directly instead of asking an AI to guess it back from stripped text.** The contract `.docx` importer flattened the doc to `p.text` and handed it to Gemini to re-segment; Gemini reasonably merged separate roman-numbered definition items into one clause body because the text it received carried no numbering cue — we had thrown away the exact structure the doc already encoded. Fix: `contracts._docx_structure` reads `para._p.pPr.numPr` (`numId`/`ilvl`) + `para.style.name` and maps `Heading N`/`ilvl` → clause level, list items → the nested level, `Title` → title, pre-clause paragraphs → preamble — a deterministic parse, with Gemini kept only as the fallback for a doc with no styles. General rule: when a file format already carries structure (Word numbering, PDF tags, spreadsheet cells), parse THAT, don't round-trip it through flattened text + an LLM. To build a `.docx` fixture with numbering in a test, set `numPr` via oxml (`w:numPr` > `w:ilvl`/`w:numId`) — `add_paragraph(style='Heading 1')` alone does not carry an `ilvl`. (Contract import fidelity, 2026-07-21)

- **xhtml2pdf renders mixed / nested list numbering poorly — compute the numbers in Python and emit explicit prefixes, don't lean on `<ol>`.** The contract clause hierarchy needed `1.` / `1.1` / `i)` (decimal then roman); an `<ol>` can't express mixed decimal+roman and xhtml2pdf's list-counter support is weak, so the PDF numbered wrong. Fix: a pure `clause_numbers(levels)` computes the label per row from the level run, rendered as a plain `<div>` with a `<b>{number}</b>` prefix + per-level indent — full control, identical in the browser preview and the PDF. General rule for xhtml2pdf: if the visual depends on CSS the engine barely supports (list-style, flex, mixed counters, custom fonts), compute/inline it yourself rather than trusting the CSS. (Clause hierarchy, 2026-07-19)

- **When Stitch stalls (unreliable — needs retries most attempts), fall back to a hand-built HTML Artifact mockup for owner approval — for precise/dense UI it's the BETTER vehicle anyway.** The Stitch-first rule exists to get *visual approval before coding UI*; a self-contained HTML page in the real design system achieves that AND renders exact numbering/tables/controls that Stitch's AI interpretation only approximates. Two generate attempts failed (~9 min) before the clause-editor mockup went up as an Artifact in ~one pass. Keep Stitch for exploratory/greenfield screens; reach straight for an HTML Artifact when the UI is a precise variation on an existing surface. (Clause hierarchy, 2026-07-19)

- **To embed a custom TTF in an xhtml2pdf PDF, register it with reportlab AND seed xhtml2pdf's `DEFAULT_FONT` map — the `@font-face` route silently mis-behaves (and is outright broken on Windows local dev).** For the contract in IBM Plex Serif, `@font-face { src: url(...) }` + a link_callback raised `Can't open file …tmp….ttf` on Windows (xhtml2pdf copies the font to a NamedTemporaryFile that stays locked); and `pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont(...))` ALONE left the PDF on Times, because xhtml2pdf resolves a CSS `font-family` through its OWN `context.fontList` (seeded from `xhtml2pdf.default.DEFAULT_FONT`), not reportlab's registry. The working, cross-platform recipe: (1) `pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('IBM Plex Serif', regular))` + a separate `…Bold` face, (2) `addMapping('IBM Plex Serif', 0/1, 0, name)` for bold, (3) `DEFAULT_FONT['ibm plex serif'] = 'IBM Plex Serif'` (lowercased key → reportlab name). VERIFY by scanning the PDF bytes for `FontFile2` + the subset tag (`AAAAAA+IBMPlexSerif-…`) and asserting the fallback (`Times-`) is GONE — a silent Helvetica/Times fallback looks fine until someone opens the PDF. Do the registration once (module guard) at generate time, best-effort (missing files → the HTML serif fallback still renders). (Contract font, 2026-07-19)

- **A plan's "Verified facts" / "locked" section is authoritative for INTENT, not for field-level ground truth — re-verify each load-bearing fact against the running code before building on it.** The go-live transition plan stated PartnerOrganisation had "no phone field" and specified a new `contact_phone`; the model already had a `phone` field that `courses.AdminProfileView` actively edits. Following the plan verbatim would have created two phone columns for one datum — the exact "two fields/surfaces → silent drift" failure this file documents ~5 times. The owner's LOCKED decisions were behavioural (the 4 numbered ones), and field naming wasn't among them, so reusing `phone` honoured the intent without the redundancy. Before adding a column/endpoint a plan names, grep the model + its existing editors; when a plan-fact contradicts the code, the code wins and the deviation gets documented (CHANGELOG + decisions), not silently followed or silently ignored. (Contract go-live T1, 2026-07-19)

- **An action dropdown must OFFER exactly what the server will ACCEPT, and must always RENDER the current value — a controlled `<select>` whose value matches no option silently displays the default option.** Three assignment surfaces drifted three ways in one arc: the cockpit offered reviewer/super only, so #66's stored org_admin assignee READ "Unassigned" (the data was intact — a role change made it invisible); the list offered every staff role, inviting picks the server refuses (`bad_assignee`); and the list never applied the first-assign readiness gate the server enforces (`not_ready`). The offer-set and the accept-set are a KEEP-IN-SYNC PAIR — the action-affordance twin of the verdict tally↔paint disease: derive the UI option filter from the server's rule (cross-referencing comments both ways), and always union-in the currently-stored value. Corollary for capability questions: "can X do Y" = the INTERSECTION of the endpoint gate and the UI affordance — answering from the API alone gave the owner a wrong "yes". (Assignment surfaces, 2026-07-16)

- **A deterministic field parser must ANCHOR each value to its label — never grab the first matching token on the page — because a downloaded document carries a print timestamp at the top that pollutes any date/year grab.** `_slip_exam` read the SPM exam year with `re.search(r'\b(20\d{2})\b', text)` (first year anywhere) and stored 2026 off a 2025 slip — the "12/04/2026, 07:25" download stamp printed at the top of the page wins over "SIJIL PELAJARAN MALAYSIA TAHUN 2025" lower down. Any `first 20xx / first N-digit number` regex over a flattened OCR page has this bug latent. Anchor to the field's own label (`PELAJARAN MALAYSIA TAHUN …`) and return blank rather than a wrong value. The raw OCR (`_debug_rows`) proved the read was correct and the parser was at fault — check that before blaming OCR. (Private-arm/switch/exam-year, 2026-07-10)

- **When the owner says "we already have a detector — extend it", route the new signal through the EXISTING mechanism even if a parallel one is cheaper.** For disqualifying UTM SPACE I first built a deterministic `offer_private_arm` override (self-correcting on deploy, no re-run) — but the owner's model is the genuineness ladder (private → fake −2), and a parallel override splits the "what counts as private" logic across two places. Extending the scorer (one mechanism for standalone + wing private) was the right call, accepting the re-run as the activation cost. Confirm which mechanism the owner is picturing before optimising for a property (no-re-run) they didn't ask for. (Private-arm/switch/exam-year, 2026-07-10)

- **When a document "read wrong / read by AI", read the doc's OWN recorded reason BEFORE theorising about the layout.** Chasing a missing exam-year chip, I gave the owner two wrong root causes ("old layout", then "the parser under-read the cert table") — both from eyeballing the OCR — when the doc's stored `vision_fields.fields._slip_ocr_diag` said `not_spm_exam`: the deterministic parser had been SKIPPED entirely by the profile `exam_type` gate. The extraction records why it fell back / how it captured (`_slip_ocr_diag`, `capture`, `authenticity`); query that first, then hypothesise. (Deterministic doc reading, 2026-07-10)

- **Before a change that OVERWRITES an extracted field set, query the current field COVERAGE and merge-preserve — a "better read" can silently drop a field the old read had.** Switching govt offers from Gemini to the deterministic parser replaced `vision_fields.fields` wholesale; but all 64 live offers carry `reporting_date` (feeds the genuineness bonus) and the parser misses it on ~2 layouts — so the Exact read would have dropped the bonus a band. Caught by counting `reporting_date` coverage in prod before the backfill; fixed by merging deterministic OVER the prior fields (deterministic wins where present, prior kept otherwise). Any replace-the-fields switch needs this check. (Deterministic doc reading, 2026-07-10)

- **Wiring a new genuineness-scored doc type touches THREE layers, not one — the serializer allowlist is the layer that silently swallows it.** After building the salary scorer + extraction wiring and writing `not_salary` to a doc, the cockpit still showed it "Verified" (owner caught it in a screenshot). Root cause: `serializers.ApplicantDocumentSerializer.get_authenticity` has a hard doc-type allowlist that omitted `salary_slip`; I verified the FE render path (`officerCockpit.genuinenessFact` handles `not_`) but not the serializer that FEEDS it. A new genuineness doc type = (1) the scorer + extraction call, (2) the **serializer allowlist**, (3) the FE render — and there are SEVERAL doc-type allowlists (`get_authenticity`, `anomaly_engine._GENUINENESS_DOC_LABELS`, `verdict_engine._income_genuineness_docs`/`_LADDER_DOCS`). Grep every one as a checklist before claiming "no FE change needed". (Salary signature model, 2026-07-09)

- **A grouped hit-rate in a throwaway calibration script is NOT a per-token frequency — confirm the literal token with `grep -il` before building a signature on it.** The first salary analysis reported `GRED` as a 10/10 govt marker; the scorer built on it found ~1. The analysis had grouped `GRED` with `NO GAJI`/`KUMPULAN`, so "10/10" was the GROUP. `GRED` is in 1/99 files; the real govt marker is the `PENYATA GAJI` title (10/99). Before trusting any candidate signature, ground-truth it against per-token file counts. (Salary signature model, 2026-07-09)

- **Anchor build monitoring on BUILD IDs, never "the latest N builds" — a push→trigger race reports the PREVIOUS commit's success as yours.** A wait-loop polling `builds list --limit 2` for terminal states exited immediately after a push because the new triggers hadn't registered yet: the two "SUCCESS" builds it saw belonged to the prior commits. Consequence chain: "feature is live" reported when it wasn't → the follow-up backfill job no-opped against the old revision ("nothing left" under the old pass marker) and the false all-clear compounded. Discipline: after `git push`, list builds, match `SHORT_SHA` to YOUR commit, then wait on those exact build IDs. If the trigger hasn't registered yet, wait for it to appear — never accept a terminal status for a SHA you didn't push. (Reporting-date bonus, 2026-07-08)

- **When a band/verdict tally changes its predicate, grep the FE for the OLD predicate in the same commit — the chip painter is a second copy of the rule and it WILL lag.** Third instance of the same disease in two days on the pathway card alone: (1) the band counted `mismatch` but the chip painted `unreadable` red (#64); (2) the chip painted not-official red but the band didn't count it (#131/#84); (3) the reporting-date bonus lifted the band's effective officialness but the chip kept the raw `auth !== 'genuine'` (#56/#75 — red Pathway chip under a Certain tile). The tally (`verdict_engine._pathway_red_chips`) and the paint (`officerCockpit.documentFacts` offer branch) are a KEEP-IN-SYNC PAIR — both now carry cross-referencing comments; any change to one is not done until the other is re-derived. The general form: a verdict rule that reviewers SEE as chips exists twice by construction; treat the pair as one unit of change. (Reporting-date bonus chip fix, 2026-07-08)

- **A spec's worked examples must become executable tests in the SAME change that implements the spec.** The red-chip ladder shipped with a counter that contradicted the owner's own locked #31 example (spec said Unsure, code computed Probable) — undetected because the example lived in a memory note, not a test; a live case (#131) surfaced it a day later. When an owner locks arithmetic ("#12 = −5 → Fail"), transcribe every example as a named regression test before writing the implementation — the examples ARE the acceptance criteria. (Pathway-chip variable correction, 2026-07-08)

- **When a wrong PROXY causes a bug, grep every surface that reads that proxy — the same flaw usually hides in the verdict, the gate, AND the display.** The STR-recipient-vs-`income_earner` mismatch produced THREE bugs from one root cause: the income verdict ignored the STR (P3), the submission gate (`income_doc_blockers`) still demanded a salary slip (blocking consent → trapping the student at submission), and the docs box filed the wrong parent's IC under STR ROUTE (`incomeSubSections` keyed off `income_earner`). Fixing only the loudest surface (the verdict) left the other two contradicting it — the verdict said "Certain B40" while the gate said "still owes a payslip." When a fix turns on "X should drive this, not the proxy Y," `grep` for every read of Y (verdict engine, the consent/checklist gate, the FE layout) and reconcile them in the same change, or they drift into visible contradictions. (P3 completion — gate + IC filing, 2026-07-06)

- **A "deferred — no live case" verdict rots; re-run the cheap detecting query at the next relevant review, don't treat the old count as durable.** P3 was parked 2026-07-05 as "no live case where it changes an outcome," but that audit predated the very documents that created the live cases (#45's STR uploaded the next day; #63 read differently). A point-in-time re-banding/impact count is only true at that instant. When deferring a correct-but-gated change, record the ONE-LINE detecting query (here: "salary-route apps with a live valid STR") as a recheck, not a closed verdict — and re-run it when a related case surfaces. (P3 STR salary-seam, 2026-07-06)

- **A dedup / uniqueness key must match the thing's real cardinality — a HOUSEHOLD-level proof keyed PER MEMBER leaks duplicates when the member tag is inconsistent.** STR (one recipient per household) was lumped into the per-member income-proof dedup, so the same screenshot re-uploaded under a different member tag ('mother' vs blank #125; 'mother' vs 'father' #45 — both literally the same file) sat in different buckets and both stayed live. The same-day cohort backfill missed them because the dupes were CROSS-member. Fix: make the dedup scope type-specific (household-wide for STR, per-member for salary/EPF) and guard-test it with MISMATCHED member tags — a same-member test can't catch a cross-member leak. Before keying dedup/uniqueness on (type, member), ask "is this doc one-per-member or one-per-household?" (Live-review #125, 2026-07-06)

- **The web type-check must mirror `next build`: Next's injected target + scoped to source, not the raw project config over the whole tree.** `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` reported ~15 errors — ALL in pre-existing test files (`scholarship.test.ts`, `soft-evidence-drift.test.ts`, older `officerCockpit.test.ts` cases) — that `next build` has tolerated for sprints. Root cause: the project tsconfig sets no `target`, so bare `tsc` defaults low and flags `Set` iteration (TS2802), while Next *injects* a higher target at build time; and the build effectively doesn't gate on those test files. Faithful local check: `npx tsc --noEmit --target es2018 --downlevelIteration --skipLibCheck` scoped to `src/**` **excluding `__tests__`** (a throwaway `tsconfig` that `extends` the base with a tests-excluding `exclude`). Separately eyeball NEW code for the one thing the target override masks — a `[...set]` **spread** (not `Set` iteration in a `for-of`, which the injected target resolves). This refines the 2026-07-05 lesson, which said "use the project config" without the target-injection caveat. (Cockpit header dates/timeline, 2026-07-06)

- **To test a "set-if-null / does-not-overwrite on re-entry" invariant, drive the REAL entry function with the field pre-seeded — don't reconstruct a full reverse cycle whose preconditions you haven't verified.** A re-award test used `_revert_to_pool()` then `fund_student()` and hit `SponsorshipError('not_fundable')`: `_revert_to_pool` only flips status back to `recommended`, it does NOT cancel the holding sponsorship, so the app stays un-fundable. Pre-seeding `awarded_at` on a fresh `recommended` app and calling `fund_student` once tests the actual call-site set-if-null cleanly. (Cockpit header dates/timeline, 2026-07-06)

- **Reusing a "once per X" component inside a per-item list needs dedup at the LIST level, not the item level.** V6 mounted the per-*earner* `IncomeClusterCoach` on every open income doc-task, so a member with two tasks (IC + slip) would render two identical coaches. The component is anchored once per cluster on its native surface (`clusterAnchorKey`); a per-item list has no such anchor unless you build one. Fix: compute a per-key anchor (first item of each key) in the list and pass a `show…` flag so only the anchor renders it. When you lift a cluster/summary component into a flat list, ask "how many times will this mount?" and dedup by its natural key. (Verification-model V6, 2026-07-04)

- **When you add a gate/precondition to a SHARED endpoint, grep the whole test tree for every caller — not just that endpoint's own test file.** The V5 QC gap floor (refuse QC-Accept while a verdict fact is red) broke `test_decision_reopen.py`, not `test_qc_gate.py`: the reopen suite drives a real QC-Accept on a document-less fixture whose live `build_verdict` is all-gaps, so the floor 400'd it. I re-pinned the QC-gate tests but never considered that other endpoints' tests exercise the same POST. Before shipping a new precondition, `grep -r '<endpoint-path>' tests/` and audit each caller — a fixture that was fine under the old contract can silently violate the new one. (Verification-model V5, 2026-07-04)

- **A status/state-machine side-effect must fire from the event that MEANS the new state — not from incidental activity that merely co-occurs with it.** `profile_complete → interviewing` was advanced on any interview *draft save*; when V3 folded the agenda into the draft, early triage (even an agenda-item delete) started saving drafts, and four live apps silently flipped into the interview funnel with no accountable owner. The trigger was wrong, not just under-guarded. When a UI change makes an existing event fire more often (or in new contexts), audit every state-advancing side-effect keyed to that event and re-anchor it to the action that actually denotes the transition (here: proposing times / submitting — plus an assigned owner). Invariant-test the target state (`interviewing ⇒ assigned AND (slots OR submitted session)`) so an incidental writer can't satisfy it. (Interview-trigger hotfix, 2026-07-03)

- **Multi-line source edits through a bash-heredoc-wrapped python string mangle escapes (`
` became a real newline → a SyntaxError written INTO the file). Write the edit script to a scratch file and run it, or use the editor tool — and always run the affected tests immediately after a scripted edit** (the collection error caught it in seconds). (Code-health S5, 2026-07-03)

- **A known incident mode documented in a memory note is still an OPEN bug — encode the protection as a code invariant.** "Never re-extract locally (no Storage → wipes vision_fields)" lived as a memory rule for weeks while every writer stayed happy to overwrite good data with an empty error read; one absent-minded run away from data loss. The fix (clobber guard: a FAILED run never replaces a prior good read of the same immutable blob) took ~20 lines. When a post-incident rule says "never do X because Y destroys Z", ask what code change makes Y unable to destroy Z. Corollary for tests: when a command infers success from a side-effect (e.g. a timestamp advancing), its tests' mocks must produce that side-effect. (Code-health S2, 2026-07-03)

- **A cross-repo "mirror of X — keep in sync" comment is not a mirror; pin it with a test that reads the other repo's file and fails loudly (never skips).** `academic_engine._SUBJECT_BM` drifted 64 keys behind `subjects.ts` in one month because the sync was enforced by a comment; both sides stayed self-consistent so nothing failed — only real students hit the "missing subject" loop. The drift test must also sanity-check its own parse (min key count), or a restructure of the source file silently turns it into a 0-missing no-op. (Code-health S1, 2026-07-03)
- **When two different outward events share one stamp field, the consumer that needs to distinguish them can't — give each its own stamp.** `decision_email_sent_at` meant both "shortlist PASS email went" and "decline email went"; `cancel_pending_decline` read it as the latter, so for every normally-processed student the cancel silently did nothing. A status-machine test must also ENTER states via the real transitions (the old test built the fixture directly in the target status, skipping the very step that stamps the field, and pinned the bug as green). (Code-health S1, 2026-07-03)

- **Before enabling a cron that auto-acts on a CLASS of existing records (emails, charges, status flips), backfill the existing rows as "already handled" in the SAME migrate-first step — or the first run blasts them all.** Switching the award email to a cool-off cron would have re-emailed every already-notified award (and the embargoed one) on its first tick; the fix was to backfill `offer_emailed_at=now()` on all live awards before the scheduler existed. When you add a "do this to everything matching X" job, the existing matches are the danger, not the new ones. (Award email cool-off, 2026-06-29)

- **The sponsor pool DETAIL serves `SponsorProfile.anon_markdown`, not `final_markdown` — they are two fields that only the approve-path keeps in sync.** Publishing a profile (flipping `anon_published`) does NOT refresh `anon_markdown`, so a student published outside the normal approve flow (e.g. a manual/SQL publish, or one whose final was regenerated after the last publish) shows sponsors a STALE profile. When publishing/regenerating, mirror the current `final_markdown` → `anon_markdown` (+ `anon_model_used`/`anon_generated_at`) and regenerate the card `anon_blurb`, exactly as `AdminRecordVerdictView`'s approve+finalise branch does. (Sponsor self-funding, 2026-06-29)

- **`manage.py sqlmigrate` renders for the LOCAL backend (SQLite) — never paste it into a Postgres migrate-first.** Local dev is SQLite, prod is Postgres; `sqlmigrate courses 0059` printed a full SQLite table-rebuild (`CREATE new__…; INSERT…SELECT; DROP; RENAME`) for what is a one-line `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` on Postgres. For migrate-first via the Supabase MCP, **hand-write the Postgres DDL** and verify it against the live schema (`information_schema.columns`, an existing sibling table's id/timestamp/RLS conventions) before applying. (WhatsApp go-live, 2026-06-21)
- **Parallel feature branches each grab the next migration number blindly — check `max(migration)` on `main` before merging any of them.** doc-slots and whatsapp-comms both created `scholarship/0067` (neither could see the other off the same base). The later merge must renumber (→`0068`) and re-point its `dependencies`. Make "diff the migration leaves vs main" a pre-merge step whenever >1 branch is in flight. (WhatsApp go-live, 2026-06-21)
- **Split go-live config into agent-settable (non-secret) and owner-settable (secret) so secrets never transit chat.** WhatsApp go-live: the agent set `TWILIO_WHATSAPP_FROM` + the template SID via `--update-env-vars`; the owner pasted `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID`/`TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` into the Cloud Run console. Ship dark, set non-secrets, owner adds secrets, then flip one `*_ENABLED` flag after a smoke test (check blast radius first). (WhatsApp go-live, 2026-06-21)
- **A "fire when within X" reminder/window with no lower bound fires instantly on a late trigger — gate on the notice the trigger actually gave.** The interview reminder ("within 24h", "within 1h") had no floor, so a same-day booking got an instant "24h reminder." Fix: gate on `event_time − created/booked_at` (the notice), keeping firing late-tolerant so cron jitter never skips a legit send. Applies to any scheduled-nudge feature. (Interview reminder-notice, 2026-06-21)

- **A server-side validator must accept EVERY state the UI can legitimately produce — or a save silently 400s and the user's input is LOST.** `_validate_findings` rejected `verdict=''`, but the cockpit's normal action (typing a finding without clicking a verdict button) sends exactly that; the whole Save-draft was rejected and the reviewer's notes vanished on reload. When you add/tighten a validation rule, enumerate the field's possible UI states — **especially the empty/default state** — and accept the legitimate ones. Bonus guard: a failed save should not look like a success; surface the error AND don't let a rejected write masquerade as saved. (Cockpit data-loss fix, 2026-06-20)
- **Any AI prompt that reasons about TIME must be given today's date — the model doesn't know it.** Gemini flagged a past event ("accident on April 3rd, 2026") as "in the future" because the gap-spotter prompt never stated the current date. Inject today's date (in the user's timezone — MYT here) into any prompt that may compare dates, with an explicit past-vs-future instruction. This is systematic: audit other prompts (`profile_engine`, `help_engine`, `vision`) before they reason about dates too. (Cockpit AI-date fix, 2026-06-20)

- **i18n parity only proves en==ms==ta — it does NOT prove a `t()` key EXISTS. A page can reference keys that were never added and ship silently, rendering the raw key path.** The sponsor redesign (R1–R4) referenced ~47 `sponsorPortal.{impact,journey,activity,…}.*` keys that were never added to any locale; the My Giving/Students/Account pages rendered literal `sponsorPortal.impact.totalGiven` etc. It went undetected for 4 sprints because parity passed (all three locales equally missing), `next build`/jest don't validate `t()` keys, and the surface was dark (no real user saw it). **Guard:** a test that scans source for static `t('ns.key')` calls and asserts each resolves in en.json (dynamic-aware: skip `${...}`/concat keys by matching only keys ending at a closing quote). See `halatuju-web/src/messages/__tests__/sponsor-i18n.test.ts` (R7) + the older `admin-scholarship-i18n.test.ts` (TD-120). A "dark/dormant" surface especially needs this — there's no live user to notice raw keys. (Sponsor Redesign R7)

- **Bind an irreversible/outward side-effect to the state transition it belongs to — not to a nearby earlier action that happens to be convenient.** Sponsor "publish" was stapled to the reviewer's Accept verdict, but that fires one step BEFORE the QC gate — so a student went in front of sponsors (visible AND fundable) while still awaiting QC. The fix wasn't a read-layer filter; it was moving the publish to the transition that actually means "cleared for sponsors" (QC Accept → `recommended`). When a side-effect leaks across a gate, ask "which button/transition is the true trigger?" and move it there, so the data model matches the state machine instead of being patched at read time. (Keep a belt-and-suspenders read gate too — here `status == 'recommended'` in both `is_pool_eligible` and `is_fundable`.) The owner had to reject two read-layer patches first ("not a solution — we need a clear transition") before this framing landed. (Publish-at-QC Sprint, 2026-07-02)
- **When you introduce a slot/uniqueness key, enumerate EVERY writer of that slot — not just the primary path.** The document slot key `(doc_type, household_member)` (TD-115) was correct for the apply form, where each `(type, member)` is unique per application. But the Action Centre lets a reviewer request several docs of the *same* type (multiple "Other", or the same income doc for a different person) — those writers were never considered, so each upload silently overwrote the last and live applicants lost documents (Theepicaa: 5 requested, 1 stored). Fix was a third key dimension (`request_code`). Before shipping any "this overwrites the existing one in the same slot" rule, list all code paths that create that row (apply form AND reviewer/officer requests AND system-gap auto-resolves) and confirm the key distinguishes the ones that must coexist. (Request-owned doc slots, 2026-06-21)
- **A hand-written `CreateModel` migration must use `BigAutoField` for `id` — the project's `DEFAULT_AUTO_FIELD` is `BigAutoField`.** Writing a migration by hand (e.g. to control a seed `RunPython`) and defaulting the PK to `models.AutoField` from memory leaves model-state drift: `makemigrations --check` then demands a follow-up `…_alter_<model>_id`. Copy the `('id', models.BigAutoField(auto_created=True, primary_key=True, serialize=False, verbose_name='ID'))` line from a recent `CreateModel` migration, and always run `python manage.py makemigrations <app> --check --dry-run` before committing a hand-authored migration. (Sponsor Redesign R5)

- **A button that "reverses" or "edits" a finalised thing must touch the same state the original action published — check what the existing control actually does before extending it.** The cockpit "Edit" on a recorded decision was frontend-only (`setEditDec`); it unlocked the form but left the student's profile LIVE in the sponsor pool. Renaming it to "Reopen" without wiring it to `SponsorProfile.anon_published` would have let a reviewer correct a decision while the old profile stayed visible to sponsors. Before building a reverse/edit flow, trace where the original action's effect LIVES (here: the pool-publish gate) and undo/redo that, not just the editor lock. (Decision reopen, 2026-06-18)
- **When a feature writes a per-PERSON quality metric, confirm the increment trigger with the owner before coding — the trigger point assigns blame.** "Reopen counts as a reviewer error" is ambiguous: count every reopen (A) penalises an exploratory reopen the super then cancels; count only saved-change reopens (B) doesn't. Surfaced the A/B fork with a recommendation rather than guessing; the owner chose B. Also: derive such a count from an audit LOG (`COUNT(resulted_in_change=True)`), never a bare counter — it stays reconstructable and carries the reason/who/when. (Decision reopen, 2026-06-18)
- **Never pass a secret as a `gcloud` flag value that can echo on error.** A wrong flag name (`--update-headers` on
  `jobs create` instead of `--headers`) made gcloud print the unrecognised-argument *with its value* — leaking
  `CRON_SECRET` into the transcript. Verify the exact flag first (`gcloud ... --help`), keep the secret in a shell var
  (never a literal), and prefer commands/paths that can't echo it. If one leaks: rotate it — update the env var
  (`--update-env-vars`, new revision) AND every consumer (all 11 Cloud Scheduler `X-Cron-Secret` headers), then confirm
  the OLD value now returns 403. (CRON_SECRET rotation, 2026-06-16)
- **Version any AI prompt and stamp the version on every artifact it generates.** Without it, a prompt redesign
  silently leaves old outputs in place and they're only caught by chance (the #18 stale-draft: an old-format profile sat
  in prod until the owner compared two side by side). Add a `PROMPT_VERSION`, store it on each generated row, and make
  regeneration version-aware (skip rows already on the current version, refresh the rest). Detect staleness by VERSION,
  never by date — and remember an idempotent "has output → skip" sweep will NOT refresh stale rows after a prompt change.
  (Sprint 2026-06-16)
- **Sensitive-attribute redaction must reach the NARRATIVE, not just structured fields.** Hiding ethnicity in the grades
  (group subjects, don't name "Bahasa Tamil") isn't enough — the student's own words ("inspired by her Tamil teacher,
  preserving her mother tongue") leak it too. The fix that preserved meaning was to GENERALISE in the prompt (keep the
  motivation, drop the label: "her mother tongue", "a teacher who inspired her"), applied in the shared redaction block
  so both the draft and the final inherit it. (Sprint 2026-06-16)
- **Don't hand-maintain a PARTIAL lookup map parallel to a canonical one — it drifts and leaks the raw key.** A 15-entry
  `_GRADE_LABELS` missed `b_tamil`, so the raw key `B_TAMIL` was fed to the prompt and surfaced non-deterministically.
  Prefer mapping inputs to a small FIXED safe vocabulary (subject GROUPS) with a generic fallback ("other subjects"), so
  an unmapped input degrades gracefully instead of leaking. And when deleting an i18n PARENT object, verify each LEAF is
  unused — TD-118 deleted `finalProfile` whole when only 6 of 7 leaves were dead. (Sprint 2026-06-16)
- Verify **feature/flag state against the running system**, not against CLAUDE.md or a summary — both rot. State a flag is on/off only after `gcloud run services describe <svc>` shows the env var; state a feature is built/read-only only after reading the code on **`main`** (the worktree), never the primary checkout (which may sit on another agent's branch). Two wrong assertions this round (`/profile` "read-only", `CHECK2_STUDENT_QUERIES_ENABLED` "off") both came from trusting docs + a primary-checkout read. (Sprint 2026-06-15)
- When an Explore/search subagent reads the repo during multi-agent work, it reads the **primary checkout's branch** (possibly not `main`). Point it at the worktree path, or re-verify any load-bearing claim against `main` before reporting it. (Sprint 2026-06-15)
- A feature flag is NOT live just because the Cloud Run env var is set — it must be **read into Django settings** (`settings.X = os.environ.get('X', '')...`). `CHECK2_AUTO_GENERATE` was used via `getattr(settings, 'CHECK2_AUTO_GENERATE', False)` but never defined in any settings file, so it was permanently False and the handoff auto-draft silently never fired. When adding/toggling a flag: grep the settings package for its name; if absent, wire it before touching the env var. (Sprint 2026-06-15)
- This sandbox cannot reach the prod Supabase DB (DNS blocked — that's why schema changes go via the Supabase MCP). To run a one-off command that needs prod DB + an external API (e.g. a Gemini backfill), run it ON the service via the internal cron endpoint (a no-arg management command in `CronRunView.JOBS`) or a Cloud Run Job — not a local `manage.py` run. (Sprint 2026-06-15)

- Bulk data loading via MCP tools should always be delegated to subagents to avoid context exhaustion. Each batch consumes significant context; 20+ batches will overflow the main window. (STPM Sprint 3)
- When modifying grade scales or lookup tables, always run golden master tests immediately — parsed data may contain legacy values not visible in the UI-facing code. (STPM Sprint 5)
- Use constants for localStorage keys instead of string literals to prevent key mismatch bugs across pages built in different sprints. (STPM Sprint 5)
- Always check `class Meta: db_table` in Django models before writing raw SQL against Supabase — default naming (`app_model`) doesn't apply when custom table names are set. **DANGER (v2.21.0): a same-purpose LEGACY table can also exist** — `StudentProfile` is `api_student_profiles` (617 rows), but a legacy Streamlit `student_profiles` (30 rows) is still there. An `ALTER ... ADD COLUMN` against the wrong one **SUCCEEDS silently** (no error — the column just lands on the dead table), so "success" is NOT confirmation. After any migrate-first `ALTER`, verify the column on the table the model actually uses AND sanity-check that a known existing column is present (e.g. the altered table should already have `stream_subjects`). This recurred despite two prior lessons — knowledge wasn't the gap, the check-at-write-time discipline was. (STPM Sprint 6; recurred + sharpened v2.21.0)
- For bulk data loads >500 rows into Supabase, generate a SQL file and use `psql` or a management command instead of MCP batches — each batch consumes context and 20+ batches will overflow the window. (STPM Sprint 6)
- Cloud Run `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` env vars are baked at build time — tagged revisions still call the main API URL unless the frontend is rebuilt with the tagged URL. (STPM Sprint 6)
- Django management commands must read config from `django.conf.settings` first, not `os.environ.get()` — Django doesn't auto-load `.env` into the environment. (STPM Sprint 7)
- For AI taxonomy classification, use a closed set of categories (no "add new if none fits") or include a two-pass approach — open-ended prompts produce hyper-specific values that defeat the purpose of a taxonomy. (STPM Sprint 7)
- Django management commands that modify data must verify the target database before running — print the database host at startup and abort if it's SQLite when production was intended. (STPM Sprint 8)
- When debugging hydration issues with Playwright, always wait for network idle before taking snapshots — pre-hydration snapshots produce misleading "not working" results. (STPM Sprint 8)
- Do not generate data on-the-fly in API responses when it should be a database entry — synthetic entries cause search gaps, badge inconsistency, and separate code paths that diverge over time. (Pre-U Sprint)
- Before inserting data into the DB, check how downstream pages render it — adding bare records can degrade rich UIs that used a different data source. (UI Polish Sprint)
- Always verify key/value mappings against actual API responses, not visual references — uppercase/lowercase mismatches cause silent display failures. (UI Polish Sprint)
- When cross-referencing CSV data with database records, never trust a single column for critical filters (e.g. bumiputera) — verify against a second source file or the institution itself. (Data Integrity Sprint)
- When matching courses across systems with different ID schemes, use name-based matching as fallback — institution types may use entirely different code conventions (FB/FC vs POLY-DIP/KKOM-CET). (Data Integrity Sprint)
- Tech debt audits must include test execution analysis — check pytest output for skipped/xfailed tests, not just code and docs. A test that silently skips is worse than a missing test because it creates false confidence. (Test Health Sprint)
- When migrating data sources (e.g. CSV → DB), immediately update or delete all tests that depend on the old source — `skipIf` guards on file existence mask the problem indefinitely. (Test Health Sprint)
- For server-rendered portals (MOHE, polycc, etc.), HTTP status codes are useless for link validation — always check rendered page content with a headless browser. (External Links Sprint)
- Never guess institution URLs from naming patterns — scrape the authoritative listing page instead. Even within a single system (e.g. IPG), URL conventions are inconsistent. (External Links Sprint)
- Design docs for rename/removal tasks must include a mechanical `grep` for all references before finalising the file list — manual estimates undercount because symbols propagate into tests, views, and utility code. (Subject Key Unification Sprint)
- Before changing a function signature, grep all call sites first — adding a new positional parameter breaks callers that pass options as the second argument. Use options objects for new optional parameters. (Saved Courses Sprint 1)
- When removing `as any`, check all types it was masking — a single `as any` often papers over multiple distinct type mismatches. Run `tsc --noEmit` after removal to catch cascading issues. (Quick Wins Sprint)
- When implementing a tech debt item, read the "What consistent looks like" field first — it defines the acceptance criteria. Skipping it leads to incomplete fixes that need a second pass. (Frontend Cleanup Sprint)
- When adding content for a trilingual project, always use the i18n system — never add content to DB fields that only support 2 languages. Check how many languages the project supports before choosing the storage method. (Tech Debt Quick Wins 2)
- Before writing the first frontend test in a project, verify that Jest is configured for the language (TypeScript needs `ts-jest` transform + path aliases in `jest.config.js`). An installed `jest` package is not enough — the config must exist. (IC Gate Sprint)
- When reusing a classifier or mapper across data sources, verify the semantics of each input column — same column name does not mean same data granularity. STPM `field` == aggregate `category`, unlike SPM where `field` is a specific sub-discipline. (Field Taxonomy Sprint 2)
- When building tools that process pipeline output, always run against real data before marking complete — synthetic test data can have different shapes (e.g. list of strings vs list of dicts) that only surface with production data. (Pipeline Sprint 3)
- Before creating migrations on a feature branch, always check `max(migration number)` on main — parallel branches creating same-numbered migrations is a recurring issue (third time in this project). Rebase or check before numbering. (Admin Auth Sprint)
- **The migration-number rule applies to TD numbers too** — a draft `TD-152` collided with another branch's `TD-152` (bursary donor) and had to be renumbered to `TD-153` mid-sprint. Before adding a TD, `grep 'TD-[0-9]' docs/technical-debt.md | sort` (or fetch+check main); numbering off a stale tree double-books. (Assignment-write-permission Sprint, 2026-07-02)
- **`next build`'s type-check OOMs on the 8 GB dev box after a full pytest run** — it prints "✓ Compiled successfully" then dies with a Jest-`WorkerError` in the parallel type-check phase. That's memory, not a code error. Confirm types with `npx tsc --noEmit --target es2018 --downlevelIteration --skipLibCheck` (bare `tsc` without the target flags surfaces bogus `TS2802 Set-iteration` errors in test files that Next's injected target resolves) and trust Cloud Build (more RAM) — it builds the same tree main does. Don't run the heavy pytest suite and `next build` back-to-back locally. (QC-gate Sprint, 2026-07-02)
- When accessing Stitch designs, use MCP `get_screen` instead of preview URLs — Stitch previews are not publicly accessible and will 404 in Playwright. (Identity Verification Sprint)
- Mark completed sub-tasks explicitly before moving on in long conversations — context loss causes duplicate work and user frustration when previously fixed code is "investigated" again. (Identity Verification Sprint)
- Never apply a fix pattern from one formula to another without independently verifying the target formula is actually wrong — "do the same for X" does not mean the same fix applies. Read the documentation block first. (Post-Launch Hardening Sprint)
- Display logic (how many items to show in a section) belongs in the frontend, not the backend — backend should rank and return a complete sorted list, not pre-split for a specific UI layout. (Post-Launch Hardening Sprint)
- For long-running subagent tasks (scrapers, deploys), verify `git log` before and after — concurrent subagents on the same branch can commit unexpected work while the main task is waiting. Use worktrees for isolation. (Pipeline Completion Sprint)
- When a user reports an "intermittent" bug, compare the failing path to a working path before hypothesising infrastructure causes — the differentiator reveals the actual bug. (i18n & Bug Fixes Sprint)
- Enforce security invariants (like "must have NRIC") via middleware, not per-endpoint checks — a single enforcement point prevents leaks when new endpoints are added and avoids the "check every time we implement a new feature" problem. (NRIC Hard Gate Sprint)
- After subagent work on frontend TSX files, always run `next build` before marking complete — TypeScript/JSX errors (like block-scoped variable ordering) are only caught by the compiler, not by reading the code. (NRIC Hard Gate Sprint)
- When a Django model field stores enum-like strings ("Ya"/"Tidak"), prefer BooleanField from the start — string booleans create type mismatches at serializer boundaries that are hard to diagnose. (i18n & Bug Fixes Sprint)
- localStorage is a cache, not a source of truth — always overwrite from the authoritative backend on login. Conditional writes (`if !localStorage`) cause stale-cache bugs that are hard to reproduce because they only affect returning users. (i18n & Bug Fixes Sprint)
- When fixing a data-type bug at a system boundary, trace the full pipeline (DB → API → frontend → cache → consumer → API) before starting — fixing one segment reveals the next, leading to multiple deploys for what should be one fix. (i18n & Bug Fixes Sprint)
- When applying Django migrations via raw SQL to bypass `InconsistentMigrationHistory`, verify the actual column types match the model AFTER applying — inserting into `django_migrations` only records the migration, it does not execute the DDL. A VARCHAR column read by a BooleanField silently coerces non-empty strings to `True`. (i18n & Bug Fixes Sprint)
- When adding a useEffect to a component with early returns (`if (x) return null`), place ALL hooks before ALL early returns — React's Rules of Hooks require hooks to be called in the same order on every render. A useEffect after an early return will crash when the condition changes. Spec reviewers should verify hook placement relative to conditional returns. (Auth Flow Canonical Refactor Sprint)
- Before drawing a data-driven conclusion that changes architecture (e.g. "use WhatsApp, not email"), verify the **authoritative** source — how the live system actually captures the field — not a convenience/back-office export. A blank or "N/A" column in an admin spreadsheet is not evidence of absence. (B40 Sprint 1)
- The frontend Jest config runs in **node** env (no jsdom, no React Testing Library). Put testable logic in pure modules (`src/lib/*.ts`) and unit-test those; don't write tests that render components — they have no DOM to render into. (B40 Sprint 2)
- When a sprint changes behaviour in an earlier sprint's code path (e.g. intake now also runs shortlisting), update that earlier sprint's tests in the same sprint, and run the **full** suite — a new-tests-only run hides the drift in the older tests. (B40 Sprint 3)
- Access an optional reverse OneToOne (e.g. `application.funding_need`) with `try/except Model.DoesNotExist` — Django's `RelatedObjectDoesNotExist` is **not** an `AttributeError`, so `getattr(obj, 'rel', None)` does not catch it and the access raises. (B40 Sprint 4a)
- The MANDATORY "prototype UI in Stitch before coding templates" rule applies to a multi-sprint build too, not just one-off pages — building the entire applicant + admin frontend across sprints without a single Stitch screen is the same miss at larger scale. Generate and get visual approval on the key screens before the first TSX file, even when the sprint plan reads as "backend + frontend together." (B40 Phase 1)
- When a field has two homes (e.g. financial data on both the application and the profile), name the single source of truth explicitly in the model docstring and make every other place read it live or snapshot it immutably — never store a second mutable copy. Two mutable copies of one fact have no defined winner and silently diverge. (B40 Phase 1.5a)
- Don't conclude a comms channel is dead from a back-office export — verify how the live system captures the field. ~600 students had verified Gmail (Google Sign-In) even though an admin spreadsheet's Email column was blank. (B40 Sprint 1)
- Keep all email/SMTP config env-driven from day one (`EMAIL_HOST`/`EMAIL_HOST_USER`/`EMAIL_HOST_PASSWORD`/`DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL`) — a personal Gmail hardcoded as the sender is both a deploy-time secret leak risk and a deliverability/ownership problem. Default to the provider relay (Brevo) and verify the sender domain. (B40 Phase 1.5a)
- A DRF serializer reused across endpoints (e.g. `ProfileUpdateSerializer` for both `PUT /profile/` and `/profile/sync/`) — toggling a field to `read_only` or changing its validation changes EVERY endpoint that uses it. Enumerate the consumers before editing (here, making `nric` read-only correctly closed the PUT *and* sync write gaps in one change). (B40 Redesign Sprint 7)
- A pure-function engine's hand-built test fixtures (`SimpleNamespace`/dicts) silently return `None` for any attribute the engine reads but the fixture omits — so adding a new engine input (e.g. `household_size`, `upu_status`) won't fail loudly in the old fixtures, it just scores wrongly. When you change an engine's inputs, rebuild its test fixture to include every attribute it now reads. (B40 Redesign Sprint 8)
- In a multi-step / tabbed form, render submit + validation errors at **form level**, not inside one step's panel. If validation can send the user to a different step (e.g. "jump to the tab with the error"), an error message scoped to the last step is invisible exactly when it's needed. (B40 Redesign Sprint 9)
- Don't probe a file path with a chained `ls A || ls B 2>/dev/null` — the failing branch is swallowed and the surviving branch's output doesn't say which path it came from, so you can mis-attribute the result (cost a wrong-path assumption this sprint: i18n is in `src/messages/`, not `src/i18n/`). Run one explicit check per candidate, or echo the resolved path. (B40 Redesign Sprint 9)
- Make browser-storage helpers (localStorage/sessionStorage) **storage-injectable** (`fn(..., storage?: StorageLike)` defaulting to a `safeSession()`/`safeLocal()` that returns null when undefined) — then node-env jest can unit-test the full round-trip with a Map-backed fake, and the helper is SSR-safe for free. (B40 Redesign Sprint 9b)
- A persistent flag (localStorage/sessionStorage) set **before** a multi-step flow goes stale if the flow is abandoned — there's no clean "cancelled" boundary to clear it, so a later unrelated run reads the stale flag. Prefer threading the intent through navigation (query param) over a persistent flag; if you must use a flag, clear it on every legitimate entry/exit boundary and accept the residual abandon edge. (B40 Redesign Sprint 9b)
- Kill background `next dev` servers **by listening port** (`netstat -ano | findstr :PORT` → `taskkill /F /PID`), not `pkill -f "next dev"` — on Windows the detached `next-server` child survives `pkill` and stays bound across turns (causes `EADDRINUSE` on restart, and a stale server serves the OLD bundle so screenshots silently go wrong). Confirm the restart actually bound before navigating. (B40 Redesign Sprint 10)
- Don't assume "push triggers deploy" also runs DB migrations — **verify the deploy actually migrates** before pushing. HalaTuju's Cloud Run triggers are auto-generated build→push→deploy with **no migrate step** (the Dockerfile CMD is just gunicorn), so shipping code that needs new columns would 500 for live users. Apply additive migrations **migrate-first against prod** (old code keeps working), *then* push `main`. (B40 Redesign Sprint 12b)
- Django `AlterField` that only changes a column's **default** does NOT rewrite existing rows — pre-existing rows keep their old value (newly *added* fields do pick up the new default). After changing a default on an existing column, **explicitly sync the existing config rows** (this left the prod cohort on the old 5/3.0 thresholds after the engine was changed to 4/2.9). (B40 Redesign Sprint 12b)
- A safety classifier blocking a prod action (e.g. pulling prod DB creds to migrate) is not a thing to engineer around — **surface it to the user and let them choose** (approve, switch mode, or run it themselves). Honest stop > clever workaround. (B40 Redesign Sprint 12b)
- Running `next build` (production) and then `next dev` over the **same `.next` directory** corrupts the dev chunk graph — the page server-renders fine (screenshots look OK) but the client throws `ChunkLoadError: Loading chunk app/layout failed` and never hydrates, so nothing is interactive. The first dev hit also times out *during* its 10–13 s compile. Before a dev-server preview that follows a build, `rm -rf .next` and let dev rebuild from scratch; give the route one warm-up navigation before interacting. (B40 plans-redesign P2)
- When verifying a prod migration via **raw SQL**, read the model's `Meta.db_table` FIRST — never assume Django's default `<app>_<model>` table name. The scholarship models use custom `db_table` (`scholarship_applications`, not `scholarship_scholarshipapplication`), so a verification query against the default name reported "0/7 columns / table does not exist" mid-deploy — a false alarm. Verify through the ORM/`dbshell`, or grep `db_table` in models.py before querying `information_schema`. (B40 plans-redesign P5 ship)
- On the HalaTuju prod DB, `manage.py migrate` can **exit non-zero on a fully successful migration**: it prints "Applying … OK" then errors `relation "django_content_type" does not exist` from the `post_migrate` signal (create_contenttypes/create_permissions). Prod has **no contenttypes/auth tables** (never created), so that signal always fails — but the additive `ADD COLUMN`s have already committed + recorded in `django_migrations`. A non-zero exit from the post_migrate step is NOT a migration failure; verify the actual columns + the `django_migrations` row directly. (B40 plans-redesign P5 ship; see TD-058)
- A nullable/optional serializer field sent as an **explicit `null`** can still 400 a serializer that only tolerates the field being **omitted** — null and absent are not the same to DRF. If the client may send `null` for an optional field, strip nulls in `to_internal_value` (or mark it `allow_null`). Cost this sprint: the apply page POSTed `coq_score: null` for 100% of profiles → 400 → swallowed into an empty pathway dropdown. (Post-launch apply polish, 2.2.1)
- Normalise enum-like inputs (gender/nationality → the engine's Malay canonical `Lelaki`/`Warganegara`) at **every** engine entry point, not just the one serializer that happens to do it. A second caller (the STPM view) passed raw `male`/`malaysian` straight to the engine and silently returned **0 eligible** for every STPM student. Extract a shared normaliser and call it at the boundary, not per-view. (Post-launch apply polish, 2.2.4)
- A **one-time prefill effect** that locks on the *first* profile snapshot misses fields that arrive later. A brand-new user claims their NRIC at the auth gate **after** the page mounts, so the early snapshot has no NRIC; locking the prefill then left the field blank forever. Gate a one-time prefill on the **awaited field's presence** (`profile.nric`), not merely "the profile object exists". (Post-launch apply polish, 2.2.7)
- Chrome **ignores `autocomplete="off"`** for inputs it heuristically classifies as address/contact and will pop its saved-address list over a custom combobox's own menu. Use `autocomplete="new-password"` on type-to-search comboboxes to suppress it (text input → no password UI), plus `data-1p-ignore`/`data-lpignore` to mute password managers. Note: this can't be reproduced in headless Playwright (no saved addresses) — apply the known-good fix and have the user confirm on their real browser. (Post-launch apply polish, 2.2.6)
- For an **additive** migration on the HalaTuju prod DB, applying the DDL + recording the `django_migrations` row via the Supabase MCP (`execute_sql`, one transaction) is cleaner than `manage.py migrate` here: it sidesteps TD-058's non-zero `post_migrate` exit and the session-pooler/CRLF credential juggling, and you verify the columns + the migration row in the same connection. Replicate Django's DDL exactly — for a `CharField(default='')`, `ADD COLUMN … NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` (backfills existing rows) then `DROP DEFAULT` (Django keeps the default app-side). Only for additive changes; a model-creating migration still needs the contenttypes/auth fix (TD-058). (Post-launch apply polish, 2.3.0)
- Stitch `generate_screen_from_text` **times out client-side on content-dense screens** and a timed-out generation does **not** reliably persist (polling `list_screens` after shows nothing). Don't sit in a poll/retry loop and don't block the sprint on it: generate **one** representative screen to get the *pattern/layout* signed off (that can cover several sprints that reuse it), switch to `GEMINI_3_FLASH` for speed, and prototype the remaining sections **just-in-time before their own sprint** rather than all up front. (Application-redesign S1)
- Under heavy main-thread context, delegate a **contained, well-specced** sprint to a single subagent (fresh context) and have the orchestrator review the diff + screenshots + re-run `next build` before deploying — keeps the sprint within budget without losing the verify/deploy gate. Spec must say "no commit/push/deploy; leave changes in the tree" so deploy stays with the orchestrator. (Application-redesign S1)
- Never run `next build` (or a second `next dev`) against a project's `.next` **while a subagent is doing a dev-server/Playwright screenshot in the same project** — both writing `.next` triggers the build-vs-dev corruption (a bogus `PageNotFoundError: /_document` + non-zero exit, despite "Compiled successfully"). Serialise: wait for the screenshot task to kill its dev server before building, or isolate the screenshot task in its own git worktree. (Application-redesign S2)
- When delegating build+screenshot work to a subagent, tell it to **SAVE screenshots to a named path and NOT delete them** (delete only the throwaway preview route + dev server) — otherwise the orchestrator can't review the UI and has to send a round-trip to regenerate. (Application-redesign S2)
- A **timed-out Stitch generation doesn't fail cleanly — it can persist LATER as a same-titled duplicate.** So when you poll `list_screens` after a timeout, the screen you find may be an OLD attempt (e.g. a pre-revision design), not your latest prompt — verify the rendered CONTENT matches what you just asked for before showing it for sign-off; don't trust the title or assume the first/newest-listed screen is yours. (Application-redesign S3)
- A content-dense Stitch screen still times out on `GEMINI_3_FLASH` when **every repeating element is fully specced** — for a list/repeat-heavy screen, describe each repeating element **once** and summarise the rest ("…and three more rows: X, Y, Z") so the render stays light enough to persist. The trimmed prompt succeeded first-try where the fully-enumerated one (8 cards × full helper text + states + bottom bar) had timed out and not persisted. (Application-redesign S4)
- When adding a new **external-SDK module** (Cloud Vision, Gemini, Stripe, any vendor whose Python or Node package gets a `from vendor` / `import vendor`), the **`requirements.txt` (or `package.json`) bump is part of the same diff as the code** — not a follow-up. Forgetting it doesn't break the build (lazy imports + graceful-degradation just say "module not installed" at runtime) but it does cost an avoidable second deploy when caught later. Cheap catch at push time: after any new `from <vendor>` line, grep `git diff --stat` for `requirements.txt` (or `package.json`) and fail loud if absent. (S13)
- When introducing a feature behind a **billable external API**, ship the code with the API **disabled** first (graceful-degradation path: `ImportError` / "AI service not configured" → soft fallback), then enable the API as a **separate explicit step** after the user's cost sign-off. Lets the code/tests/migration land safely; the fallback path is exercised in production for free; teething surfaces there before any billable call. Then flip the switch + one cheap public-sample-image smoke (e.g. `gs://cloud-samples-data/...`) to prove project-level provisioning before asking the user to upload real data. (S13)
- **When `next build` OOMs (`WorkerError`) more than ONCE in a row, kill stray `node.exe` first — it may be real starvation, not the known transient.** Repeated jest/next runs across a session leak detached worker processes; this sprint 20 stray `node.exe` had accumulated and starved the 8 GB box, failing two consecutive builds. `taskkill //F //IM node.exe` then rebuild → clean. The single-retry rule for the static-gen transient still holds, but a *second* failure means free memory before retrying again. (Award-standardise, 2026-06-29)
- **A new read-serializer field must be added to EVERY frontend type that mirrors that endpoint — not just the admin one.** S6 added `closure_reason` to both the admin `AdminScholarshipDetail` and the student read serializer, but only updated the FE `AdminScholarshipDetail` type — the student `ScholarshipApplication` type still lacked it, so the in-programme page's `app.closure_reason` failed `next build` (TS compile). jest does NOT catch this (node-env, no page type-check); `next build` (raw) does. When you add a field to a Django read serializer, grep the FE for every interface that types that endpoint's response and update each. (Post-award S6)
- **A migration-state drift that `makemigrations` re-proposes every sprint should be RETIRED, not re-dropped each time.** The `scholarshipcohort.name` `help_text` drift has been hand-dropped from the generated migration for 5 consecutive sprints (S1–S5) — a reliable reflex, but pure recurring friction. The fix is a single standalone state-only migration that records the `AlterField` once (no DDL for a help_text change), after which `makemigrations --check` is clean. Logged as TD-147. General rule: when you find yourself manually editing out the *same* autodetector op twice, stop and retire the drift in an isolated no-op migration. (Post-award S5)
- **Branch a new sprint off `origin/main`, never the worktree's local `main` — the local ref goes stale.** A worktree's `main` is only as current as the last `git checkout main && git pull` *in that worktree*; pushing earlier sprints to `origin/main` from a feature branch does NOT advance local `main`. This sprint, `git checkout -b feat/... main` landed 62 commits behind — the S1–S3 migrations vanished and `makemigrations` mis-numbered a colliding `0073`. The trap was masked because a sibling branch happened to equal `origin/main`. Fix: `git fetch` then `git checkout -B <branch> origin/main` (or assert `git rev-list --count main..origin/main` is 0 before branching). Recovery is cheap (reset the branch to origin/main, re-apply edits) but only if you catch it before committing. (Post-award S4)
- **Never pipe `npm run build` (or any pass/fail gate) to `grep` and read the pipeline's exit code as the build's.** The pipe's exit code is the *last* command's — grep's, not npm's. When npm exits 1 with a TypeScript/lint error, grep happily matches the `Failed to compile`/`Type error` lines and returns 0, and you read "EXIT=0" as "build green." This masked **two** broken web pushes for TD-059 (deploy-budget breach: 3 pushes instead of 2). Either `set -o pipefail` at the start of the shell, or check `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` explicitly, or capture to a file (`npm run build > build.log 2>&1; echo "EXIT=$?"`) and read the unmasked exit code separately from any greps. (TD-059)
- **When changing a shared model's shape, grep ALL parallel type definitions before declaring done.** This project keeps two frontend interfaces for `FundingNeed`: `lib/api.ts` (student-facing) and a separately-typed `lib/admin-api.ts AdminScholarshipDetail.funding_need` (admin-facing). Updating only the first broke `next build` on the admin page (`Record<string, number | string>` left `categories?.length` typed as `string | number` → no `.length`). Before pushing a model-shape change, `grep -rn "<ModelName>"` across `lib/` and confirm every parallel definition matches. (TD-059)
- When a unit wraps an external call (AI/Gemini, HTTP, etc.), **extract the payload/prompt builder as a pure function and unit-test it directly** — don't rely only on tests that mock the *whole* call. The AI sponsor-profile generator referenced four model fields the profile-canonical refactor had removed and would have 500'd on a real "Generate", but the only test mocked the entire `generate_sponsor_profile`, so the broken `_build_prompt` was never executed and the schema drift failed nowhere (rotted silently across the refactor + several sprints, masked further by the dormant programme / no CI API key). A pure-builder test (`test_profile_engine.py`) makes input-schema drift fail loudly while the external call stays mocked. (Application-redesign S5c, TD-060)
- Don't run the **full backend `pytest` suite concurrently with `next build`** on this (8GB-RAM) machine. `apps/courses` loads the large course DataFrame at app init (`AppConfig.ready()`), and under memory/CPU contention from the Node build, `TestEligibilityEndpoint` setUp raises `ERROR` (not assertion failures) — 24 spurious errors that vanish (`24 passed`) when the class is re-run alone. Serialise the two, or run the eligibility tests separately. Treat a burst of **setup ERRORS confined to one heavy test class** as a contention smell, not a regression — re-run in isolation before believing it. (Application-redesign S5a)
- When proposing to **delete a field that has a confusingly-similar name to a kept field**, name the exact DB column + state both readers and writers in the proposal.
