"Drop the phone input" is ambiguous when there are two phone columns; "drop the dead `phone` column (no readers/writers outside /profile); keep `contact_phone` (canonical, /apply ⇄ /profile)" is not. The user reasonably pushed back on the ambiguous framing — would have been faster to disambiguate the first time. (S14)
- For an opinionated default (e.g. "auth email *is* the contact email unless the user says otherwise"), prefer a **read-time fallback in the API** over a **one-shot DB backfill**. The backfill mutates rows the user didn't choose, irreversibly merging "they actively chose this" with "we wrote it for them" — and locks the policy. A read-time fallback is one line to change later, naturally handles new users (phone signups have no auth email to fall back to), and lets admin SQL distinguish "explicit" from "implicit" by inspecting the empty column. Used for `contact_email` in S14 (574 rows untouched). (S14)
- **Unit tests on a parsing/OCR heuristic are necessary but not sufficient — the heuristic also has to be validated against ONE real document before deploy.** Synthetic test fixtures encode my assumptions about the document's structure (where the state line sits relative to postcode; what a "name line" looks like). On a real MyKad the assumptions are subtly wrong (state sits BELOW postcode in Vision's reading order; "TAMAN SEMANGAT" looks identical to a one-word name under "all-caps no-digit" filtering). S15's Vision address extraction took **3 deploys** to converge because each iteration's tests passed but the real-document output was wrong. **Practical fix for the next OCR heuristic:** before shipping, ask the user to send a real-document screenshot, dump the OCR text for it, and validate the heuristic against THAT text — not just synthetic samples. The same protocol catches the same class of bug for ANY parser-from-real-world-input (PDFs, scanned forms, screenshots, etc.). (S15)
- **In plan mode, the Explore brief must explicitly verify model location for any migration-class task** — not rely on my mental model of which model the field belongs to. S15 brief said `siblings_studying` lives on `StudentProfile`; it actually lives on `ScholarshipApplication`. The Plan agent caught it before any code was written. Cost was a re-write of the migration name + scoped target. The fix is procedural: when scoping a column add/drop/move, the Explore agent prompt must include "confirm which model the field currently lives on by reading the model file" as an explicit step. The Explore agent confirmed surrounding context but didn't verify the model location because I didn't ask. (S15)
- **When a user-facing list looks "too short", grep for a SECOND copy of that list on the other side of the wire before assuming the UI is the only fix.** The SPM stream dropdowns were under-populated (frontend `subjects.ts` pools), but the merit engine kept its OWN hardcoded copy of the same pools (`engine.py`), and the 30% stream weight was computed from the backend copy. Fixing only the frontend would have let students pick subjects that silently scored on the 10% elective weight instead of 30% — a worse, invisible bug than the original. Whenever the fix is "add items to a list that drives behaviour", ask: does any other layer (engine, serializer, validator, report) hold a parallel copy that must move in lockstep? If yes, change both in the same commit and add paired tests asserting equal membership/size. (S18)
- **`status='submitted'` ≠ "user is at /application Step 4."** The decision engine scores silently at submit and the applicant stays at `submitted` until the +48h scheduler fires; only `shortlisted` applicants see the post-shortlist Step-4 tabbed UI (Quiz/Story/Funding/Documents/Consent). Pre-reveal applicants see the "received" status card. **Before scoping any change that affects the /application Step-4 UI, run `SELECT status, COUNT(*) FROM scholarship_applications GROUP BY status` to see who actually sees the affected UI today** — not just `WHERE submitted_at IS NOT NULL`. In S19 I mis-framed a "parent_ic universal compulsory" change as having retroactive impact on 12 submitted applications; user pushed back, and verifying showed all 12 were pre-reveal — the change was forward-looking, not retroactive. The wrong framing would have caused unnecessary worry / unnecessary work. (S19)
- **When the user names a UX/convention explicitly** ("let's be consistent across the forms"), encode it in CODE not just docs — a tiny component (`InfoBox` in S19; `FieldLabel` extracted in S15) is the cheapest enforcement mechanism and self-documents via prop types. Inline `className` strings + a comment are forgettable; a typed component is not. (S19)
- **`for…of set` triggers Next.js downlevel-iteration TS errors** on this project's tsconfig target. Prefer `Array.from(set).every(…)` or `.some(…)` for Set/Map iteration in `.tsx` files — same intent, no downlevel flag needed. (S19)
- **To change golden-master-protected logic safely, prove it with a *differential audit*, not just "tests pass."** For TD-063 (merit stream classification) the verification that gave real confidence was: (1) the golden master staying byte-identical at 5319 proved the untouched/fallback path didn't move, and (2) a script that ran a matrix of inputs through BOTH the old and new code paths and printed *every* divergence — so each difference could be eyeballed and confirmed as an intended fix (cross-stream students the heuristic mis-classified) rather than a regression. The audit surfaced a design trap a plain unit test wouldn't have (storing a *subset* of stream subjects can lower a score → must store the full list). Bake the audit's cases into permanent unit tests once green, then delete the throwaway script. (TD-063, 2026-05-30)
- **A cross-language data duplication (TS pool ↔ Python pool) is often a symptom, not the disease.** The real cause is usually that one side discards information the other then has to reconstruct. Before reaching for codegen or a shared-JSON build step to keep two copies in sync, check whether the consumer can simply be *told* the answer (here: send the student's stream pick) so the second copy degrades to a fallback. Cheaper and removes the drift risk for live data. (TD-063, 2026-05-30)
- **"Drop the dead column" often isn't just a migration — verify the *expand* step actually happened first.** TD-061's columns were described as "dead" (frontend stopped writing them in S14/S15), but they were still wired into the `/profile` GET response, the update serializer, both admin serializers, the CSV export, and the AI prompt. Worse, the swap was half-done in a way that hid a real bug: `/profile` read/wrote the *canonical* `household_income`/`household_size` while the GET response + serializer still listed only the *legacy* `family_income`/`siblings`, so `/profile` edits to household income/size were silently dropped (DRF ModelSerializer ignores unknown input keys; the GET never returned the canonical ones). Before scheduling a column drop, grep every reader/writer (`SELECT`/serializer fields/response dicts/CSV/admin/FE types) and confirm the canonical replacement is actually used end-to-end — the "dead" column may still be load-bearing, and the canonical one may be silently inert. (TD-061, 2026-05-30)
- **A new cross-language data/identity surface is often a symptom that one side discards information.** Before building a "sponsor login" (or any new auth/portal), confirm it has a *destination* — a model, a view, something the user lands on. "Sponsor login" had none (only admin-generated SponsorProfile markdown), so the right move was a register-interest lead capture, not a fake account. Building auth before its product is a door into an empty room. (Post-shortlist sprint, 2026-05-30)
- **Test-green is not ship-confidence for a multi-screen stateful flow facing imminent real users.** Phase C (apply→confirm→assign→interview→accept) was verified by pytest + health checks (401-not-500) but never click-tested in a browser before the real batch. For any multi-step stateful UX with imminent users, add an explicit interactive smoke (Playwright MCP against the live test account) to the deploy step — endpoint health checks don't exercise the screens. (Post-shortlist sprint, 2026-05-30)
- **When asked to copy a UI/pattern from another product, check the product *model* matches before porting it.** A marketplace's "register to enter" flow is wrong for a public-utility tool (the open course guide). Evaluate the borrowed pattern against the existing funnel's value prop, not just its looks. (Post-shortlist sprint, 2026-05-30)
- **When unifying voice/pronouns across a form, enforce one referent per pronoun on a single screen — not literal fidelity to the request.** The request "Support I'd Like From You" reads cleanly in isolation, but that step's *questions* already use "you" = the student ("Would you like help…?"), so the title's "you" = the org would put two referents for one pronoun on one screen — a new inconsistency dressed as a fix. The repair was "Support I'd Like From **Us**" (I = student, us = org), keeping the form's established "you = student / us = org" contract. Before changing any pronoun in shared copy, grep the surrounding strings for the *other* uses of that pronoun and confirm they all still point at the same party. (v2.16.5, 2026-05-30)
- **Set `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` for any inline `python -c` that prints Tamil/non-Latin text on this machine, and never dump scratch files to `/tmp`.** Windows console defaults to cp1252; Tamil code points raise `UnicodeEncodeError` mid-print (the *data* is fine — only stdout choked), and `open('/tmp/…')` silently fails because there is no `/tmp` on Windows. Both wasted a round-trip more than once. Prefix the command with the env var and write scratch dumps to a real workspace path. (v2.16.5, 2026-05-30)
- **Reuse a technical pattern across features, but never reuse its trigger model without re-deriving *who acts and when*.** Doc-assist and the interview gap-spotter share plumbing (Gemini + structured JSON + a deterministic decider), so the first plan gave both the same admin-on-demand trigger — and the user rejected it. Gap-spotter serves the *admin* preparing an interview; doc-assist serves the *student* fixing a bad upload *at upload time*, so an admin trigger re-introduces the very round-trip the feature removes. Before reusing a trigger/flow from a sibling feature, state the actor and the moment for each feature explicitly; if they differ, the trigger must differ even when the plumbing is identical. (v2.17.0, 2026-05-31)
- **Route every LLM/external-AI call through one tiny private seam so the whole AI surface mocks by patching a single function.** Both Gemini engines (doc-assist + gap-spotter) and the structured-JSON variant go through `vision._call_gemini_json`; every test `@patch`es that one seam, so CI runs 24 AI-feature tests with zero billable calls and no SDK import. When adding a new AI capability, extend the existing seam rather than calling the SDK directly from the new engine — it keeps the mock boundary singular and the verdict logic (which must stay deterministic) testable in isolation. (v2.17.0, 2026-05-31)
- **Never run two Claude instances against the same working tree — give each its own `git worktree`.** During v2.20.0 a second instance was doing the v2.19.0 sprint-close in the SAME checkout; this instance read v2.19.0's uncommitted doc edits as "abandoned leftovers from a previous session" and committed them, colliding with the other instance's close (resolved by a `git reset --soft` + one consolidated commit). The pre-flight "commit any uncommitted work" rule assumes a single writer — it is actively wrong when another instance is mid-edit. Before touching git in a shared checkout, confirm no other instance/agent is active; for genuinely parallel work use `git worktree add` so each has an isolated tree, index, and HEAD. (Sharpens the subagent-worktree note; v2.20.0, 2026-05-31)
- **For an admin↔student data wall, make it structural (the function can't receive the data), not prompt-trust (the prompt asks it not to leak).** The Cikgu Gopal help engine takes only `doc_type`/`verdict`/`first_name`/`target_language` — no application/profile/score object — and a signature test asserts exactly those four params. This is a stronger, cheaper guarantee than a system-prompt "never reveal scores" instruction (which a clever prompt could still coax). When a feature must not expose a class of data, design the seam so the data is *absent*, then test the absence. (v2.20.0, 2026-05-31)
- **A second auth scope that shares the one auth context must route its OAuth through its OWN one-shot flag, not the shared pending-action key — otherwise it triggers the first scope's post-login gate.** HalaTuju has one `AuthProvider`; a signed-in-but-NRIC-less user is `status='needs-nric'`, and the resume effect opens the student NRIC modal whenever `KEY_PENDING_AUTH_ACTION` is set after login. The sponsor sign-in deliberately uses a separate `KEY_SPONSOR_SIGNIN` (sessionStorage) read only by `/auth/callback`, so it never populates the pending-action key and the NRIC modal never fires for a sponsor — with zero edits to the fragile `AuthGateModal`. When adding any new login scope (Phase F mentor, etc.) onto a shared auth context, give it an isolated return-flag and confirm the other scope's gate stays dormant; don't overload the existing reason enum unless that scope genuinely needs the same post-login flow. (v2.22.0, 2026-05-31)
- **For any sprint that touches MONEY or legal/regulatory ground, surface that dimension and confirm the model with the user BEFORE designing the schema — don't infer it from a roadmap line.** E3's roadmap had "deferred money to a later phase", so I scoped + stubbed the match with no money; the user's actual intent included the funding, and reframing it mid-sprint reshaped the whole data model (a `Sponsorship` stub was thrown away). The reframe also *improved* it: "hold a sponsor's money + refund to bank" (fund custody — regulated) became "a **final donation** to the charity + an **internal directed-giving balance**" (bookkeeping), which sidesteps the custody/refund problem. The lesson is to ask the money/legal question first, not to rely on a pause catching it. (v2.26.0, 2026-06-01)
- **Model pooled/allocated funds as a LEDGER of states (e.g. balance = donations − holding allocations), not a mutable balance field you debit and refund.** "Release on lapse" then becomes a status change — the allocation simply stops counting — with no refund transaction, no drift, and a fully auditable trail; and framing inbound money as a *final donation* where possible turns a custody/refund problem into internal bookkeeping. (v2.26.0, 2026-06-01)
- **For a hard "this data must never leak" boundary, use a plain `Serializer` with explicit `SerializerMethodField`s — NOT a `ModelSerializer` — and prove it with a planted-identifier leak test.** The sponsor-pool card/detail (`SponsorPoolCardSerializer`/`SponsorPoolDetailSerializer`) expose only derived, non-identifying values; because there is no model passthrough, a future field added to `StudentProfile`/`ScholarshipApplication` literally cannot reach a sponsor unless someone writes a method for it (allowlist by construction, not by remembering to exclude). The test plants a real name/NRIC/address/phone/email/school on the fixture and asserts none appears in `json.dumps(serializer.data)` — so the guarantee is verified, not promised. A `ModelSerializer` with `exclude=` or even `fields=` is the wrong tool here: it re-includes by default and a new field can silently slip through. (v2.24.0, 2026-05-31)
- **When an LLM generates text shown to an outside party who must NOT learn identifying facts, add a deterministic pre-publish scan for the SUBJECT's own identifying tokens as a structural gate — prompt-instruction + human review are necessary but not sufficient.** The anonymous sponsor blurb is generated from non-identifying inputs but fed the student's free-text narrative, so it *could* echo a name/school/place. `pool.scan_anon_for_identifiers` checks the blurb for the student's own name/school/city/NRIC/phone/email tokens and `AdminPublishAnonProfileView` blocks publish on a hit. Stoplist the generic tokens (school-type words, name connectors) to limit false positives, and **err toward blocking** — for an anonymity gate a false positive is a minor annoyance, a false negative is the actual harm. (v2.25.1, 2026-06-01)
- **A flag-gated backend needs a frontend that DEGRADES to the pre-feature state on the gated error — then one env var keeps BOTH tiers dark.** When `SPONSOR_POOL_ENABLED` is off, the pool API 404s; the `/sponsor` frontend catches that and shows the existing "coming soon" shell rather than an error screen. So shipping the whole feature (backend + frontend) to prod with the flag off changes nothing a user sees, and flipping the one flag lights up both tiers at once. Design the gated-error catch into the FE deliberately (treat 404 as "not available yet", not as a failure), so the dark deploy is genuinely invisible end-to-end. (v2.25.0, 2026-05-31)
- **When a privacy-critical feature must ship dark, gate it with a master env flag (default OFF → 404) and build/test on synthetic data — don't park it on a branch.** E2a (the anonymised pool) landed on `main` behind `SPONSOR_POOL_ENABLED=False`: the code, migration, and tests are in, the 404 fallback is exercised in prod for free, there's no long-lived branch to rot, and going live is a one-env-var decision after the external (lawyer) gate. Prefer this over "build on a branch until approved" whenever the off-state is a safe no-op. (v2.24.0, 2026-05-31)
- **Session isolation has a LOGOUT half, not just a login half — `signOut()` defaults to `scope: 'global'` (revokes every session for the identity), and a prefix-greedy bulk-clear can wipe sibling scopes' storage.** After fixing the *login* bleed with PKCE (v2.23.1), student logout still logged out admin/sponsor (v2.23.2): `clearAll()` deleted every `halatuju_*` key incl. the sibling `halatuju_admin_session`/`halatuju_sponsor_session`, and all three `signOut()` used the default global scope (one Google identity shared across the three clients → global revoke kills the siblings server-side). Fix: exclude sibling session keys from the bulk clear + use `signOut({ scope: 'local' })` everywhere. When you isolate multi-scope/multi-client auth, audit **both** directions (login *and* logout) and check any `localStorage` bulk-clear for keys it shouldn't own. (v2.23.2, 2026-05-31)
- **Any browser Supabase client that does OAuth must set `flowType: 'pkce'` — a separate `storageKey` alone does NOT isolate sessions.** Under the supabase-js default (`flowType: 'implicit'`, confirmed `auth-js@2.95.3` `GoTrueClient.js:24`), OAuth returns the session in the URL **hash** (`#access_token=…`), which *any* client mounted on the callback page reads regardless of its storage key. HalaTuju's student `AuthProvider` is mounted **globally** (`app/providers.tsx`), so it sat on `/admin/auth/callback` + `/sponsor/auth/callback` and silently copied the admin/sponsor Google session into the student storage key (admin login → also logged into the student app; admin logout didn't clear it). PKCE returns a `?code=` exchangeable only with the verifier under the *initiating* client's key, so a non-initiating client can't claim it. This hole existed from the admin client (March) and was invisible until a third scope (sponsor) prompted the question. Treat OAuth session isolation as something to **reason about and set explicitly (PKCE) on every client**, not something a distinct storageKey gives you for free, and not something "nobody reported it" can vouch for. (v2.23.1, 2026-05-31)
- **For a NEW user-type's auth, start from the isolated-Supabase-client pattern (admin: own `storageKey` + own `*AuthProvider`) — do NOT reuse the student client, even if the first cut only needs Google.** The project already settled this for admins ("Separate admin auth with isolated Supabase clients", decisions.md 2026-03-16). E1 (v2.22.0) ignored it and rode the student client with a flag because Google-only "just about" worked; adding email/password one day later (E1c) forced ripping it out and rebuilding on `sponsor-supabase.ts` + `SponsorAuthProvider`. The shared-client shortcut is a false economy the moment the new scope needs email/password, a distinct post-login destination, or to NOT be entangled with the student anonymous/NRIC machinery — which is almost always. Default to isolation on the first commit. (v2.23.0, 2026-05-31; supersedes the `KEY_SPONSOR_SIGNIN` half of the v2.22.0 lesson above)
- **Changing a Gemini/doc-assist extraction JSON shape has a hidden frontend consumer — the generic field renderer.** S2 changed the results-slip extraction from `subjects: [name]` to `results: [{subject, grade}]`; the admin doc-fields renderer does `Array.isArray(v) ? v.join(', ') : v`, which prints `[object Object]` for an array of objects. The extraction lives in the backend (`vision.py` `_FIELD_SCHEMAS`) but its *shape* is consumed verbatim by `Object.entries(vision_fields.fields).map(...)` on the frontend. Before changing any extracted-field shape, grep the renderer(s) of `vision_fields.fields` (and any typed interface like `AdminApplicantDocument.vision_fields`) and update the element handling + the TS type in the SAME diff. (Verification-verdict S2)
- **Build a new verdict/decision surface on top of the SAME function that already gates the related action — don't re-derive the logic in parallel.** The Identity verdict reuses `services._ic_identity_blockers` (which also gates consent), so the scorecard and the consent gate can never disagree about the same IC. Re-implementing the name/NRIC match logic in the verdict engine would have created a second copy that silently drifts (cf. the recurring FE/BE pool-duplication lessons). When adding a read-only "what's the status" surface, source it from the existing enforcement function, not a fresh re-derivation. (Verification-verdict S1)
- **Recover a timed-out Stitch generation via the preview URL's `node-id`, not `list_screens`.** `generate_screen_from_text` on a content-dense prompt times out client-side but persists *late* (the #72/#76 behaviour); meanwhile `list_screens` appears to page/cap (~10 items) so the just-created screen isn't necessarily near the top, and polling it can falsely look like "nothing generated." The reliable recovery: the Stitch **preview URL** the user can copy contains `?node-id=<id>` — that `<id>` is the **screen id**, so `get_screen` (name `projects/<p>/screens/<id>`) resolves it directly. Verify the returned `title`/content matches what you asked for (a timed-out gen can also persist as a duplicate). (Verification-verdict S4)
- **When you delegate a contained UI sprint to a subagent, re-run the gates yourself before committing — don't trust the subagent's report.** S4 was built by a subagent that reported build EXIT=0 / jest / parity all green; the orchestrator still independently re-ran `npm run build` (EXIT=0), the helper jest, and `check-i18n`, and read the new component + the api/wiring diffs, before committing. A subagent's self-reported "verified" is a claim, not evidence; the cost to re-run the gates is minutes and it's the only way the verify/deploy gate stays real. (Sharpens #73; Verification-verdict S4)
- **A subagent can name a gate command that doesn't exist and assert a config fact that's false — re-run/verify against the repo, not the report, even when the visible result looks right.** In S5 the FE subagent reported `npm test → 226 passed`, but there is **no `test` script** in this project's `package.json` (jest runs via `npx jest`); and it replaced every `primary-600` with `indigo-600` claiming `primary-600` "is not defined" when `primary.600` (#1066c2) **is** in `tailwind.config`. Both claims were wrong; the jest *count* was right (re-run `npx jest` to confirm) and the indigo *outcome* was harmless (this admin page already uses indigo as its accent). The discipline isn't "distrust the result", it's "verify the **command** and the **reasoning**": run the real gate command yourself, and check any asserted config/constant against the actual config file. (Sharpens #117; Verification-verdict S5)
- **For a dense DESKTOP admin screen, Stitch-prototype ONE pattern per screen from the start — don't ask for the whole cockpit in one generation.** A fully-specced desktop cockpit (4 verdict tiles + draft + caveats + a grouped documents drawer + a 4-toggle record panel + tools) timed out **twice** client-side and didn't appear in `list_screens` for minutes; both attempts then persisted *late* as duplicates (the #72/#76 behaviour, but worse on desktop density). A trimmed single-purpose prompt (the documents drawer alone) succeeded **synchronously** first try. So: for desktop, generate one representative component-screen at a time (drawer; verdict panel; …) rather than the all-in-one; reuse already-approved patterns for the rest; recover any timed-out gen via the preview URL's `node-id` → `get_screen` and verify its CONTENT (a stale same-device screen can masquerade as yours). (Sharpens #72/#77/#116; Verification-verdict S5)
- **A module-level constant referenced before it's defined throws at import, not at call — Python binds top-to-bottom.** Check-1 placed a new schema (`_IC_GEMINI_SCHEMA`) high in `vision.py` but reused the `_STR` shorthand defined ~80 lines lower (in the supporting-doc section) → `NameError: name '_STR' is not defined` at test collection (import time), before any test ran. When reusing a shared module constant in a NEW block, confirm the constant is defined ABOVE your block, or inline the literal. Cheap to catch — running the gates before commit surfaced it immediately. (Check-1 Identity)
- **A prompt instruction can silently degrade extraction in a way unit tests won't catch — prefer deterministic post-processing when both can do the job.** Check-1 Academic added a "drop the Malay grade-band words from the subject" instruction to the results-slip Gemini prompt; the one slip extracted under it returned an EMPTY results table. The deterministic `academic_engine._split_band` already handled the bands, so the instruction was reverted. When a regex/post-processor and a prompt tweak can both achieve a transformation, do it deterministically — it's testable and can't perturb the rest of the model's output. (Check-1 Academic)
- **Adding an extracted (doc-assist) field does NOT retro-extract documents already uploaded — they keep the OLD field set until re-run.** Check-1 Pathway added `candidate_nric` to the offer-letter extraction; an offer uploaded before the deploy showed IC "Couldn't read" because its stored `vision_fields` predated the field. Fix = Re-run/re-upload. So: when you add an extracted field, expect existing rows to lack it; render the absence honestly ("Couldn't read", not "Not checked"), and tell the user a re-run re-extracts. Backfilling old docs would require a (billable) bulk re-extract — usually not worth it. (Check-1 Pathway)
- **A new verdict reason code must be wired in FOUR places, or it renders a raw i18n key.** `offer_no_identity` (Check-1 pathway) was added to `verdict_engine` + `resolution.CODE_TO_TICKET` + the officer i18n block, but **not** to `actionCentre.KNOWN_CODES` — so the student Action Centre treated it as an unknown/officer item and rendered `admin.scholarship.verdict.item.offer_no_identity` verbatim. The complete chain for any new system reason code: (1) `verdict_engine` emits it → (2) `resolution.CODE_TO_TICKET` maps it (fact/kind/doc_type) → (3) officer tile copy at `admin.scholarship.verdict.item.<code>` ×3 locales → (4) student copy at `scholarship.actionCentre.item.<code>` ×3 locales **AND** the code added to `actionCentre.ts KNOWN_CODES`. Miss #4 and the officer tile looks fine while the student sees the raw key. (Check-1 live-testing fixes)
- **Don't reuse one document type's `vision_*` column as a signal for a different document type — use that document's own authoritative status.** The officer Academic verdict judged *results-slip* readability partly from `vision_name_match`, which is the supporting-doc/IC full-text heuristic and is left blank/`not_found` for some slip name spellings even on a perfect read → a clean slip showed "could not be read" AND "entered 8 of 9 subjects" at once. A results slip has its own name check (`academic_engine._slip_name_status`, sv-authoritative — the same one the student checklist uses). When wiring a fact's verdict, source each signal from THAT fact's own engine, not a column that happens to exist on the row from another pipeline. (Check-1 live-testing fixes)
- **Geometric normalisation (de-rotation/deskew) must be GATED to the case it fixes — applying it unconditionally perturbs the cases that were already fine.** The first slip rotation attempt de-rotated EVERY slip by its measured angle; OCR angle-noise on upright slips nudged adjacent rows and broke a clean upright slip (Theepicaa FIZIK↔KIMIA). The shipped fix only de-rotates when the median word angle is clearly off-axis (|θ|≥25°); upright slips are untouched. And: validate any OCR-geometry change against REAL captured fixtures of every orientation before deploy — not synthetic ones, not the live UI. (Check-1 live-testing fixes)
- **`manage.py sqlmigrate` shows SQLite DDL in dev — NOT the Postgres DDL that runs on prod.** Dev is SQLite, prod is Supabase Postgres. SQLite emulates `ADD COLUMN` with a full table rebuild (CREATE new / INSERT-SELECT / DROP / RENAME), which is alarming and useless for a prod migrate-first; Postgres just does `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`. For a migrate-first, apply the **Postgres** `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`s via the Supabase MCP (additive, nullable or `DEFAULT ''`), then record the `django_migrations` row, then VERIFY the columns + the row exist before pushing. Don't paste the local `sqlmigrate` output at prod. (Income Check-1)
- **`x || ''` widens a string-literal union to `string`, which then fails a typed call — and jest won't catch it, only `next build` will.** Reading wizard answers as `app.income_route || ''` produced `string`, not `'' | 'str' | 'salary'`, so `incomeRequirements(ans)` failed the type-check. Jest (babel/ts-jest, no full type-check) passed; `next build` (real `tsc`) caught it. Fix: annotate the state or build a typed object with `as <Union>` at the call site. Reminder that the gate for FE type-safety is `next build`/`tsc`, not jest. (Income Check-1)
- **The resolution layer keys student tickets by `code` (one open ticket per code per application) — so a verdict that can legitimately raise the SAME code for several subjects must AGGREGATE, not emit duplicates.** The multi-earner income verdict initially emitted `earner_ic_missing` once per member with a member-tagged `_item`; `_ticketable_unresolved` builds a `{code: …}` dict and `ResolutionItem` has a per-(application, code) uniqueness, so two members' missing ICs would collapse to one ticket (or hit IntegrityError). Fix: collect the members and emit ONE item carrying a `members` list param; render it as a localized, joined label list. Before emitting a repeatable verdict item, check whether the ticket layer can hold more than one of that code — if not, aggregate into a list param. (Income Check-1 multi-earner)
- **A locale-agnostic verdict item that carries a CODE param (member, category, …) needs a t-aware param renderer, not raw `String(v)`.** The income `members` param is `['father','brother']`; `String(v)` yields "father,brother" (raw, un-localized). A shared `localiseParams(params, t)` maps the code array through `t('…member.<code>')` and joins — used by BOTH the student Action Centre and the officer tile so the same gap reads naturally in en/ms/ta. When a verdict/anomaly param is a code (or list of codes) rather than display text, localize it at render, and route both consumers through the one helper. (Income Check-1 multi-earner)
- **A verdict that gates on DECLARED INTENT must fall back to OBSERVED EVIDENCE — and any new gating field needs a legacy-data audit before it ships.** The income verdict was built around the wizard answers (route/earner/member tags) as hard gates, assuming the student uploads exactly what the declared route expects. Live testing (app #21) found a confirmed STR recipient who uploaded the father's **salary slip** instead of an STR screenshot getting red "no proof of income" — the STR branch only accepts an `str` doc, so the payslip in the drawer was ignored. Separately, the whole pipeline submitted **before** the wizard existed, so 6 submitted apps have `income_route=''` and untagged docs the new cluster keying can't assemble. Two cross-cutting takeaways: (1) when a verdict gates on a user's *declared* category, always fall back to verifying the *evidence actually present* (the declaration is a hint, not a gate); (2) before shipping a verdict that keys off a NEW field (route, tag, flag), audit the existing rows that predate it — a pipeline audit (how many submitted apps lack the new field?) is part of the migrate-first checklist, not an afterthought. Here it showed only ~7 of a scary-looking 15 actually need remediation (9 were merely shortlisted and self-heal). (Income Check-1 multi-earner, 2026-06-05)
- **Changing the completeness/consent gate breaks every "complete-app" test helper at once — grep for ALL of them before declaring done.** "A complete application" is encoded independently in multiple fixtures (`_make_ready`, `_make_complete`, `_complete`, `_complete_app`, plus inline compulsory-doc builders), each hard-coding the old doc set (`ic + results_slip + parent_ic + str`); there is no single shared fixture, so the gate v2 change (offer letter compulsory + route-aware income) invalidated 14 tests across 4 files at once, with no compile-time link to find them. Before editing `application_completeness`/`consent_blockers`, grep for every helper that builds the compulsory-doc set and update them in lockstep — and always run the FULL suite (never new-tests-only); that is the only thing that surfaces the drift. (Sharpens #48; TD-085 S1, 2026-06-05)
- **A cockpit/admin feature that reads application fields must have them in the ADMIN serializer's `Meta.fields` — it's a curated allowlist, not the full model, and not necessarily parity with the student serializer.** TD-085 S2's `incomeDocLayout` needed `income_route`/`income_earner`/`income_working_members`, but `AdminApplicationDetailSerializer.Meta.fields` didn't list them (nor did the FE `AdminScholarshipDetail` type) — so `next build` failed AND the data wouldn't have been present at runtime. The student `ApplicantDocumentSerializer` having the per-fact `*_check` methods did NOT mean the admin detail exposed the application's own fields. Before building an admin/cockpit surface that reads model fields, grep the admin serializer's `Meta.fields` (and the matching FE type) and add anything missing in the same diff. `next build` catches the FE half; the serializer half is silent until runtime. (TD-085 S2, 2026-06-05)
- **For a Stitch redesign of an EXISTING screen, prefer "show the already-approved pattern + describe the delta" over regenerating the whole screen — especially when Stitch is being flaky.** TD-085 S2's fresh coloured mock timed out and dropped (the content-dense flakiness, #72); rather than burn retries, the existing S5 "Officer Documents Drawer" was used as the layout base and the single delta (per-fact colour coding) described for sign-off. Faster, and the user still approves the visual change. Reserve a fresh generation for genuinely new layouts. (TD-085 S2, 2026-06-05)
- **Before writing or re-wording a coach/verdict message, trace which matcher actually PRODUCES it and confirm the target document — don't write copy against an assumed check.** The user's "IC number doesn't match the one registered to your account" wording literally fits the *student's own IC* (identity match vs profile NRIC), but the screenshot was the *father's IC* — a cluster check that never compares the number to the account. Starting from the desired copy instead of the producing verdict nearly put a truthful-sounding but wrong message on the wrong card. For any help_engine/verdict copy change: open `verdict_for_document` (or the relevant engine), find the exact branch + the data it compares, and confirm the document/route with the user before drafting words. (Sprint Gopal-polish, 2026-06-06)
- **For a scripted multi-language/file edit with embedded quotes or unicode, write the script to a temp file and run it — never pipe it through a bash heredoc.** Rewriting the 19 i18n fallback strings inline via `python - <<'PYEOF'` broke on apostrophes (`'Lulus'`, English contractions) — bash mis-parsed the quoted body. A `Write` to `_scratch.py` + `python _scratch.py` + delete is reliable for any non-trivial scripted edit (i18n bulk rewrites, JSON surgery). (Sprint Gopal-polish, 2026-06-06)
- **A uniqueness rule enforced in a view almost always has a DB twin — check `Meta.constraints` before relaxing it.** Relaxing only the create-view's `.exists()` guard to allow re-apply after an auto-close still failed with `IntegrityError: UNIQUE constraint failed (cohort_id, profile_id)` — there was a model-level `UniqueConstraint`. The TDD test caught it. When changing an application-creation/uniqueness rule, grep the model `Meta.constraints` first. NB for migrate-first via MCP: a Django `UniqueConstraint` WITH a `condition` is implemented as a **partial unique INDEX**, not a table CONSTRAINT — swap it with `DROP INDEX` + `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX … WHERE …` (check `pg_indexes` for the real name/def first), not `DROP CONSTRAINT`. (Sprint application-reminders, 2026-06-06)
- **To add a scheduled job, mirror the lightweight HTTP cron-endpoint pattern (`CronRunView` + a Cloud Scheduler HTTP job), not a separate Cloud Run Job.** `CronRunView` runs a whitelisted management command inside the already-running api (which holds all DB/email config); a Cloud Scheduler HTTP job POSTs to `…/internal/cron/<job>/` with the `X-Cron-Secret` header (the secret IS the auth — no OIDC). Mirror the live `halatuju-vision-outage` scheduler exactly (region, `X-Cron-Secret` from the service's `CRON_SECRET` env, `Asia/Kuala_Lumpur`). Add the job name to `CronRunView.JOBS`, deploy, then `gcloud scheduler jobs create http …`. Smoke-test by curling the endpoint with `-d '{}'` (an empty POST 411s at the LB without a body). (Sprint application-reminders, 2026-06-06)
- **When a pure/`SimpleTestCase`-tested function starts touching the ORM, its fakes need the new relation stubbed — and the genuinely DB-dependent assertion belongs in a `TestCase`, not the fake.** Adding `_cluster_proof_identity` (which calls `application.documents.filter(...)`) to `student_income_ic_check` broke four `SimpleNamespace`-fake tests that only modelled the old relationship path's fields. Fix was a two-parter: give the relationship-only fakes an empty `documents` (a lambda chain) so the new lookup is a harmless no-op, and cover the real proof-match in the DB-backed `test_verdict_engine` instead of trying to fake a queryset. Before extending a no-DB function to read a relation, grep its existing tests for `SimpleNamespace`/`SimpleTestCase` and decide which assertions move to a real `TestCase`. (Sprint income-card, 2026-06-06)
- **`next build` "Compiled successfully" is the FE type gate; `tsc --noEmit` is stricter and includes test files, so treat its test-file errors as out-of-scope unless they're in a file you touched.** An isolated `tsc --noEmit` lit up pre-existing `ApplicationPayload`→`Record` conversion errors in `scholarship.test.ts` that `next build` excludes and that weren't in any changed file. Don't chase `tsc` noise from test files you didn't edit — confirm it's not yours (git status) and trust the build gate. (Separately: a non-zero `next build` exit on Windows with "✓ Compiled successfully" present is the `spawn UNKNOWN`/SIGABRT SSG flake, not a code failure — won't recur on Cloud Build's Linux.) (Sprint income-card, 2026-06-06)
- **"Standardise X everywhere" — grep `lib/` for an existing helper before writing one.** The request to format every IC as `XXXXXX-XX-XXXX` was already 90% solved by a shared `formatNric()` in `lib/scholarship.ts`; the job was finding the unwrapped display sites, not writing a formatter (two local duplicates already existed too). A 30-second grep avoids a third copy. (Sprint income-card, 2026-06-06)
- **A test fake that sets the "vision ran" stamp on the WRONG field hides a real production gap — match the field the production code path actually writes.** The new `income_rel_doc_unreadable` nudge gated on `vision_run_at`, but a birth certificate is a FIELD-EXTRACTION doc: `run_field_extraction_for_document` stamps `vision_fields_run_at`, while `vision_run_at` is only ever set by the IC/MyKad path (`run_vision_for_document`). The regression test passed because the `_add_doc` helper sets `vision_run_at` unconditionally — so the test went green while the feature was dead in prod (Gopal stayed silent on an unreadable BC), costing an extra deploy. When a check reads a per-doc-type signal (a run-at stamp, a verdict field, an extracted dict), confirm which code path writes it for THAT doc_type and make the fixture stamp the same field — don't trust a generic helper's defaults. (Sprint income-card, 2026-06-06)
- **A doc's extraction SCHEMA existing does NOT mean the upload pipeline runs it — membership in the handler's doc-type sets is the real switch.** The birth-certificate `_FIELD_SCHEMAS`/`_DOC_HINTS` entry + a `TestBirthCertificateWiring` test all existed and passed, yet a BC was never read in production: it was absent from `views.SUPPORTING_NAME_CHECK_TYPES` (OCR) and `vision.GEMINI_EXTRACT_DOC_TYPES` (field extraction), so the upload handler's `if doc.doc_type in …` branches skipped it entirely — child/mother names were always blank and the mother-relationship verdict could never confirm. When a feature relies on extracted fields for a NEW doc_type, assert the doc_type is in the actual pipeline sets, not just that its schema is registered. (Sprint income-card, 2026-06-06)
- **Before adding a heading or a standing notice to a component, grep the parent wrapper for the same element — shared wrappers already render titles/notes.** The Review page showed "Your application" twice (`ScholarshipReview` printed its own `<h1>` while `application/page.tsx`'s `wrap()` already prints it), and the post‑submit screen showed two near‑identical email notices (the "What happens next" box's note + `nav()`'s standalone note). Both shipped and were caught only in live testing. When you add a title/banner/notice to a child, check what the page wrapper and shared nav/footer helpers already emit, and either suppress one (e.g. `nav({email:false})`) or don't duplicate it. (Sprint review-submit-flow, 2026-06-07)
- **Any hand-declared serializer field or raw `setattr` write to a length-limited column MUST carry an explicit `max_length` mirroring the DB column — otherwise an over-long value is a generic atomic-rollback, not a clean 400.** A plain `serializers.Serializer` (unlike `ModelSerializer`) does NOT inherit the model field's `max_length`, and `save_application_details`/`sync_profile_fields` write via `setattr` which bypasses serializer validation entirely. `parents_occupation` (varchar 255), then `city` (100), `name`/`school` (255) all had unguarded web inputs + unguarded serializer fields → Postgres `value too long` → the whole save rolled back as "Could not save". When adding/auditing a free-text field: (1) is the serializer a plain `Serializer` or does the value reach the DB via `setattr`? then declare `max_length` to match the column; (2) put a `maxLength` on the input so it's stopped at the keyboard; (3) free-text Text/`text` columns still want an anti-spam cap (we use 5000). (Sprint input-length-guards, 2026-06-07)
- **A blanket `catch {}` that shows one generic save error hides both the cause and the fix from the user — preserve the API's field-level 400 and name the offending field.** The save handlers discarded DRF's `{field: [messages]}` body, so a "too long" rejection looked identical to a network failure. The client now attaches `err.fieldErrors` and the forms phrase "Your answer to '{question}' is too long". When a form `catch` only ever shows one message, check whether the API returns actionable field errors worth surfacing. (Sprint input-length-guards, 2026-06-07)
- **When a user asks "what is X?" / "how does X differ from Y?" about a UI or domain semantic, read it as "the model isn't settled yet" — propose the WHOLE model (a scale / table / state-matrix) for sign-off before coding, not the literal first tweak.** The verdict colour model took three deploys (Kent scale → swap blue/amber → "blue needs a green") because each step answered one question instead of settling the model. The turn that converged it was presenting the full 4-band Kent table for the user to react to. Offer the structure, get the decision, then implement once. (Sprint verdict-confidence-alignment, 2026-06-07)
- **A presentation/verdict helper should take the whole domain object, not a single field — a single field rarely survives the first compound rule.** `factTileTone(status)` had to become `factTileTone(fact)` the moment "blue needs a verified value" landed (it needs the `evidence` array, not just the status), rippling to every call site + the test helper. Default these mapping helpers to the full object up front. (Sprint verdict-confidence-alignment, 2026-06-07)
- **Lock an enum/taxonomy option list (a product decision) BEFORE generating the migration — and validate it against the real DB.** The family profession field churned through ~5 `rm migration && makemigrations` cycles because coding started before the option list was settled, and the owner kept adding options mid-build. Reading the actual `parents_occupation` entries on prod (33 rows) settled it in one pass (~95% coverage) and exposed gaps the a-priori list missed. For any choices/enum field: agree the list first (validate against production data where it exists), THEN write the model + migration once. (Sprint family-redesign, 2026-06-08)
- **To view a Stitch screenshot, download it to an explicit Windows temp path first — `Read` can't open a URL, and a git-bash `/tmp` path doesn't resolve against the Windows filesystem.** `Read` on the `lh3.googleusercontent.com` URL fails (local-files only), and `curl -o` into git-bash `/tmp` writes somewhere `Read` then can't find. Fix: `curl -sL "<screenshot-url>=w1100" -o "C:/Users/<user>/AppData/Local/Temp/preview.png"`, `Read` that absolute Windows path, then delete it. (B40 Phase E/F Sprint 1, 2026-06-08)
- **When testing that a best-effort email FIRES, populate the recipient field in the fixture — `_send` no-ops silently when `to_email` is empty, so a fixture gap reads as a code bug.** The award-confirmed-email test failed `0 != 1` because `_fundable_app` never set `notify_email`; the best-effort send returned False and `mail.outbox` stayed empty. A best-effort send swallows the no-recipient case, so the assertion misattributes a fixture gap to the code. Set the realistic recipient (`notify_email`) in the fixture. (B40 Phase E/F Sprint 2, 2026-06-08)
- **A timed-out Stitch generation on this project needs ~90–150s before the first `list_screens` poll — polling at ~45–70s twice made a successful generation look like a failure.** Generation here is slower than a typical poll, and `list_screens` is a large/expensive dump, so don't poll early or often. Wait ~90–150s, then poll every ~30s; the owner can also paste the preview `node-id`s from the Stitch UI, which `get_screen` resolves cheaply (metadata + screenshot URL, not inline HTML). (B40 Phase E/F Sprint 3, 2026-06-09)
- **Before scoping a sprint as a "new page / new model", grep for the surface it's supposedly adding — it often already exists, which reshapes the whole plan.** Sprint 5 (F6 reviewer profile) was scoped from the roadmap as a greenfield `/admin` page + a fresh model; a `Glob` of `admin/**/page.tsx` turned up an existing `/admin/profile` page with its own `AdminProfileView`, `getAdminProfile`/`updateAdminProfile`, and an established (approved) card pattern. The real sprint was "extend an existing surface", not "build a new one" — which also surfaced the dependency-direction call (don't widen the lower-layer `courses` view; add a scholarship endpoint + a new card). One cheap `Glob`/`Grep` for the page route + the endpoint name, before reading the roadmap's file table as gospel, prevents planning a build that half-exists. (B40 Phase E/F Sprint 5, 2026-06-09)
- **When a timed-out Stitch gen never surfaces in `list_screens`, don't keep polling — present a concrete mock (ASCII/`AskUserQuestion` preview) for sign-off; the owner often pastes the real preview `node-id` in reply.** Sprint 5's reviewer-card gen timed out and wasn't in the (paged/capped ~30-item) `list_screens` after two polls. Rather than spin, the design was shown as an `AskUserQuestion` ASCII preview — the owner replied with the actual Stitch preview URL (the gen had persisted late), which `get_screen` then resolved for a faithful build. The ASCII mock both unblocks and gives the owner the choice; it's strictly better than a poll loop when the screen extends an already-approved pattern. (Sharpens #72/#116/#151; B40 Phase E/F Sprint 5, 2026-06-09)
- **In HalaTuju TS, prefer `Array.from(set)` over spreading a `Set`/`Map` (`[...set]`) — the project tsconfig rejects the spread (TS2802) but production code passes because it never uses it.** The pagination component failed `tsc --noEmit` only on `[...new Set(...)]`; `Array.from()` is the codebase-idiomatic form and type-checks. When writing new TS here, reach for `Array.from`. (Partner pagination, 2026-06-09)
- **A git worktree shares `.git` but NOT gitignored paths like `node_modules`, so the frontend can't be type-checked/tested in a fresh worktree until you provide deps — link them, don't copy.** Verify a worktree's frontend by junctioning the main repo's deps in (`cmd //c "mklink /J node_modules <main>/halatuju-web/node_modules"`), running `tsc`/`jest`, then removing the link with `cmd //c "rmdir node_modules"` (which unlinks without deleting the real folder — never `rm -rf` a junction). (Partner pagination, 2026-06-09)
- **A Stitch generation can fail with an AUTH error (expired MCP credential), not just a timeout — treat it the same: fall back to the ASCII-mock-via-AskUserQuestion approval, don't retry the dead credential.** F4's `generate_screen_from_text` returned "missing authentication credential" (the interactively-authed Stitch MCP session had lapsed — these servers can be absent/expired between sessions). Retrying is futile until re-auth; the unblocking move is the established path (#153) — present the concrete mock for sign-off, which works especially well when the screen extends an already-approved pattern. So: any Stitch failure (timeout OR auth) → straight to the ASCII mock; only re-auth + regenerate if the owner specifically wants a fresh render. (Sharpens #153; B40 Phase E/F Sprint 11, 2026-06-09)
- **A test that pins a versioned constant to a literal is a guaranteed future failure — assert against the constant, not the string.** F9a bumped `CONSENT_VERSION` `2026-draft-4`→`-5` and broke `test_complete_onboarding...` which hard-coded `'2026-draft-4'`; the test meant "the consent uses the current version", so the fix was `assertEqual(c.version, services.CONSENT_VERSION)` — now true across every bump. Same class as #48/#131 (a change to shared state breaks an earlier sprint's test) and it surfaced ONLY on the full suite. When a test asserts a value that lives as a module constant (version strings, default thresholds, enum members), import and assert the constant unless the literal value IS the thing under test. (B40 Phase E/F Sprint 9, 2026-06-09)
- **Inviting a user via Supabase fails with `422 email_exists` whenever the email already has an account — handle it as success, not a 502.** The admin invite shipped only handling brand-new emails; the most common real case (the invitee already signed in as a student or via Google) collapsed into a misleading "Failed to send invite email". The fix: on `422 email_exists`, skip the invite email and create the local admin row anyway — it links to their existing account by email on next sign-in (`get_admin`'s email-backfill path). When touching ANY invite/auth flow, test the already-registered branch explicitly. (Admin Roles realignment, 2026-06-09)
- **After narrowing a shared TypeScript union, run the FULL `next build` — `tsc --noEmit` on the edited files won't catch the cross-file ripples.** Narrowing the admin role union (dropping `'viewer'`) left a `canWrite !== 'viewer'` comparison in an UN-edited cockpit file; `tsc` on the changed files looked clean but `next build` failed (`TS2367` no-overlap). A type change ripples to every consumer, not just the file you edited — the project-wide `next build` is the real gate (sharpens the build-vs-tsc point). (Admin Roles realignment, 2026-06-09)
- **A live policy/threshold change to a scoring engine needs a re-score-of-PENDING tool, scoped to un-released decisions only — never re-judge a decision that's already been communicated.** Changing the B40 income rule flipped one already-scored (but not-yet-released) applicant from rejected→shortlisted; `rescore_pending_decisions` re-runs the *real* engine (no logic duplication) over `status='submitted' AND decision_released_at IS NULL` rows, so released/emailed verdicts are untouchable by construction. Build this as a reusable cron job, not a one-off SQL update, so any future threshold change is a single trigger. (B40 income policy, 2026-06-09)
- **Run sprint-close BEFORE tearing down the feature worktree — the close has to commit docs (retrospective, CLAUDE.md, decisions) and push, which needs a working tree on the branch.** A worktree was removed on request, then had to be re-created to run the close. Sequence: finish → sprint-close (commit + push) → only then `git worktree remove`. (Admin Roles realignment, 2026-06-09)
- **The "local FE → prod API" verify trick works for FE-ONLY changes; a feature that also changes the backend needs a self-contained local stack — and a local backend can't reach Supabase's direct DB host (it's IPv6-only) nor mint uploads without the Storage service-role key.** Verifying the Action Centre wasted several rounds: pointing the local front-end at the live API showed *none* of Phase 1 (the new server code wasn't deployed); then a local backend against the live DB failed (`could not translate host name db.<ref>.supabase.co` — IPv6-only direct host); then uploads failed for want of `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`. The path that worked: local backend on a fresh **SQLite** + a seeded dummy + GEMINI **and** the Storage key, front-end pointed at `localhost:8000`. So: for any FE+BE change, plan the self-contained local stack up front (SQLite is lighter — empty course tables skip the 1 GB DataFrame load); use the Supabase **pooler** if you must hit the live DB. (Action Centre, 2026-06-10)
- **On a long Windows session, kill orphan `node.exe` before `next build` — a `Static worker exited … spawn UNKNOWN errno -4094` is process-spawner exhaustion, not a code fault.** `next build` printed "✓ Compiled successfully" (so the type-check + compile — the code-correctness signal — passed) then crashed in static generation; 22 stale `node.exe` workers had accumulated. `taskkill //F //IM node.exe //T` then rebuild → clean exit 0. Treat "Compiled successfully" as the gate for code correctness; a later static-worker crash on Windows after a long session is environmental — free the workers and retry, don't chase it as a bug. (Action Centre, 2026-06-10)
- **When you relax a gate that emits a shared blocker/error code, enumerate EVERY code path that can emit that same code before declaring the fix done — a sibling branch can re-impose the block you thought you removed.** Relaxing `document_red_blockers` for an optional income doc (#4) only touched the `salary_slip`/`epf` branch; the `parent_ic` branch *also* rolls up a proof-coherence check that emits the SAME `income_document_mismatch`. It happened to be safe (it cross-checks the STR, not the optional slip, on the STR route — confirmed by reading `_cluster_proof_identity`), but the near-miss is the lesson: `grep` the blocker code, read each emitter, and prove the gate is actually open end-to-end (a unit test asserting the optional doc does NOT block catches it). (Verification-accuracy pass, 2026-06-11)
- **When a deterministic verdict consumes an AI-extracted field, gate the field's PROVENANCE/TYPE, not just its value.** A SARA-only letter passed as a current STR because `_str_currency` (deterministic, correct) trusted a Gemini-inferred `status` word — the AI read non-STR wording ("terpilih menerima SARA") as "approved". The deterministic rule was sound; its INPUT was an AI guess. Fix: make the AI's `source_type` classification a gate (an `unknown`/wrong-type source → unconfirmed regardless of the status text). General principle: any AI-extracted field feeding a deterministic decision needs its *kind* validated (is this even the right document?), because a hallucinated/misclassified value will otherwise be trusted by the rule. The structural answer is deterministic label-anchored capture for fixed-format issuers (see the document-extraction audit). (SARA≠STR, 2026-06-11)
- **A label-anchored parser validated only on clean digital PDFs is NOT validated — test it against the MASHED pypdf output AND the image Vision OCR of the same doc type.** L86 says "validate on a real document"; this sharpens it: production sends THREE text shapes for the same doc, and they break different assumptions. (1) A clean digital PDF: label then value on the next line — naive `find_value` works. (2) A scanned/photographed doc via Vision OCR: label and value land in SEPARATE COLUMNS (mobile MySTR: all labels, then all values) — `find_value("label")→next line` grabs the next LABEL, not the value (read name/NRIC layout-independently instead). (3) pypdf on some PDFs concatenates a whole page onto ONE line — `\b\d{12}\b` finds no boundary after an IC mashed into the next word (`…2306NO`), and `find_value` swallows everything after a label to end-of-line. The deterministic-capture sprint hit all three; fixes were digit-boundary regexes `(?<!\d)(\d{12})(?!\d)`, layout-independent name/NRIC extraction, and a `_clean_short` guard that rejects a value that ran past the next label. Before trusting a new parser, dump and test it on a real sample of EACH shape, not just the cleanest one. (Deterministic capture Sprint 1, 2026-06-11)
- **In a verification gate, "we haven't read it yet" must be a distinct state from "we read it and it's clean" — never let an unscanned input count as a pass.** `doc_match_verdict` accepted a not-yet-read upload as `'ok'` (its D1 policy "pending → accept, don't trap the student behind a slow scanner" conflated a genuine OCR outage with a merely-deferred/rate-limited read), so an unscanned re-upload auto-closed its task before the scan finished. Fix: a separate `'pending'` verdict that HOLDS the task, plus force-reading the one interactive upload so 'pending' only survives a true read failure (where the reviewer is the backstop). General rule: any accept/close decision that depends on an async read must check that the read actually ran and succeeded — a missing/deferred read is "unknown", not "fine". (Upload-race fix, 2026-06-12)
- **A route/branch-specific requirement needs route-specific copy — a verdict code that only one branch can raise must not be described with branch-generic wording.** `income_proof_missing` fires only on the STR income route, but its i18n said the generic "salary slip, EPF, or STR", inviting an upload that the str-typed Upload button then files as an STR and fails. The code was route-aware; the string wasn't, because the copy was written once without reconciling against which branch emits it. When wording a blocker/request, check every emitter of that code and make the copy as specific as the narrowest path. (Upload-race fix, 2026-06-12)
- **Before writing a field to an existing "edit" endpoint, check what that endpoint does AFTER the write — a generic save path can carry side effects (status reverts, re-validation, notifications) that your new use case must not trigger.** Adding a post-submit income route switch by reusing the broad details PATCH looked free (it already accepted `income_route` and allowed post-submit statuses), but the PATCH calls `revert_if_profile_incomplete` — so the moment the salary route's new requirements were unmet it would flip `profile_complete` → `shortlisted`, silently un-submitting the student. A dedicated endpoint that writes only the income fields + recomputes tasks (never reverts) was the right call. General rule: a shared mutator's post-write hooks are part of its contract; read to the end of the handler before reusing it. (Income route switch, 2026-06-12)
- **When prompting Stitch for a BRANCHING form, describe each branch's follow-up explicitly and don't name documents in the route-choice labels — or it renders a blended card.** The route-switch mockup labelled the salary option "salary slips or EPF" and showed the salary "who works" chips under the STR option (instead of STR's three earner options). The owner caught all three on review (the point of the Stitch-first gate). Fix: frame the choice as the decision (STR-or-not), make each follow-up conditional on its branch, keep document names out of the choice labels. (Income route switch, 2026-06-12)
- **For an assurance/verification feature, fix the THREAT MODEL and the STANDARD OF PROOF before designing — they determine everything, and "robust" must not be confused with "unfoolable".** The IC-genuineness work first drifted toward a black-box "looks_official" boolean / default-on / full audit trail — which over-claims. The honest, defensible design fell out only once the owner pinned the frame: the threat is casual/wrong-document fakes (not determined forgers, which OCR/AI can't stop), the standard is "highly probable from a few independent checks shown with evidence", and the reviewer is the scorekeeper. A system that openly says "consistent, not yet verified" is MORE trustworthy to a sponsor than one that flashes a green "verified" and is later fooled. State these three up front in any verification design. (IC genuineness, 2026-06-12)
- **Validate a detector's discriminating signal on REAL positives AND a real negative before building — the obvious marker is often the foolable one.** The IC fingerprint spike showed a typed fake still carried the header text "KAD PENGENALAN" (it was typed in); what actually separated fake from genuine were the PHYSICAL markers (face, chip, card-look) the fake couldn't carry. Had we keyed the check on text markers alone it would have passed the fake. Run the candidate signals over real genuine docs + a real fake and keep the markers that actually separate them. (IC genuineness, 2026-06-12)
- **To validate a Gemini-only feature on prod data, call the Gemini seam in isolation — do NOT re-run the whole Vision pipeline locally, because Cloud Vision OCR needs ADC/an API key the local pooler shell doesn't have.** Validating IC genuineness by calling `run_vision_for_document` locally also re-ran Cloud Vision `document_text_detection`, which failed (no `GOOGLE_CLOUD_VISION_API_KEY` locally — Vision authenticates via the runtime SA on Cloud Run; only `GEMINI_API_KEY` is in the service env), blanking the IC's name/NRIC read and setting `ic_service_down`. The Gemini genuineness call itself was fine. For a local prod-data check, exercise only the function under test (the Gemini seam) and read existing stored fields — don't trigger sibling steps that depend on credentials the shell lacks. (IC genuineness live validation, 2026-06-12)
- **"Fold the new signal into the existing AI call for free" only holds when that call already sees the MODALITY the new signal needs — check the modality, not just that a call exists.** Sprint 2's plan assumed genuineness would add a field to the supporting docs' existing extraction prompt at ~zero cost. But that extraction reads OCR TEXT (or is deterministic); genuineness ("is this a real document IMAGE?") needs the image, so it's actually one extra multimodal call per doc (like the IC). Before promising "zero extra cost", confirm the existing call operates on the same modality (text vs image vs structured) the new feature requires. (Doc genuineness Sprint 2, 2026-06-12)
- **When you add a code to a verdict fact's `unresolved`, add its `admin.scholarship.verdict.item.*` i18n in the SAME change — the verdict-item namespace is separate from the anomaly-flag namespace.** Sprint 1 added `ic_low_confidence` to the Identity verdict but only added `scholarship.admin.anomaly.ic_low_confidence` copy; the cockpit verdict detail resolves `admin.scholarship.verdict.item.{code}`, so the caveat would have rendered as a raw key. The officer FLAG and the verdict ITEM are two different i18n namespaces for the same concept — wire both. (Doc genuineness Sprint 2, 2026-06-12)
- **A surface whose value depends on accumulated runtime data must ship a copy'd empty-state that explains the precondition — never a bare zero, which reads as "broken".** The AI-reliability card ships before any reviewer has recorded a verdict on prod, so it has nothing to score; a blank/zeroed card would look like a failed feature to a stakeholder. Fix: an explicit empty-state (`reliability.empty`) saying it populates once reviewers record decisions. Any metric/dashboard/leaderboard that only fills in after runtime accumulation needs this — the day-one state is the most-seen state, so design it deliberately. (Verdict scorekeeper, 2026-06-12)
- **When a tech-debt row bundles two independent fixes, resolving one means explicitly re-scoping the remainder — don't flip the whole row closed.** TD-083 was two debts: surface the override-rate metric (done in Sprint 3) AND add an explicit `officer_verdict.overall` accept/decline toggle (deliberately not built — the card derives reliability from the four per-fact decisions). Marked the row as a partial resolution with the unbuilt half re-stated, so it isn't silently lost. Before closing a multi-part TD row, separate what shipped from what didn't and leave the remainder actionable. (Verdict scorekeeper, 2026-06-12)
- **A cron-sent email that "falls back to `DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL`" for its RECIPIENT will email a no-reply mailbox — verify the prod value, don't assume it's the admin.** The refresh reminder defaulted its recipient to `DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL`, which I described as "→ your tamiliam@gmail.com"; prod `DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL` is actually `noreply@halatuju.xyz` (a *sender* address). The L137 smoke-test (curl the cron endpoint) caught it because the 200 response echoed the resolved recipient. Set an explicit recipient env var for admin-facing cron emails, and always smoke-test the endpoint end-to-end (it reveals the real recipient) rather than trusting a fallback. (Course-data Sprint 1, 2026-06-13)
- **A "just add a unique constraint" step is rarely just that — check what currently relies on the constraint's ABSENCE before scoping it into a sprint.** The slot-model DB `UniqueConstraint(application, doc_type, household_member)` looked like a one-liner but breaks existing tests that deliberately pre-create two same-slot docs to verify the single-instance sweep, and changes a long-standing invariant (multiple same-slot rows were possible). It got deferred. Before committing to a constraint, grep for fixtures/flows that create duplicates of the constrained tuple, and budget the rework. (Slot model Sprint 1, 2026-06-13)
- **When another agent is active in the repo, a local commit on shared `main` is NOT private — use worktree isolation.** A "implement, don't push" search fix, committed locally on `main`, got swept into the other (course-data) agent's sprint-close push and deployed ahead of plan. No harm here, but it broke an explicit hold. `parallel-work-isolation.md` exists for exactly this; branching normally off shared `main` doesn't isolate local commits from someone else's push. (Slot model Sprint 1, 2026-06-13)
- **For a paginated catalogue scrape, anchor each card on its STABLE per-item heading and walk UP to the enclosing row — don't require one container to hold every field.** The UP_TVET scraper's first heuristic demanded a single `div` carrying h6 + all labels + the detail anchor; the portal splits a card's fields across sibling Bootstrap columns, so it matched ~1 card/page instead of 20. Re-anchoring on the one numbered `<h6>` per card (then walking up until the ancestor has the Sektor label + the detail link) fixed it. Caught instantly by validating on the real page (`--max-pages 2`), not synthetic fixtures (L86). General rule: scrape from the most stable repeating element (the heading/title) outward, and validate the per-page COUNT on a real spike before trusting the parser. (UP_TVET Sprint 1, 2026-06-13)
- **Before INGESTING an external catalogue into a golden-master-adjacent table, run a no-write inventory first — and expect cross-source identity (codes AND names) to be fuzzy.** UP_TVET is ~1000 programmes whose codes don't match our synthetic IDs AND whose institution names are full ("INSTITUT KEMAHIRAN BELIA NEGARA …") where ours are abbreviations ("IKBN …"), so neither a code diff nor a name diff is clean. A `scrape → audit (Awam/Swasta split, by-institution, new-vs-held)` pass with NO DB writes produced the real scope numbers safely, before any `CourseRequirement` row (which feeds the eligibility DataFrame) was created. Make the inventory's new-vs-existing an explicit upper bound and surface the list for human judgement rather than asserting a clean diff. (UP_TVET Sprint 1, 2026-06-13; same family as #19 + the SPM ID-mismatch lesson)
- **A spike that validates one layer does NOT validate the sprint — before scoping a "mirror/extend X" sync, verify the TARGET MODEL's shape AND the ID provenance of the data being diffed, not just the source format.** The course-data Sprint 3 spike confirmed e-Panduan's `jenprog=spm` cards have the *same scrape structure* as STPM, and the roadmap concluded "mirror `sync_stpm_mohe`". But the sync TARGET (`Course`) had a different shape (merit in `CourseRequirement`, URL in `CourseInstitution`, no `is_active`) AND a mixed ID scheme — only 89/390 SPM courses carry MOHE KOD PROGRAM codes; the other ~300 use internal `POLY-*`/`KKOM-*`/`50PD…` schemes the portal never emits. A naive whole-catalogue key diff would have flagged ~300 as "removed" and tripped the mass-deactivation guard. Caught by reading the model + one read-only prod query (id-scheme breakdown) BEFORE coding. General rule: a key-based diff sync silently breaks when the two sides use different ID provenance — check the target model's fields and the existing data's ID scheme as an explicit scoping step, separate from validating the scrape. (Course-data Sprint 3, 2026-06-13; sharpens #19/#88/#152)
- **A telemetry/status call added to an existing command must be guarded as a UNIT — guarding only the writer isn't enough when its ARGUMENTS touch the DB.** Instrumenting `refresh_stpm` to record dashboard freshness, I made `record_status` itself best-effort (try/except), but passed `StpmCourse.objects.count()` as an argument — evaluated BEFORE `record_status` runs. `refresh_stpm`'s orchestration tests run it with `call_command` mocked and NO database, so the count raised and broke 4 green tests. Fix: wrap the whole instrumentation block (arg computation included) in try/except. Telemetry must never break the tool it observes, and "never break" includes the no-DB tests that exercise the tool's control flow. (Course Data Dashboard Sprint 1, 2026-06-13)
- **A read-only status/metrics dashboard should compute what it can LIVE and persist only what it must (freshness/history).** The Course Data dashboard computes coverage counts live from the DB (no drift, no migration cost) and persists only `last_run_at` per source via a tiny `CourseDataStatus` table the tools upsert. Counts are always current; freshness is recorded. Don't store what you can derive; do store what only a past run knew. And design the day-one empty state ("never run") deliberately — it's the most-seen state before any harvest (sharpens #176). (Course Data Dashboard Sprint 1, 2026-06-13)
- **Before reaching for a Cloud Run Job to dodge the request timeout, check if the slow work PARALLELISES — a read-only fan-out (HTTP GETs, independent reads) often fits in one request once concurrent.** The course-data health check is ~650 link GETs; sequential it risks the ~300s Cloud Run request timeout (so does the cron endpoint, which is itself just a request), which argued for a Cloud Run Job. But the checks are independent read-only GETs → a `ThreadPoolExecutor(--workers 20)` brings it to <1 min, so BOTH the weekly cron (`CronRunView`) and a synchronous admin button run comfortably in-request with zero extra infra. Size the work (count the items) and try concurrency before adding a Job. (Course Data health, 2026-06-13)
- **A "monitor X, don't change X" feature must be read-only BY CONSTRUCTION, not by intent — exclude the mutating flags at the call site + prove it with a test.** The health-check orchestrator runs `validate_course_urls` *without* `--fix` and `audit_data` (DB-read only) — no `--apply`, no scrape — so no cron/button path can mutate the catalogue even by mistake; a test asserts the seeded dead link SURVIVES a run. When the owner says "reporting only, no overwrite", encode it as the absence of write flags in the wrapper + a test that proves nothing changed, not just a comment. (Course Data health, 2026-06-13)
- **A per-item checker run for the FIRST time over the full real dataset will hit malformed inputs the mocked tests never saw — make the per-item function catch EVERYTHING (build the request inside the try), or one bad row aborts the whole run.** `validate_course_urls` shipped + deployed green (tests mock `check_url`), but its first real run over all 652 prod URLs crashed on a stored hyperlink with no scheme (`kkpasirsalak.mypolycc.edu.my`) — `urllib`'s "unknown url type" `ValueError`, raised because the `Request(url)` build sat OUTSIDE check_url's try/except, so it escaped and killed the run after the audit step but before link_health recorded. The concurrent `pool.map` re-raises on iteration, but the sequential path would've crashed too — it had just never been run against this URL. Fix: wrap the whole function body (request build included) so any malformed URL → `('error', …)`, plus a real-input regression test (`check_url('foo.edu.my')` → 'error', no mock). General rule: a reachability/parse/scan function applied across real data must never raise on a single bad item; test it with a genuinely malformed input, not only mocked-protocol cases. (Course Data health hotfix, 2026-06-13; same family as L86 real-data validation)
- **A strict stdlib HTTP check (`urllib` default SSL) reports many ALIVE gov/edu sites as failures — don't equate "checker errored" with "link dead".** The first full link check flagged 174/652 as "errors"; the owner clicked one and it was alive. Diagnosis (probing a sample default-vs-unverified-SSL): the dominant cause was **TLS cert-chain rejection** (`URLError`/`SSLError`) on Malaysian `*.moe.edu.my`/`*.edu.my`/gov sites whose certs `urllib`'s CA bundle rejects though browsers accept them — plus only 1 schemeless URL and 1 genuinely-dead. Fix: normalise schemeless→`https://`, send a browser UA, and **retry without verification on a TLS failure**, classifying a responding host as `insecure` (reachable, subset of alive) not `error`. Lesson: when a link/HTTP monitor's "broken" count is implausibly high, hand-validate a sample before trusting it; a bare-stdlib client is far stricter than a browser, so "couldn't verify" ≠ "down" — make reachable-but-cert-invalid a first-class state. (Course Data link-check accuracy, 2026-06-13)
- **A health/monitor surface must persist the OFFENDING ITEMS, not just the counts — or the user sees "142 errors" and can't act.** The link check first stored only `{alive, dead, errors}` counts; the per-URL list went to a buffer discarded each run, so the owner literally couldn't see which links were broken. Fix: store a bounded `failures` list (`{url, kind, institution(s), refs}`) in the status, grouped by reason on the dashboard with a CSV export. General rule: when you build a "N problems found" metric, also persist *which* N (with enough context — here the institution behind each URL — to triage), or the number is a dead-end. (Course Data problem-links, 2026-06-14)
- **A reachability check run from a SERVER must report two severities — "broken" (actionable) vs "couldn't verify" (slow/blocked from here) — because the checker's network is not the user's.** The Cloud Run health check flagged dozens of live MY gov/edu portals (IPG, matriculation, polytechnics — 10-15s to first byte) as "connection failed", because a 10s timeout from Singapore ≠ a 10s timeout from a Malaysian browser. The owner clicked them and they loaded fine. Lumping timeout/conn with gone/DNS made the dashboard a wall of false positives the owner couldn't trust. Fix: split failures into BROKEN (gone/DNS-not-found/malformed — won't change on retry, really needs fixing) vs COULDN'T-VERIFY (timeout/conn — almost certainly alive), retry transient failures once, and raise the timeout to match observed first-byte latency (10s→20s). The headline counts BROKEN only. General rule: when a monitor probes external hosts from one network and reports to users on another, a slow/blocked response is "unknown", never "down" — make that a first-class, visually-demoted state. (Course Data link-check recalibration, 2026-06-14; sharpens the cert-invalid `insecure` lesson)
- **A bare apex subdomain can have NO DNS while its `www.` host resolves — a "broken link" may be one missing label, not a dead site.** All 15 HalaTuju matriculation URLs were stored as `https://X.matrik.edu.my` (no `www`), which returns `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED`; the live sites are only served at `http://www.X.matrik.edu.my`. So they were genuinely unreachable as stored (a real fix), but the site itself was fine — the fault was one DNS label, not link rot. Verified each in the LOCAL Playwright browser (which reaches MY sites the Cloud Run checker can't), then re-pointed via audited MCP `UPDATE` with the old values recorded for reversibility. General rule: before declaring a domain dead, try the `www.`/non-`www.` variant — and verify from a network that can actually reach the host (local browser), not the sandbox/server that flagged it. (Matrik URL fix, 2026-06-14)
- **A 401/403 from a link checker is AMBIGUOUS — never silently count it as "alive", give it its own surfaced bucket.** `check_url` treated 401/403 as alive ("reachable, just a login wall") — sound for portals that gate bots, but it also hides the case the owner hit: Politeknik Port Dickson's stored bare URL returned a 403 because the real site moved to `/web/` — a genuinely broken link the checker would have passed. HTTP alone can't tell a login wall from a wrong/old path, so the honest design is a THIRD severity, `gated` (Access-blocked, 401/403), shown for a human to eyeball — distinct from BROKEN (gone/dns) and COULDN'T-VERIFY (timeout/conn).
