General rule: when a signal is genuinely undecidable from the data you have, don't fold it into the optimistic OR the pessimistic bucket — make "ambiguous" a visible state. (Course Data 403 handling, 2026-06-14)
- When an admin/officer view must show "what the student saw" (a question, a prompt, a label), reuse the SAME source function the student UI uses (here `titleSourceFor`), don't re-derive from a parallel description key. The cockpit Outstanding box originally showed `verdict.item.<code>` (an internal caveat *description*) while the student had been asked `actionCentre.item.<code>.title` (the *question*) — two strings for one thing, guaranteed to drift. Reusing the student's title source makes the two render identically by construction. (Check-2/Check-3 S4 review)
- Before promising a "test it locally" on a stack whose secrets live only in the cloud, check for the backend `.env` (here `halatuju_api/.env`) FIRST. If it's absent (correct, per the secrets policy), an authenticated local smoke is impossible — say so up front and route to a live test-account Playwright smoke post-deploy (lesson re: test-green ≠ ship-confidence), instead of discovering it at the testing step. (Check-2/Check-3 S4)
- For a "ship it, review on the live app, then refine" loop, set the expectation up front that each review round is a separate web deploy — it will blow past the ≤2-deploys/feature guideline legitimately. Flag the budget before the 3rd deploy and offer to batch the next set of tweaks. (Check-2/Check-3 S4: 1 feature deploy + 2 review-round deploys)
- When another agent shares the repo, VERIFY the current branch (`git branch`/`git status`) before any merge/push — and do your own work in a dedicated worktree. The primary checkout had been switched to the other agent's branch (`td-sprint0-cleanup`) with their uncommitted WIP; a `git merge --ff-only && git push origin main` run there silently advanced *their* branch + applied my files to *their* worktree, and the push reported "Everything up-to-date" (my commit never reached origin/main). Recovery was a refspec push (`git push origin <mybranch>:main`) that touched no local refs/worktrees. General rule: never assume the shared checkout is still on the branch you left it on. (Cockpit live-review, 2026-06-14)
- An i18n "orphaned key" scan that only greps for the full dotted path (`t('a.b.key')`) MASSIVELY over-reports, because many keys are built dynamically — `t('admin.scholarship.statuses.' + status)`, `anomaly.` + code, `verdict.item.` + code, `docsDrawer.*`. Doing TD-118, a naive scan flagged ~110 keys as dead when only ~29 truly were; bulk-deleting would have broken every dynamic group. Before deleting any i18n key, check whether its PARENT path is concatenated/templated anywhere; treat dynamically-addressed subtrees as live. And keep a scoped cleanup scoped — when the scan reveals a bigger latent orphan set (here the retired Verify-&-accept card's keys), log it as its own debt item (TD-120) rather than scope-creep the small change. (TD-118, 2026-06-16)
- When a new ROLE is added to an auth system, audit every place that assumes the old default — "post-login landing", "default home", "who can see X" — because those were written before the role existed. The B40 `reviewer`/`viewer` roles were routed to `/admin` like every admin, but `/admin` is specifically the partner-org dashboard (`getPartnerDashboard`) which 403s a reviewer ("not a partner organisation admin"). The bug wasn't in the new role; it was in the unrevisited assumption that "admin == partner-org admin". Fix: landing now derives from `role.role`, not a hardcoded default. General rule: adding a role is not done until you've grepped for the places that branch on "is this user an admin?" and confirmed each still holds. (Reviewer access, 2026-06-16)
- Domain context can INVERT a plausible fix — confirm the real-world meaning of a "wrong-looking" signal before coding a flag for it. I was about to add a "salary-slip gross == net → suspicious" flag (#54 read RM357 gross==net), but the owner's note "both parents retired" revealed these are PENSION slips, where gross==net is normal (no EPF/statutory deductions) — the flag would have false-flagged every pension and simple payslip. General rule: when a data pattern looks anomalous, ask what legitimate real-world case produces it before treating it as an error. (Cockpit live-review #54, 2026-06-14)
- A human (incl. Claude) eyeballing documents is NOT a system — it's the exact manual, non-reproducible work an eval harness exists to replace. Asked to find fakes, I visually audited 47 slips and wrongly condemned a genuine cropped one (a24) as a fake. The owner caught it. The fix was to build the deterministic signature scorer so the SYSTEM labels (a24 → "review", correctly); my eyeball verdict and the scorer flatly disagreed, proving the manual pass untrustworthy. General rule: when the task is "classify a corpus reliably and repeatably," the deliverable is a scoring tool + calibration against labelled data, never a one-off human read — and validate any hypothesis against the data before coding the rule. (Genuineness signatures, 2026-06-16)
- A pure relocation can hide a runtime break that the test suite stays GREEN through, when the broken line is flag-gated OFF in tests. Moving genuineness out of `vision.py` left a `_GENUINENESS_DOCS` reference on a line that only runs when `DOC_GENUINENESS_CHECK_ENABLED` is ON (off in CI) — 1267 tests passed while prod would `NameError`. A `grep` for the moved names after the move caught it; an import-surface characterization test now pins it. General rule: after relocating code, grep the old names across the tree AND add a test that asserts the public surface still resolves — behaviour tests don't exercise flag-gated branches. (Genuineness signatures, 2026-06-16)
- "It's a machine token, so detect it deterministically" can still fail on real-world inputs — a QR code is machine-readable in principle, but `cv2.QRCodeDetector` couldn't decode it on 85% of genuine phone photos/scans. The robust move was to ask a multimodal model whether a QR is PRESENT (which it sees even when blurry), not to decode it. General rule: before committing to a deterministic detector, test its recall on the actual (messy) corpus; "deterministic" is only better than "AI" if it actually works on the inputs you'll get. (Genuineness signatures, 2026-06-16)
- After merging `main` into a long-lived branch, run `manage.py migrate` BEFORE trusting any test/tool that builds ORM rows — new migrations the merge pulled in aren't applied to the local/throwaway DB, and the failure is SILENT and misleading: the eval harness scored 0/335 ("all errored"), which read like a code regression but was just a stale local schema. General rule: a merge can change the schema; re-migrate the local DB before running anything that touches the ORM, and read "everything suddenly fails identically" as a possible migration-state problem, not a logic bug. (Genuineness signatures, 2026-06-16)
- A write surface with no save confirmation breeds distrust and workarounds — and a committed surface that keeps its write controls breeds duplicates. #15: a reviewer wasn't sure his interview findings saved (no "Saved ✓"), so he kept a separate copy and reported them "lost"; meanwhile pressing Save *after* Submit spawned a second draft session (the save path created a new draft when none existed). Two general rules: (1) every save the user initiates must give explicit confirmation; (2) once a record is submitted/committed, REMOVE its write controls (freeze to read-only) rather than leaving them live — a live "Save" on an already-committed thing will eventually create a duplicate or silently overwrite. (Scheduling+cockpit, 2026-06-18)
- When a user reports "lost data", confirm the USER's actual sequence of actions before theorising from the code. I diagnosed #15 as two-overlapping-text-boxes confusion from reading the components; the owner clarified the reviewer simply wasn't sure it saved. The DB state (the text was safe in another column) plus the user's account beat a code-only theory — and would have sent me building the wrong fix (relabel boxes) instead of the right one (save confirmation + freeze). (Scheduling+cockpit, 2026-06-18)
- **When data feeds a cache built ONCE at app boot, a data change isn't live until you restart AND re-verify against the new revision id — not just "after the insert".** MBPK eligibility falsely failed its first live check: the running revision had booted before the 10 MBPK rows were inserted, and eligibility reads a pandas `requirements_df` cached at app boot (`apps.py:61`), so its cache had no MBPK rows. The fix wasn't a code change — it was restarting the api (`--update-env-vars=PISMP_DATA_RELOAD=<v>`, no rebuild) and re-checking against the NEW revision. General rule: for any data that hydrates a boot-time cache, "I inserted the row" ≠ "it's live"; force a fresh revision and confirm you're testing it, not the stale one. (PISMP catalogue, 2026-06-18)
- **Before retiring a "spurious-looking" data/catalogue row, read its full content AND check it against the source of truth — surface the trade-off, don't delete on assumption.** Twice this sprint a surprising row looked like junk and wasn't: `…041S004` looked like a duplicate Sains to delete but `…00P` was the wrong one to keep (owner: "P is correct and 4 is wrong"), and the B/D/L Pendidikan Khas rows looked low-value but carried bespoke Braille/BIM/autism descriptions worth preserving. General rule: a surprising row is a signal to investigate against the authoritative source (here the PDF guide), not an error to sweep — and back up before any retire so the call is reversible. (PISMP catalogue, 2026-06-18)
- **Verify the actual code path before asserting a code change is required.** I claimed Tier-2 PISMP requirements needed an engine change to accept student-key subjects; in fact `map_subject_code`'s lowercase fallback already passes unknown subject codes straight through to the student grade key, so the requirements went in as pure data. General rule: trace the function that would "need" the change and confirm it doesn't already handle the case before scoping code work — especially in an engine with a golden master you'd rather not touch. (PISMP catalogue, 2026-06-18)
- When a screenshot shows a labelled status (a header/badge/info-box), read the WHOLE component's state before assuming the defect is in the content. #4: I twice diagnosed the prose (income line, then narrative specifics) as the bug when the real bug was the panel header saying "draft" while the footer already read "Final profile (v2 — with interview)". The contradicting footer was right there; I'd pattern-matched to the income work we'd just done. General rule: header + body + footer must agree — check the label against the data state, not just the data. (Profile income honesty, 2026-06-18)
- **Before designing a new UI step that parallels an existing one, find and reuse the component the app already uses for that job.** Sprint 2's first-cut PISMP subject picker was a bespoke vertical list built straight from the Stitch mock; the owner's live-review asked for "the UA method" — which was the existing `ProgrammePicker` combobox used by every other programme pathway. Reusing it deleted the custom component and matched the app's convention. General rule: when a step mirrors an existing one (course pick, institution pick, date pick…), grep `src/components` for the established picker and prototype *to it*, rather than drawing a new control from the mockup. (PISMP picker, 2026-06-19)
- **When a feature consumes an existing transform, check what that transform does to the NEW data shape — and test with an input that actually exercises it.** The PISMP picker read eligible courses through the legacy `deduplicate_pismp`, which appends an "(Aliran Bahasa Tamil)" descriptor — written when names had no aliran suffix, now double-labelling on top of the post-Sprint-1 "(SJKT)". It only shows for vernacular-stream students, so my all-A+ synthetic probe never surfaced it; it took a real Bahasa-Tamil-credit profile (the owner's live screenshot) to see it. General rule: a new consumer of a shared transform inherits its quirks — read the transform against the current data, and use a test input that triggers its branches (here: a student WITH a Tamil credit), not just the convenient happy-path case. (PISMP picker, 2026-06-19)
- **When a user reports "X is missing", verify against the authoritative source before treating it as a bug.** The owner asked why 4 SJKT specialisms didn't appear; the eligibility engine was correct — the official 2026 IPGM PDF says Matematik needs A− in BOTH Mathematics and Additional Mathematics, and Pendidikan Jasmani's science pool is Sains Sukan/Biologi/Sains/Sains Tambahan (not Physics/Chem). The student's grades genuinely missed those. Checking the PDF (pp. 7, 11) prevented a "fix" that would have broken correct requirements. General rule: for a domain rule (entry requirements, grade thresholds, eligibility), confirm against the source document before changing data — "doesn't appear" often means "correctly filtered". (PISMP picker, 2026-06-19; same family as the domain-context-inverts-a-fix lesson)
- Never run `npm run build` and `npm run dev` against the same `.next` folder — the production build's artifacts corrupt the dev server (ChunkLoadError, broken hydration → a page that renders but won't respond to clicks). When switching from a build to a dev preview, clear/relocate `.next` first. (Interview scheduling, 2026-06-19)
- A code fix to a state machine does NOT repair records already stuck in the bad state. After shipping such a fix, repair the affected rows (here #16 was left in a sticky 'cancelled' state from an earlier test, so the owner kept seeing the bug despite a live deploy). Symptom to recognise: "I still see it after you deployed" on a specific record = stale data, not stale code. (Interview scheduling, 2026-06-19)
- **Report build/deploy times in the owner's timezone (MYT), not the tool's UTC.** The gcloud build watcher emits UTC; quoting it raw ("08:03") read to a UTC+8 owner as an 8-hour misalignment ("your time and mine appear unaligned"). Convert to MYT (UTC+8) in any status line that shows a timestamp. (Reviewer comms + verdict SLA, 2026-06-19)
- **Remove a Windows git-worktree's `node_modules` *junction* with PowerShell `[System.IO.Directory]::Delete(path)` — not `cmd //c rmdir` from Git Bash (the nested quoting mangles → "syntax is incorrect"), and NEVER `Remove-Item -Recurse` on it (that follows the link and deletes the *target*, i.e. the primary checkout's real `node_modules`). Sequence: `Directory.Delete` the junction first (removes only the link), then `Remove-Item -Recurse -Force` the worktree dir. `git worktree remove --force` fails with "Invalid argument" while the junction is still present, so de-junction before removing. (Sponsor redesign R1, 2026-06-19)
- **An OCR-read identifier is NOT safe to compare with exact string equality — anchor identity on the reliably-read field and treat the noisy one as soft.** The offer-letter NRIC, read by image-Gemini, came back non-deterministically (`0806201578` vs `080620101578` on the same document); an exact-equality identity check turned that dropped digit into a false "different person" flag (#36), caught only by the held-out run. Fix: anchor on the OCR'd IC + profile NRIC, treat the offer NRIC as soft corroboration with a bounded edit-distance tolerance, and let the NAME be the robust offer-side check. General rule: before comparing two extracted identifiers for equality, ask which extraction path is reliable — anchor on that one, and tolerate noise (edit-distance/fuzzy) on any field read by a multimodal model. (Doc-eval offer/UA/IC/BC-EPF, 2026-06-20)
- **Validate a classifier on HELD-OUT data, not just the calibration corpus — the corpus can't show you a generalisation gap.** The offer-letter genuineness + identity logic was clean on the local corpus but the #36 false-mismatch only surfaced when run against 10 unseen production docs. General rule: for any scoring/matching system, a local-corpus pass proves it fits the data you tuned on; a held-out pass is what proves it generalises — budget the unseen run as part of "done", not a nice-to-have. (Doc-eval offer/UA/IC/BC-EPF, 2026-06-20)
- **Don't mint sequential IDs (TD-NNN, migration numbers) on a long-lived feature branch — they collide on every merge from a busy `main`.** A `_GENUINENESS_DOCS` tidy was renumbered TD-120→124→127→130→132→133 as the other agent reused each number on `main`; the churn ate real time across merges and was only ended by resolving the item outright. General rule: allocate shared sequential IDs at merge-to-main time, not on the branch; if a branch must carry one, expect to renumber it at merge and don't anchor anything else to it. (Doc-eval offer/UA/IC/BC-EPF, 2026-06-20)
- **An item being *visible in-app* is not the same as the user being *notified*.** A reviewer-raised Action-Centre request (`AdminResolutionItemView`) showed in the student's cockpit but sent no email — students only saw it on their next login. Root cause: the notification path (`send_due_query_emails`) was wired only to system/clarify items, never to the new `source='officer'` create-path. General rule: whenever you add a new way for staff to create student-facing work, trace it to the notification trigger in the SAME change — "it appears in their list" is not delivery. Prefer resetting the existing batched/idempotent notify-stamp over a per-item email (avoids spam + Brevo quota burn). (Officer-request notification gap, 2026-06-21)
- **A UI prefill that drives SIDE-EFFECTS must be persisted — or a downstream single-field reader will disagree with the reality those side-effects created.** The income wizard pre-ticked the roster earner and used that to TAG uploaded income docs (`household_member=mother`), but only saved `income_working_members` on an explicit toggle. A student who accepted the prefill and just uploaded left tagged docs + an empty list; the cockpit layout, the gate, and the income verdict all read ONLY that list → income shown Optional / verdict red `income_earner_undeclared`, on 5 live apps. General rule: when a prefilled/derived value both (a) seeds an input the user may never touch and (b) drives a side-effect (tagging, a stored verdict input), PERSIST it on mount — don't wait for an interaction — AND make the downstream reader tolerant by reconstructing from the same authoritative signals (here `effective_working_members`: the tagged docs, then the roster) when the field is empty. Two independent records of the same fact (a list vs. the tagged docs) WILL drift; reconcile them at the source. (Salary-route working-members fallback, 2026-06-21)
- **Verifying/fixing Malaysian gov/edu links needs a network that can REACH them — the agent sandbox AND Cloud Run (outside MY) cannot reliably, so a reachability verdict from either is an environment artifact, not truth.** Building the authoritative-index URL refresher, the live reconciliation from the sandbox marked ALL 15 `*.matrik.edu.my` URLs "unreachable" and the JS-heavy MOE page wouldn't render — the same root cause as the prod check's 122 "timeouts": these sites are slow/geo-restricted/DNS-flaky from abroad. So: don't bulk-write speculative URL corrections from an environment that can't verify them (you'd replace working URLs with stale ones). The reliable verify path is the OWNER's browser (they're in MY and can reach the sites) or a MY-based run; the tool does the reachability check correctly *when run from a capable network*, and the authoritative index supplies candidates, but a human who can actually load the sites makes the final call. (Authoritative-index link refresher, 2026-06-14)
- **Tie a status/stage transition to the event the user actually performs — not a proxy step they may skip or do last.** The `profile_complete → interviewing` flip was wired to the reviewer opening the Interview-Stage *capture draft*. But reviewers run the interview through a different surface (scheduling: propose times → interview@ email → Meet) and fill the capture form last or never — so the board read "Complete" through a booked/concluded interview (Theresa). General rule: when a stage has several entry paths, anchor the transition on the EARLIEST authoritative signal that the stage has truly begun (here: interview comms / `propose_slots`), and keep the later step as a fallback, not the trigger. Guard the flip so it only moves FORWARD (here: advance only from `profile_complete`, never pull a decided case back). (Interviewing-on-propose, 2026-06-23)
- **A "hide to avoid duplicate noise" decision is only valid while the duplicate surface is actually reachable — re-check it when the surrounding flow changes.** Check-2 system bad-doc tickets (`*_unreadable`) were hidden from the student's Action Centre on the rationale that Gopal already coaches the same doc inline on the Documents tab. But once the post-submit student became form-LOCKED (Action Centre = their only surface), that Documents-tab coach was unreachable — so the "duplicate" was gone and the hide just left the student unable to act on a doc the system genuinely couldn't read (the cockpit showed an open request the student never saw). General rule: when you suppress one of two surfaces to de-dup, leave a note tying the suppression to the OTHER surface's reachability, and re-evaluate when a lock/visibility change removes it. (Check-2 unreadable-doc visibility, 2026-06-23)
- **A "never happened" state and a "service is down" state are different — don't collapse them, because one self-heals and the other doesn't.** `ic_identity_blockers` mapped an IC with no Vision run (`vision_run_at=NULL`) to `ic_service_down` ("our service is temporarily unavailable, try later"). But the service wasn't down — the OCR was simply never run on that doc (a transient upload-time pipeline failure that was never retried), so "try later" was a lie: nothing would ever re-process it. The student sat permanently behind a false outage message while the cockpit verdict showed the real state — two surfaces, two messages. General rule: when a check depends on a side-effect having run, distinguish "the side-effect failed/erred" (a real outage → retry-and-maybe-wait) from "the side-effect never ran" (→ RUN it; add a sweep that re-attempts and always records an outcome so it can't loop). And verify the side-effect is actually idempotently retried somewhere, not assumed to have happened at creation time. (IC OCR self-heal, 2026-06-23)
- **For a Next.js git-worktree, junctioning the main checkout's `node_modules` can break Next's module resolution (`Cannot find module '@next/env'`) — run a real `npm ci` inside the worktree instead.** Refines the "junction deps in" note (lessons above): a junction is fine for `tsc`/`jest`, but `next build` resolves modules in a way the junction defeats. The reliable path for building/Playwright in a JS worktree is its own `npm ci`. (Bursary + auth scope, 2026-06-26)
- **Filter Cloud Build status polls on `substitutions.SHORT_SHA`, not `COMMIT_SHA`.** GitHub-triggered builds populate `SHORT_SHA`; filtering on `COMMIT_SHA` silently returns zero rows and reads as "no build running". (Bursary + auth scope, 2026-06-26)
- **A new Supabase table gets `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` but NOT `FORCE` — mirror the existing working-table pattern, don't reach for a generic "lock it" snippet.** `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` also subjects the table OWNER role to policies, which blocks the app's own access; the established tables (`whatsapp_messages`, `course_data_status`) use ENABLE-only (verify `relforcerowsecurity = false`). The `get_advisors` INFO row for an RLS-on/no-policy table is expected, not a defect. (Bursary + auth scope, 2026-06-26)
- **Don't weight a signal you can't actually read.** The BC genuineness scorer (run text-only in prod — no image read wired) kept the Jata Negara crest + barcode as weighted signatures; they could never be credited, so they only ever dragged genuine plain BCs toward `suspect` (3/13 unseen) while catching zero fakes. General rule: every signature in a scored list must be evaluable on the inputs that path actually has; a visual signature in a text-only scorer is dead weight that penalises the honest case. (Doc-recognition Layer-1 go-live, 2026-06-27)
- **Anchor document recognition on durable BODY fields, not just the page title.** Genuine STR Semakan screenshots cropped above the "Semakan Status" title scored `unrecognised` because the title was the form's only identity anchor. Real phone screenshots are routinely cropped, and desktop layouts wrap/interleave labels — so a single title anchor is fragile. Fix was multiple anchors incl. a status field present on every instance. General rule: pick identity anchors that survive the crops and OCR-layout noise your real corpus actually exhibits (validate on held-out, not just clean fixtures). (Doc-recognition Layer-1 go-live, 2026-06-27)
- **Validate every hard-coded signature string against real OCR before locking it in.** An owner-supplied STR term `Sumbangan Asas Rumah` was coded verbatim, but the document spells it `Rahmah` (it's SARA) — so it matched nothing and silently capped scores. General rule: a printed-string signature is only as good as its exact match; round-trip each one through the actual OCR text of a real doc, never trust the spec spelling. (Doc-recognition Layer-1 go-live, 2026-06-27)
- **Version a model that produces stored verdicts, and stamp the version on every result.** The doc-recognition signature model now carries `MODEL_VERSION` on each result (persisted in `vision_fields.authenticity`) so a mis-recognition is traceable to the exact model revision and performance is comparable across versions. General rule: any deterministic model whose outputs persist should travel a version; bump it on every calibration change (a guard test pins the stamp's presence, but the bump discipline is on the author). (Doc-recognition Layer-1 go-live, 2026-06-27)
- **For an ad-hoc prod DB *write*, use Supabase MCP `execute_sql` (with `RETURNING`) — not a multi-line `manage.py shell -c` via `gcloud run jobs execute --args`.** A multi-line Python script passed through the `^@^`-delimited `--args` intermittently mis-parses and aborts BEFORE the `a.save()`, so the write silently doesn't persist while reads in the same script appear to work (only `print` source lines surface in the logs). Wasted two job runs on the #12 backfill before switching to `execute_sql … RETURNING` which applies + confirms in one call. The Cloud Run job is fine for *management commands*; reserve it for those, and verify any write by re-reading the row. (Decision-flow hardening, 2026-06-27)
- **A comment that says "flag-gated / dark by default" is not a flag check — gate the UI on a real flag value the backend exposes.** The Bursary-agreement cockpit panel rendered for every accepted/sponsored applicant (showing false "signed" ticks) because its render condition checked only `status`; the "dark by default" was only in a code comment, and the backend never surfaced `BURSARY_AGREEMENT_ENABLED` to the frontend. A dark feature isn't dark until the surface is gated on the actual flag. (Decision-flow hardening, 2026-06-27)
- **Don't overload one state field for two concerns.** The decline cool-off used a single "silent until release" mechanism to mean BOTH "decision not yet final" AND "student not yet told" — so a final decision displayed at its pre-decision status. Separate finality (flip the status now) from notification (embargo the email); mask the student surface if they shouldn't see it yet. (Decision-flow hardening, 2026-06-27)
- **When a verdict gains a new signal, every surface that shows the doc must consume the SAME signal — a serializer allow-list is a silent desync point.** The offer-validity gate made the pathway *verdict tile* read offer genuineness, but the cockpit *Documents-drawer chip* derived its colour only from `pathway_check`, because `get_authenticity` had an explicit doc-type allow-list that omitted `offer_letter` (so `doc.authenticity` was always null on the FE). Result: the chip showed green "Verified" while the tile said "not official" — two surfaces, one fact, contradicting each other. General rule: when you add a signal to one consumer of a serialised object, grep every other consumer + the serializer's per-type gates; an allow-list that quietly excludes a type desyncs the UI without erroring. (Offer-validity gate, 2026-06-27)
- **A lenient field matcher must drop ISSUER/administrative boilerplate, or it false-clashes on noise.** The offer-vs-declared pathway matcher flagged a genuine matriculation offer as a MISMATCH because the offer's programme line OCR'd as "PROGRAM MATRIKULASI KEMENTERIAN PENDIDIKAN" — the ministry words clashed with the declared stream "Sains" while the institution matched. A token-overlap matcher only distinguishes if its stoplist covers the boilerplate that appears on real letters (ministry, issuer, "rasmi/surat"), not just institution-type words. Validate the stoplist against the wording real documents actually carry. (Offer-validity gate / #30, 2026-06-27)
- **To rename a status/enum *value* on a live table, use expand-contract: make the new value canonical but keep the old one TOLERATED for one release, deploy code first, then migrate the rows, then drop the alias in the next sprint.** A naive migrate-first value-rename has no safe order — whichever side moves first, the other misreads the rows (and can fire side effects, e.g. spurious cron emails) for the deploy window. Tolerating both (choices + read-sites + behaviour sets) makes the deploy order-independent and the data move a non-event. (Post-award lifecycle S1, 2026-06-28)
- **After `makemigrations`, read the generated file and drop operations you didn't author.** A model edit left un-migrated on `main` (a `ScholarshipCohort.name` help_text change) attached itself to the next migration — my status-rename `0073` came out with a third, foreign `AlterField`. `makemigrations` snapshots ALL pending model drift, not just your change; never assume the generated migration is only your edit. (Post-award lifecycle S1, 2026-06-28)
- **A chain that verifies identity by NUMBER must not then be re-litigated by an exact NAME/number compare.** The IC-number chain confirmed the mother (BC number == STR number), but the first cut re-anchored the BC mother-row against the proof with an `or` fallback — when the proof name was unread (`''`), it fell back to the *wrong card's* name and went red again. Once a strong key (the IC number) settles identity, set the dependent statuses to `match` directly; don't run a second exact compare that the same OCR drift will fail. (L2-1 IC-number chain, 2026-06-29)
- **Migrate the local sqlite before running an eval/fixture harness that builds rows.** `eval_doc_recognition --auto-ok` reported "0 genuine" — actually 335 `OperationalError: no column closure_reason`, because the local DB lagged the post-award column additions. A harness that builds real rows needs the schema current; when an eval reports an implausible zero, check for a migration gap before debugging logic. (L2-1 IC-number chain, 2026-06-29)
- **When a sprint adds new lifecycle states, audit the SURFACE-LOOKUP helpers that gate where a user can act — a new status is invisible to the UI until the lookup includes it.** The post-award lifecycle (S1–S6) added `awarded`/`active`/`maintenance`, but `_current_application` (the helper behind the student's document-upload + Action Centre endpoints) still filtered on `POST_SHORTLIST_EDITABLE`, which stops at `interviewed` — so an awarded student silently could not reach those surfaces at all (every call 403'd `no_application`). Adding states to the model + the gating sets is not enough; the "where can this user act" lookups are a separate axis. General rule: when you introduce statuses, grep the helpers that resolve "the current object for this user" and decide, per helper, whether the new states belong — and guard the broadening (here: `revert_if_profile_incomplete` only acts on `profile_complete`, so a funded student on the shared upload surface can't fall out of funded status). (Post-award S7 bank-details, 2026-06-29)
- **…and the FE RENDER-GATE is a second, equally-easy-to-miss surface — I fixed the backend lookup above but the SAME sprint's page still didn't SHOW the Action Centre to a funded student.** `/scholarship/application` mounted `<ActionCentre>` only for a hand-listed `profile_complete|interviewing|interviewed`, so an awarded student got the task created + served by the backend but the page rendered the neutral "received" card instead — the bank upload was invisible. The API test + the component-in-isolation test both passed; nothing checked "does the awarded student's PAGE actually mount the component". General rule: a status-gated feature has TWO independent gates — the backend "can this user act / fetch" lookup AND the FE "which branch of the page renders" switch — and a new lifecycle state must be added to BOTH. Make the FE gate a single TESTED predicate (`showsActionCentre(status)`) instead of an inline `status === 'a' || status === 'b'` chain, so a unit test pins the membership and the "fell through the render switch" class can't silently recur. (Post-award S7 bank-details FE fix, 2026-06-29)
- **NEVER re-run document Vision/Gemini extraction from a local checkout — it destroys the stored `vision_fields`.** A local checkout has no Supabase Storage access, so the extractor reads the document blob as "no text", sets `student_verdict='unreadable'` and overwrites the good fields (corrupted #76/#16 offers). The local→pooler pattern gives DB access only. Re-extraction must run on the live service (cockpit "Re-run" / admin re-run endpoint); local backfills are safe ONLY when they read ALREADY-STORED `vision_fields`, never when they re-OCR the blob. (Bursary data-quality, 2026-06-29)
- **Before declaring "no source of truth exists", check the virtual/linked records, not just a name search.** I told the owner matric colleges weren't in the catalogue — they were, stored as `KM <State>` and linked via the `matric-*` virtual courses, which a `institution_name__icontains='Kolej Matrikulasi'` search missed. Search the relationship (CourseInstitution links off the virtual course), not just the display name. (Bursary data-quality, 2026-06-29)
- **A fuzzy catalogue match against a large, near-duplicate name set will silently pick the wrong row — require a UNIQUE match and preview before applying.** Token-matching STPM schools (~250 per bidang) would have moved students to a different school (SMK→SMJK, "Tun Hussein Onn"→"Bandar Tun Hussein Onn 2") because the type prefix drops as a generic token and subset-alignment over-matches. For a many-candidate set, demand a single match (0/>1 → abstain) and dry-run; when in doubt, don't match at all (casing-only). (Bursary data-quality, 2026-06-29)
- **When you normalise a derived display value at the source, audit EVERY write path into it — not just the canonical one.** I normalised programme/institution casing via the recommender catalogue (the source of truth), but `confirm_pathway` (the student-confirms-their-offer path) writes the programme name *verbatim from the offer letter*, bypassing the catalogue entirely — so one ALL-CAPS offer (#107) shouted on its public sponsor-pool card while the catalogue stayed clean. A field can have several writers; a normalisation that only covers the main one leaves the rare path to leak. Grep every assignment to the field (`chosen_programme['course_name'] =`) and apply the same guard. (Programme-name casing hotfix, 2026-06-29)
- **A `next build` that dies with a Jest-worker `WorkerError` (V8 hex-address stack) on this 8 GB box is almost always OOM, not a code fault — raise the Node heap before debugging the diff.** Two consecutive builds "failed" with `Jest worker encountered N child process exceptions`; re-running with `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=4096` completed clean with no code change. Next.js static generation peaks above the default heap on low-RAM hardware. When you see WorkerError, retry with the larger heap first. (Reaffirmed too: `npm run build | tail -N` reports `tail`'s exit code, masking the real failure — read the tail text for "Build error", don't trust the piped exit 0.) (Award-panel embargo, 2026-06-29)
- **A status/task-gated UI feature has more than one render STATE — and the terminal (resolved/done) state is the easy one to forget.** The bank-details task tested fine in its open/upload/confirm states but its RESOLVED card showed "From your reviewer" + a blank title, because `bank_details_missing` was never added to `KNOWN_CODES` — so the generic title resolver mistook the resolved item for a free-text officer ticket. The open state was masked by a dedicated component; the resolved path was never exercised. General rule: when a task has a special-cased OPEN renderer, check what its RESOLVED/done/empty states fall through to — and register any new task `code` in the shared KNOWN_CODES/title maps, with a test that pins the code's title source + attribution. (Bank done-card fix, 2026-06-29)
- **A smarter document READ is wasted unless the insight reaches the DECISION as a structured signal.** The latest model already wrote "salary slip, not an STR proof" in its prose `warnings`, but the verdict only consumed the coarse `source_type='unknown' → unconfirmed` — so the card still said "probable STR" + a SALINAN word-salad. The fix wasn't a better read; it was lifting the read into a **structured state** (`wrong_type`) that `_str_currency` returns and the verdict engine maps. When a model "knows" something in prose but the UI still misbehaves, check whether the decision layer actually CONSUMES that knowledge as data. (STR-proof model S1, 2026-06-30)
- **Param-driven per-state copy = an ICU `select`, not a renderer change.** The verdict item already passed its `params` (incl. `status`) to next-intl, so making `str_not_current` say one decisive sentence per state was pure i18n: `{status, select, wrong_type {…} rejected {…} other {…}}` in all three message files — zero component logic. Keep ICU strings apostrophe-free (a lone `'` starts an ICU quote) to avoid escaping. (STR-proof model S1, 2026-06-30)
- **On a multi-sprint feature branch, merge `origin/main` at every sprint boundary — not just at close.** Five sprints in, the branch was 5 commits behind `origin/main`: the owner had pushed a parallel STR-proof series + an award-email reword touching the exact files I'd changed (`emails.py`, the i18n JSONs, `views.py`). It auto-merged clean and tests passed, but discovering a same-files divergence only at sprint-close is how a painful conflict (or a silent semantic clobber) happens. Fetch + merge `origin/main` into the branch each sprint; re-run the suite. (Post-award signing, 2026-07-01)
- **Canonical docs live at the worktree-root `docs/`, NOT `halatuju_api/docs/`.** Retrospectives, `decisions.md`, `lessons.md`, `technical-debt.md`, and `docs/scholarship/*` specs all sit at the repo root `docs/`. New scholarship/bursary docs belong in root `docs/scholarship/`. (Post-award signing, 2026-07-01)
- **Before building an "entry point" for a flag-gated feature, check the EMBARGOED surfaces first.** I built a redundant Action-Centre "sign your agreement" task before realising the existing `awardPanel` already routed awarded students to `/scholarship/award` — it was just hidden behind `AWARD_ACCEPTANCE_ENABLED` during an earlier sprint. A "missing" feature may be a dark/embargoed existing one; grep the flags before greenfielding. (Post-award signing, 2026-07-01)
- **A fact's TILE colour can be driven by the wrong evidence — check what actually qualifies it.** Routing an *unsure* income through the `review` status would have shown #13 as 🔵 Probable, because the "blue needs a green" rule fires on ANY verified evidence and the earner-IC/relationship greens qualify — even though those say nothing about whether the income is B40. Fix: unsure/over return `recommend` (amber unconditionally); only a genuinely-probable income returns `review` with its OWN green (`income_salary_probable`). When a tile's band looks too confident, ask which evidence is qualifying it — incidental greens from a different sub-check can leak the band up. (STR-proof S2, 2026-07-01)
- **CORRECTION to the S1 "use an ICU `select`" lesson: this app's `t` has NO ICU engine.** `lib/i18n.tsx`'s `t` does flat `{var}` regex substitution only — so the S1 `str_not_current` `{status, select, …}` rendered its raw template to officers (#13/#102). Param-driven per-state copy here is NOT an ICU select; it's **flat per-value keys** (`str_not_current_<status>`) picked in code (`verdictItemKey`). Disregard the earlier "per-state copy = ICU select" note for this codebase. Guardrail added: `messages/__tests__/no-icu-messageformat.test.ts` fails the build on any `{x, select|plural|…}` in a catalogue, so the whole class can't recur. (STR-copy fix, 2026-07-01)
- **When an LLM keeps misreading ONE safety-critical field despite an explicit prompt, stop tuning the prompt — add a deterministic guard off a HARD signal it already reads.** The extraction prompt *told* the model to read "Lulus" (the value under the "Status Permohonan STR" label), and it *still* returned the label "STR" (#23, even at v1.2). The fix wasn't a better prompt; it was leaning on the **paid amount** the model DID read — you're not paid STR money unless Lulus — as a deterministic approval rescue. Keep the LLM read as PRIMARY (Lulus), the deterministic signal as an ADDITIVE rescue (never a downgrade: a genuine doc can legitimately show RM0). This is the WAT split in miniature: probabilistic read, deterministic backstop on the money-adjacent decision. (STR payment guard, 2026-07-01)
- **A "can't confirm" state must not narrate a specific CAUSE it doesn't know.** The `unreadable` copy asserted "the page is cropped — re-upload", but the page was complete and merely misread — and it had already passed genuineness. Telling an officer to chase a re-upload for a doc the system accepts is worse than saying nothing. Copy for an uncertainty state should describe the uncertainty ("couldn't confirm approval"), not invent a mechanism ("cropped") that a different subsystem (genuineness) actually owns. (STR unreadable copy, 2026-07-01)
- **A fallback input must be gated on explicit intent, not on the mere absence of the primary.** The declared-income RM field first showed the instant a salary-route member was ticked (gated only on "no payslip uploaded yet"), beside the empty payslip cards — inviting a student who HAS a payslip to skip it and just type a number, inverting the evidence hierarchy and needing a same-day 2nd deploy. The absence of the primary ≠ "can't provide the primary". Gate a fallback behind an explicit opt-in ("can't get a payslip?"), so the primary path stays the default. (Income model 2A, 2026-07-02)
- **A soft consistency/anomaly signal should fire only in the direction that HARMS the applicant.** The household-size check flags only when described people OUTNUMBER the stated size (a too-small denominator overstates per-capita income → looks less needy). The reverse — a household larger than the itemised roster — is common and benign (not everyone is listed one-by-one), so flagging it would flood reviewers with noise. For any advisory cross-check, ask which direction is the harmful one and fire only there. (Income model 2C, 2026-07-02)
- **The `SimpleTestCase` fake QS (`_FakeDocs`/`_FakeQS` in test_income_engine) filters by `doc_type` only, NOT `household_member`.** So any per-member document logic (a member's own payslip/EPF/support doc) is silently cross-attributed when another member has a same-type doc on file — a shared salary slip made a working guardian read as "satisfied". Test per-member gap paths with NO competing same-type doc, or use a DB `TestCase` where `_cluster_docs` filters for real. (Income model 2C, 2026-07-02)

- **When you tighten a "counts as evidence / clears the gap" rule, the tests that made the LOOSE version pass are the ones that must flip to assert the new failure case — and they were green *because* of the bug.** `has_income_support_doc` cleared the declared-income gap on mere presence; four tests created a field-less `income_support_doc` and asserted it cleared/accepted — pinning finding #2 (a blank image proves a wage) as green. Grep for the helper that builds the doc, seed the discriminating field explicitly (`student_verdict='ok'` vs `'wrong_doc'`), update the loose assertions, and ADD the blank-case companion. (Verify-model V1, 2026-07-03)
- **A negative test that needs "an item NOT in set X" must pick one structurally outside X, not one that merely happens to be outside today.** `test_admin_rerun_vision_rejects_unsupported_type` used `guardianship_letter` as its "no automatic check" example; V1 gave guardianship a check and the test broke. Use a type that is inherently un-checkable (a `photo`), so a future feature adding a check to some doc type can't silently invalidate the negative test. (Verify-model V1, 2026-07-03)
- **A roadmap written against an earlier tree describes a "before" that may already be partly built — verify each finding's failure reproduces in the CURRENT code before implementing its fix.** V1's guardianship_letter finding implied the verdict/resolution/chip needed building; reading the code showed they existed and only the extraction TRIGGER was missing (one frozenset membership). Confirming the real gap shrank the change and avoided rebuilding working code. (Verify-model V1, 2026-07-03; reinforces the standing "verify against the running system, not a summary" lesson)

- **When you make an ACCEPT path depend on a NEW field, every fixture that models the accept case must set that field — a fixture missing it silently flips to the new reject/hold branch.** V2 added a `student_verdict`-based pending/unreadable hold to BC/EPF in `doc_match_verdict`; three clean-doc fixtures (`_bc_doc`, `_epf_doc`, the resolution clean-BC test) set `vision_fields.fields` but no `student_verdict`, so a "reads fine" doc started returning `'pending'`. A read doc in production always carries `student_verdict='ok'` — the fixtures pre-dated the gate. Fix: set the field on the accept fixtures + add explicit blank/errored companions. (This is the V1 "tests encoded the loose version" lesson one layer down — tightening an accept breaks the fixtures that modelled the accept, not the reject.) (Verify-model V2, 2026-07-03)

- **When a lifecycle change breaks a test, CLASSIFY it before touching it: an intended-semantics change a stakeholder OWNS is a decision to surface, not a fixture to silently rewrite.** V3's #6/#8 broke four tests; two were fixture-only (reporting-date carve-out, back-date a query's created_at for a per-item lapse), but two encoded genuine product forks — "does a locked app still show review items?" and "does a late query delay the reviewer?" — that only the owner could settle. Rewriting those fixtures to match my guess would have shipped the wrong behaviour to a live student system. Commit the WIP "DO NOT MERGE" while the forks are open, take them to the owner, then reconcile the fixtures to the funnel (create items while non-locked, then flip status). (Verify-model V3, 2026-07-03)

- **When you add a detector that reads a COMMON state as a gap, the shared base test fixture becomes a positive case for it — sweep the fixtures for incidental triggers, don't just add the feature.** V4's broad new detectors (no-bill, no-results-slip, roster-undercount, employed-no-EPF) fired for the default check2 app and disrupted five unrelated tests (crowding out the gap they were testing; breaking a 'no questions' assertion). Fix: make the base fixture neutral for the new detector (roster-consistent; satisfy the doc-requests in 'no questions' tests) and count only the relevant kind in cap assertions. The same breadth is ALSO the live signal — a detector that trips the default fixture will trip many real applicants, so it's the raise-condition to watch/tune post-deploy. (Verify-model V4, 2026-07-03)

- **Verify web types with the project's own tsconfig (`npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json`), never an overridden `--target`/`--downlevelIteration`.** A local `tsc --target es2018 --downlevelIteration` accepted a `Set` spread (`[...set]`) that `next build`'s tsconfig rejected → a green local check, a red Cloud Build. The override also hid narrow-union comparison errors. `next build` OOMs on the 8 GB box, so `tsc -p tsconfig.json` is the faithful pre-push type check — an overridden target is not. (Cockpit live-review, 2026-07-05)
- **When a fix generalises from one flagged case, encode the STRUCTURAL signal, not the case's incidentals.** The SGD detector first matched the employer NAME (NTUC/FairPrice) — useless as a rule; the systemic signal is the `Pte Ltd`/`Private Limited` incorporation suffix + CPF/SDL/S$ currency markers. Add a test that asserts the incidental alone (a bare company name) is NOT sufficient, so a future edit can't regress to name-matching. (Cockpit live-review, 2026-07-05)
- **A re-run re-extracts EVERY field — a fix that depends on one field's value isn't done until the extraction reads that field robustly.** The SGD conversion "changed on re-run" because the re-read grabbed the basic salary (understated) and re-guessed `currency=MYR`. Anchor on the most stable signal (employer suffix over a currency guess) and read the definitive figure (total earnings over basic), or a re-run will flip the verdict. (Cockpit live-review, 2026-07-05)

- **"Verified live" via LOCAL code against PROD data is NOT "deployed" — confirm a build actually TRIGGERED and SUCCEEDED for your SHA before saying it's live.** During the stuck-students round I ran my uncommitted local code against the prod DB (dev settings + the Supabase session pooler) to confirm #56/#88 flip to READY, and reported them "live". But the Cloud Build webhook never fired for two pushes (`a5cd592`, `9fe52e72`) — the actual service kept running the old code, so the cockpit was unchanged. The build-watch returned empty and I moved on instead of treating "no build appeared" as a failure. Local-code-vs-prod-data proves the LOGIC is right on real data; it says nothing about whether the SERVICE runs that logic. After every push: match `SHORT_SHA` to your commit in `gcloud builds list`, wait on that build ID; if no build appears within ~60s, the webhook missed — manually `gcloud builds triggers run <trigger-id> --branch=main`. (Stuck-students verification-gate round, 2026-07-08)

- **For a "why is the verdict/gate X?" question, run the engine FIRST; never narrate a mechanism from a field-dump.** On #28 I proposed "mislabelled earner", was corrected, proposed a second wrong story, and only then ran `build_verdict` / `student_income_ic_check` — which showed the real cause (the extraneous mother IC cross-checked against the father's single-recipient STR). Verdict/gate outputs are the product of a multi-step engine; the raw document fields don't reveal which check fired. A read-only shell diagnostic (or `stuck_report`) against prod costs one minute and replaces two wrong guesses. (Stuck-students verification-gate round, 2026-07-08)

- **A locked principle must be routed through ONE shared helper, not re-implemented per code path — the gate, the verdict, and the display each make the "same" decision and WILL diverge.** STR precedence and "trust the verdict card" were correct in `verdict_engine` but the consent gate used raw parallel checks (`offer_official_status`; a salary-only suppression), and the father-link used the typed name while the verified IC sat unused — each a silent violation of a stated principle. The durable fix is not "patch the gate too" but "make the gate call the same helper the verdict does" (`_fact_band`, `income_established`, `student_name_for_link`). When you lock a principle, grep for every decision site and converge them on one function. (Stuck-students verification-gate round, 2026-07-08)

- **Before adding a promotion-side "keep-better" quality model for a doc type, check the downstream safety nets first — most of the work is already done elsewhere.** Phase 3 started as "per-doc quality for payslip/bills/results/offer/BC", but reading the code showed str/salary/epf/bills are re-collapsed to best-live by `dedupe_income_proof` AFTER promotion, and every type's `doc_match_verdict` already returns unreadable/pending (→ not usable → the live doc is kept) when core fields don't read. So the ONLY unguarded gap is two BOTH-usable copies differing on an axis the usable-gate + generic (usable, genuine, recency) proxy ignore — which turned out to be just officialness (offers) and field-completeness (slips). Map the existing gates/dedup before writing a new ranking model; you usually need it for one or two types, not all. (Upload stage→judge→promote sprint, 2026-07-09)

- **When a shared per-document fact exists (genuineness `gf`), audit that EVERY `documentFacts` branch emits it — not just the "main" identity/income ones.** The electricity wrong-type chip computed correctly but rendered as a soft amber "Check" because the utility branch of `officerCockpit.documentFacts` was the ONE doc-type branch that never pushed `gf` (every other branch did). No test/type caught it — the omission was invisible until a live wrong-type doc (#83) showed the wrong pill. When adding a doc type to a shared fact/chip, grep every branch of the render function and confirm it emits the shared fact + applies its cap. (Utility-bill genuineness sprint, 2026-07-10)
- **To model a new document type's signatures, calibrate on real OCR FIRST (measure token hit-rates), then set thresholds — don't hand-write markers from domain knowledge and hope.** The `eval/` pipeline (`fetch_corpus` + `capture_ocr`, read-only, gitignored snapshots) let me measure NO AKAUN=100% / TNB=92% / Tarikh Bil=88% on 27→90 real bills before finalising, which is what produced 0 false-rejects and the load-bearing "issuer marker ⟹ never rejected" rule. Reuse the harness (it already supports `--types <anything>`); never re-extract into `vision_fields` locally. (Utility-bill genuineness sprint, 2026-07-10)
- **A duplicate key in a hand-maintained Python dict literal is silently swallowed — Python keeps only the LAST value, and nothing flags it.** `vision._DOC_HINTS` had two `electricity_bill` (and two `water_bill`) entries; the detailed Extraction-v2 hint was dead because a shorter duplicate later in the same literal won. It only surfaced because I inspected both while wiring a sibling. When a dict-configured value (a prompt hint, a schema, a mapping) seems ignored, grep for a duplicate key BEFORE debugging the consumer. Durable prevention: enable `ruff F601` (duplicate-key-in-dict) or a tiny AST test over the config dicts. (Water-bill genuineness sprint, 2026-07-10)
- **Cross-check a bonus label against an independent signal before trusting its calibration count.** A raw scan showed operator `LAP` (Lembaga Air Perak) hitting 5 bills — inflated by the bare substring `LAP` ("laporan"). Cross-checking each operator label against the bill's address-state proved 0 mislabels and showed the real Perak bills land as `unrecognised`. Keep identity markers distinctive multi-word tokens (`AIR TERENGGANU`, not the bare `SATU`="one"). (Water-bill genuineness sprint, 2026-07-10)
- **A permission/role check DUPLICATED across modules drifts silently when a new role is added — tie the copies to one constant + a guard test.** Adding the `qc` role updated `services._can_review` but missed the stale copy in `scheduling._can_review`, so an assigned qc reviewer passed the real write gate (`_can_review_app`) yet was rejected inside `propose_slots` with `not_reviewer`. The tell: a **400 (not 403)** on an action the user is clearly allowed to do, and the failure is **independent of the input** (changing the interview times didn't help). Prevention shipped: one shared `services.REVIEW_ROLES` + a test asserting the two `_can_review` copies agree across every role. When you add an auth role, grep EVERY `_can_review`/role-tuple, not just the "main" one. (Reviewer/sponsor bug batch, 2026-07-10)
- **A fetch-once shared client store needs a refresh hook for EVERY action that mutates what it holds.** `SponsorPortalProvider` fetches pool/wallet once on mount (tab-switch speed) and exposed only `refreshReferrals`; the later fund action mutates the pool+wallet server-side but had no way to invalidate them, so a funded student lingered on the "available" list until a hard reload. When a mutating action lives on a different screen from a cached list, wire an explicit refresh (or optimistic removal) at the mutation site. (Reviewer/sponsor bug batch, 2026-07-10)
- **A "failed to send email" from a third-party auth provider is usually THEIR SMTP config, not your app code — and rotating an SMTP key is a multi-location change.** The invite 502 was Supabase Auth's own Brevo SMTP still on a deactivated key (`535`), separate from the app's `EMAIL_HOST_PASSWORD` which had been rotated. Read the provider's auth logs (the `535`/error_id) before touching app code; a Brevo key rotation must be applied in BOTH Cloud Run env AND the Supabase dashboard SMTP. (Reviewer/sponsor bug batch, 2026-07-10)
- **A document type can be read by TWO independent paths (deterministic parser + Gemini) — a fix to one is invisible to the other; diagnose from the stored `capture` field first.** After the LAP water-bill date fix (Gemini prompt), the owner re-ran an Air Selangor bill and nothing changed — because that PDF locked onto the DETERMINISTIC parser (`doc_parse._parse_water`, capture "Exact"), which the prompt never touches. I diagnosed from the bill's content before checking which path read it. When a doc-reading fix "has no effect", read `vision_fields.capture` (deterministic vs ai) FIRST — it decides which fixer applies. (Utility-bill cockpit sprint, 2026-07-10)
- **When you add an at-a-glance VALUES summary, derive it from the same source as the DECISION it sits next to — and confirm the exact field list + placement, not just the visual form.** The utility Period is formatted from the same `_bill_as_of` as the recency chip, so the shown month can't disagree with the green/amber/red tier. The layout was preview-confirmed, but the field set (Account was unwanted) and note-ordering (values must sit above the notes) needed two follow-ups — confirm those too, not only the look. (Utility-bill cockpit sprint, 2026-07-10)
- **A multi-day / remote-station stint has no natural "close" moment — schedule the sprint-close as its own pass, don't expect it to ride the last commit.** The 12–14 July batch shipped every fix the instant it was green (18 commits, all deployed), but CHANGELOG / retro / CLAUDE.md / memory all stayed at the previous close because the ceremony that normally accompanies a single local sprint's final commit never came. When work is pushed continuously across days, the close is a deliberate separate step. (Remote batch, 2026-07-12→14)
- **A test fixture is a claim that a state is reachable — when a newly-tightened gate fails a pile of fixtures at once, suspect the fixtures, not the gate.** Tightening the query and assignment stage-gates failed 51+ fixtures, every one pairing `status='shortlisted'` with `profile_completed_at` — a state production cannot produce (0 such rows live). The fixtures had been built to whatever each test needed, and passed only because nothing had ever asserted the pairing was illegal. Correct the fixtures to a reachable state; never weaken the gate to match an impossible one. (Remote batch, 2026-07-13)
- **When a default value is also a legitimate value, test MEMBERSHIP, not the returned value.** A status-tone guardrail asserted `statusTone(s) !== DEFAULT_TONE` for every status and failed on `closed`/`withdrawn`/`expired` — which are *legitimately* grey, the same grey as the unknown-status fallback. "Not the default" only means "explicitly mapped" when the default is a value nothing real uses. Export a membership predicate (`hasStatusTone`) and assert on that. Applies to any lookup with a sensible fallback that overlaps a real case (status colours, role→permission defaults, locale fallbacks). (Status-vocabulary sprint, 2026-07-14)
- **For an OCR heuristic, make the STRUCTURAL fallback correct independent of the heuristic's hit-rate — then the fix ships safely even when you can't calibrate the regex.** #117's water-bill address parser couldn't be tuned locally (the `eval/` corpus is gitignored PII, only on the remote station), but the load-bearing change was "if the address can't be read, return `None` → Gemini" — correct whether the regex catches 0% or 100%, because Gemini reads these bills. So the loop-breaking fix landed and green; the regex calibration + the live Re-run of the 6 bills became an explicit post-deploy step, not a blocker. When a heuristic can't be validated in the sandbox, separate "is the safety net correct?" (test that) from "how often does the fast path hit?" (calibrate live). (Check-2 #117 gaps, 2026-07-14)
- **Token-overlap is the wrong comparator for a hierarchical code space — canonicalise to distinct codes first.** `SAINS` and `sains_sosial` share the `sains` substring, so the generic clash detector read a real stream clash as a match. Both sides go through `parse_stpm_stream` to distinct codes (`sains` vs `sains_sosial`) before an equality compare, and the clash fires only when both are present. When adding a new field to a fuzzy matcher, check whether one legal value's name is a prefix/substring of another's — if so, map to codes, don't overlap tokens. (Check-2 #117 gaps, 2026-07-14)
- **A managed auth provider (Supabase) has no native password/credential TTL — a time-boxed credential must be enforced by your OWN scheduled rotation, never a provider setting or a frontend gate.** The 7-day partner temp-password expiry looked like it might be a config flag; it is not. A frontend "expired" check is bypassable (the raw password still yields a Supabase session), and a request-time backend gate can't stop a direct set-password. The only real boundary is invalidating the credential in the provider, so the enforcement is a daily cron that rotates the password dead (the login gate is just the friendly message for the ≤24h window before it fires). When asked to "expire" a managed credential, budget for a scheduled job, not a setting. (Partner onboarding, 2026-07-14)
- **Supabase admin `updateUser` MERGES `user_metadata` — to CLEAR a key you must set it to `null`, not omit it.** The partner set-password endpoint built its new metadata by excluding `temp_password_issued_at`; the field lingered on the live account anyway (verified in `auth.users`) because GoTrue shallow-merges the provided metadata into the existing blob. Harmless there (the field is only read while `must_change_password` is true, which set-password clears), but it would have made the expire-temp-passwords cron re-rotate an expired account every day instead of once. Fix: set the keys to `None` (JSON null) so the merge removes them; assert the null in tests, not key-absence. When updating a managed-provider metadata blob, know whether it replaces or merges — assume merge unless documented otherwise. (Partner onboarding live-test, 2026-07-15)
- **When proposing a differentiated-messaging feature (per-reason emails, template variants, dynamic copy), put the EXISTING copy in the proposal before designing the variants.** The reviewer decline-email work (2026-07-15) was designed and approved, then withdrawn once the current `interview` decline template was actually read — it already reads as a "limited funds" message, so the generic was sufficient. The "do we even need variants?" call can only be made against the real copy, so surface it first and spend no design effort until it's on the table. (Decision-trail sprint, 2026-07-15)
- To run a SCOPED management command on the live service, the arg-less `CronRunView` cron endpoint won't do (it `call_command(job)` with no args, 20 mixed-type docs/run). Reuse the isolated one-off Cloud Run Job pattern: build the image (push→deploy), then `gcloud run jobs create` on the NEW image, cloning the api service's env into a scratch file (`--env-vars-file`) so secrets never hit the transcript, run the scoped command, read results back from the DB, then delete the job + scratch file. (School-leaving-cert genuineness, 2026-07-15)
- A genuineness scorer can be calibrated against a live corpus WITHOUT persisting raw OCR text: have it store its per-doc marker hit-list (`markers.label_names`) in `authenticity`, then read that back after a re-extraction pass to see exactly which signatures fired on each real doc. (School-leaving-cert genuineness, 2026-07-15)
- **Any new code branching on a PartnerAdmin's role must honour the `is_super` bridge, never the bare `role` column** — the `role` column DEFAULTS to `'reviewer'`, so a super with no explicit role reads as a reviewer. The role endpoint already does `'super' if admin.is_super else admin.role`; a new reviewer-only gate that checked `admin.role == 'reviewer'` misfired on supers until it checked `is_super` first. Mirror the endpoint's effective-role computation. (Reviewer-onboarding sprint, 2026-07-15)
- **A set-password / change-password form needs an `autocomplete="username"` field (the account email) or the browser password manager saves the credential with an empty username** — its "Update password?" prompt then shows a blank Username box the user must fill by hand. A read-only email field carrying `autocomplete="username"` fixes the association (and doubles as "whose account is this"). The password fields should be `autocomplete="new-password"`. (Reviewer-onboarding sprint, 2026-07-15)
- A deterministic label-anchored parser MUST be validated on REAL OCR of the target documents before its reads are trusted in prod, not just a synthetic fixture — school-issued / multi-issuer docs vary enough (multi-column OCR, labels on their own line, template boilerplate) that a happy-path fixture hides the real failure modes. Ship it STRICT (fire only on a validated clean read, defer to Gemini otherwise) and confirm the distribution with a live re-extraction. (School-leaving cert fields, 2026-07-15; re-learn of L86/S15)
- Before quoting the blast radius of a verdict-BAND change, quantify against the REAL predicate (trace/run the exact condition, including any genuineness/officialness gate), not a rough SQL proxy — and re-quantify after each scope refinement. A "~8" estimate for the undeclared-pathway re-band was first too broad (would have hit many + broke 4 tests) then, with the genuine-only gate, was actually 2. (Undeclared pathway, 2026-07-15)
- **Static help/docs prose drifts silently because it isn't gate-tested — when you rebuild it, re-verify every claim against the live code, and add a currency rule so it can't drift again.** Rebuilding the reviewer guide into a role-aware manual surfaced two facts that had been wrong for weeks: it said Save "sends the final profile to sponsors" (publishing moved to QC-Accept on 2026-07-02) and still referenced reviewer sponsor-vetting (moved to org_admin on 2026-07-15). Neither was caught because prose has no test. Fix: every capability claim traces to `docs/scholarship/role-matrix.md`, and the matrix now carries a currency rule (a role-power change updates its chapter + FAQ in the same commit). When user-facing docs describe behaviour, tie them to the authority doc and check the live gate, don't trust the existing copy. (Guide & FAQ manual, 2026-07-16)
- **Never `git commit --amend` on the shared HalaTuju repo without first re-reading `git log`/reflog** — other sessions push to `main` continuously, so `main` can advance BETWEEN your commit and your amend, and `--amend` will then rewrite THEIR tip commit (folding your change into it and diverging). Recovery is `reset --hard origin/main` when your commit is already an ancestor of origin, then re-commit clean. Prefer a fresh commit over `--amend` here. (Cockpit verified ticks, 2026-07-15)
- **A "verified/document-matched" indicator on an AGGREGATE field must reconcile the documented TOTAL against the stated value in the engine — not tick merely because a supporting document exists, and not re-derive the aggregation on the FE.** The first household-income tick fired whenever any payslip amount was read, so #137 (documented RM7,874.73 vs stated RM7,000) would have shown "verified". Fix: a backend `household_check` compares the earner-sum to the stated figure within tolerance; the FE ticks off `matches`. Per-document match facts can live on the FE (`documentFacts`); a household aggregate cannot. (Cockpit verified ticks, 2026-07-15)
- **Attribute a field-level verification badge to the EXACT data set the check verifies, not the one on screen.** `academic_engine.student_slip_check` always compares the results slip against the student's SPM `profile.grades` (never `stpm_grades`); an STPM applicant who uploaded an SPM slip got that SPM match shown as a tick on their headline STPM grades (#132). Suppress/attach the tick by the verified set, not the display. Corollary latent bug: an STPM slip vs SPM grades yields a VACUOUS `results='match'` (no subject overlap) that also mis-greens the officer Documents chip/verdict — harden `student_slip_check` (results ≠ match on zero overlap) when it surfaces. (Cockpit verified ticks, 2026-07-15)
- **A dispositive proof that SETTLES a verdict must not silently SILENCE the completeness asks around it.** An STR settles the B40 income verdict, but `_parent_has_income_evidence` let "the STR is under this parent's name" also mean "we know this parent's income", suppressing the pension / informal / salary asks for the STR earner (#117: a retired father with no pension follow-up). The fix was to split two notions that had been fused: "economic status known" (drives the means test + the household-size tick) vs "income document on file" (drives the soft completeness asks). When a strong proof short-circuits one surface, check every OTHER surface that reads the same predicate before assuming the short-circuit is correct there too. (STR salary picture, 2026-07-16)
- **Before concluding WHY an auto-query didn't fire, check the live item timeline — don't stop at the stage-gate hypothesis.** For #117 the plausible story was "it's past the Completed stage so the machine stopped asking". The live data disproved it: a sync on 2026-07-14, while the app was still `profile_complete`, raised two OTHER new queries yet skipped the one in question — proving the cause was a data-predicate (the STR mask), not the stage. A subagent that only read code guessed the stage-gate; the live resolution-items row settled it. (STR salary picture, 2026-07-16)
- **A verification badge / tick must be attributed to the EXACT value shown, not a related "preferred" value.** The Pre-U institution tick compared the offer against `decl_inst` (which prefers a confirmed `chosen_programme` institution), but the field displays `pre_u_institution` — so #117 got a green tick asserting "SMK P Temenggong Ibrahim matches the offer" when the offer was actually for a different school (Kolej Tingkatan Enam Gombak). Compare against the shown field specifically, and suppress the tick when a broader signal (the overall Pathway chip) contradicts it. (Cockpit income/household arc, 2026-07-16)
- **A "we'll update your record to match" action must write EVERY field the UI reads for that fact, not just the canonical one.** `confirm_pathway` wrote `chosen_programme` but left `pre_u_institution`/`pre_u_track` — the fields the cockpit displays for an institution pathway and the ones the offer's Pathway chip compares against — so a confirmed student kept the old school + a red stream clash despite the copy's promise. When a confirm/auto-fix writes a derived value, enumerate every surface that reads that fact and update them together (and backfill the already-actioned rows). (Cockpit income/household arc, 2026-07-16)
- **For a HalaTuju migrate-first, never trust `manage.py sqlmigrate` for the prod DDL.** The local dev DB is SQLite, so `sqlmigrate` emits a destructive-looking full table-rebuild (`CREATE new__… / INSERT / DROP / RENAME`) for any AddField; prod is Postgres, where the same AddField is a plain `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`. Hand-write the Postgres DDL and mirror an existing sibling table's exact types via `information_schema` (e.g. `disbursements` for a payments table). (Payments module Sprint P3, 2026-07-16)
- **Running a local Django mgmt command against HalaTuju prod: reach for supabase.md §8 first.** The service's direct `DB_HOST` (`db.{ref}.supabase.co`) is IPv6-only and won't resolve locally ("could not translate host name"); the fix is the `aws-1` session pooler with `DB_USER=postgres.{ref}` (individual DB_* vars so psycopg2 keeps the dot). Also set `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` or a `→`/`·` in the command output crashes on the Windows cp1252 console mid-run. (Payments module Sprint P3, 2026-07-16)
- **Never count test failures from a `tail`-ed slice of a background run.** A full-suite run piped through `tail -4` left only the last 4 lines in the task output file; `grep FAILED` on it found 11 of 18 failures, and the "fix → rerun → new failures appear" loop repeated twice before the truncation was spotted. Capture the run with `| grep -E "^FAILED|passed|failed"` (no tail), and always reconcile the FAILED-line count against the summary line's number before acting. (Payments live-review arc, 2026-07-17)
- **A Google service-account capability has TWO gates: the Workspace DWD scope AND the API enablement in the GCP project.** Granting `auth/drive` in Domain-wide Delegation still 403s with `accessNotConfigured` until `gcloud services enable drive.googleapis.com` runs on the SA's project. Probe scopes empirically (a token refresh per scope tells you exactly what's granted — don't ask anyone to remember), then expect the API gate next. (Payments live-review arc, 2026-07-17)
- **Before importing a spreadsheet as payment "history", confirm per batch that the money actually moved.** The owner's CSV carried two batches; one (16/7) was a plan, not a record — it was backfilled as completed disbursements and had to be surgically deleted next day. An import that writes released money rows should get an explicit owner yes per batch date. (Payments live-review arc, 2026-07-17)
- **A field-for-field two-way sync must respect a field's provenance — a blank/weaker source cannot be allowed to overwrite a confirmed one.** `copy_pathway` blindly copied the profile's `chosen_programme` onto the open application whenever ANY pathway field changed, so a student's unrelated pre-U edit (with a blank profile programme) wiped the app's offer/officer-confirmed value with `{}` (#117). When two records sync and one field carries authority (`.source`), guard that field: never overwrite populated-with-empty, never overwrite confirmed-with-student. The other fields can copy freely. (Academic-box follow-ups, 2026-07-18)
- **When the same lookup table must exist in two runtimes (Python backend + browser FE), don't assume the copies stay in sync — add a cross-runtime parity test.** Python can read the FE's `ms.json` directly (same repo), so a backend test can assert `card_display._TRACK_LABEL` equals the FE labels and fail the build on drift. This is the cheap enforcement that makes duplication safe when a true single source (API-supplied text, or build-time codegen) isn't worth the machinery. Make it skip (not fail) when the other runtime's folder isn't in the checkout. (Pre-U label parity guard, 2026-07-18)
- **Never reuse a Django-reserved management-command flag, and avoid a custom `dest` that differs from the option string when the command is also driven by `call_command`.** `seed_contract_template --version` collided with `BaseCommand`'s built-in `--version` (argparse `ArgumentError`); the "fix" of `dest='version'` then broke `call_command` in tests, which maps kwargs to the option string via the parser's opt-mapping and can't resolve a mismatched dest. The clean fix: rename to `--template-version` with its natural dest. Reserved flags: `--version --verbosity --settings --pythonpath --traceback --no-color --force-color --skip-checks`. (Contract Module S1, 2026-07-18)
- **A continuation sprint on a SHARED repo must re-establish its branch before editing.** Sprint 2 opened with the working tree on `main` (not my feature branch) and `main` two commits ahead of where Sprint 1 branched — so my new file "didn't exist" and my commit wasn't in `git log`. Start every continuation with `git branch --show-current` + `git log --oneline -3`; if you're not on your branch, `git checkout <branch>`; then `git rebase main` (or merge) to build on the latest — never assume the tree is where the last sprint left it. (Contract Module S2, 2026-07-18)
- **When a code path gains a hard prerequisite, fix the tests at the single shared factory, and pick the new fixture's values to keep existing assertions true.** The bursary cutover made `sign_agreement` require a template (flag on), breaking 17 pre-cutover tests; fixing the one app factory (`_fundable_app` → deploy a template + pin `comprehension_template`) fixed all 17, and setting the template counterparty to 'Suresh' (the legacy settings default) kept the `assertIn('Suresh', rendered_html)` assertions valid with zero per-test edits. (Contract Module S2, 2026-07-18)
- **Stitch: generate ONE screen first, then batch the rest referencing its design system.** Firing 6 fresh `generate_screen_from_text` calls at once made the first 5 time out and not surface in `list_screens` (they race the design-system creation); only the last returned synchronously because the shared design system existed by then. Generate one screen (it mints the design system), grab its `designSystem` id, then fire the rest with that id — they render faster and return node-ids. Never re-fire a timed-out screen (it duplicates renders); wait for `list_screens`/the owner's pasted preview URL. (Contract Module S3, 2026-07-18)
- **Adding an `_AdminBase` endpoint means classifying it in `test_org_fence.py` (`FENCED_OR_EXEMPT`) — expect the guard to fail first.** The org-fence CI guard fails any new `_AdminBase` subclass nobody classified. This is by design (a fence nobody wired fails CI); budget for the one-line-per-endpoint classification in any admin-surface sprint. (Contract Module S3, 2026-07-18)
- **On the low-disk dev box, a background cleaner prunes `node_modules` during long sessions — chain FE install + verify as ONE command.** Mid-sprint, jest failed with "ts-jest not found" then a missing `@babel/types`, and `ls node_modules` showed 0 entries. A standalone `npx jest` after a separate `npm ci` fails because packages are pruned in between; `npm ci && npx jest` (or `&& npx next build`) runs the check inside the window before the cleaner strikes. A clean `tsc --noEmit --target es2018 --downlevelIteration` plus a chained jest/build is the reliable signal. (Contract Module S4, 2026-07-18)
- **Append to a possibly-IDE-open file with the Edit tool, not `cat >>`.** A `cat >>` append of a large client-fns block to `admin-api.ts` (which the IDE had open) was silently reverted when the IDE autosaved its stale buffer; tsc caught it as "no exported member" for every new fn.
